### [1] [How Much Geometry Does Newtonian Dynamics Fix?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21642)
*C S López-Monsalvo*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper reconstructs Newtonian mechanics without assuming a fixed spacetime geometry, deriving inertia as a theorem; it introduces a dynamical bilinear form and propagator, identifies torsion as a particle-carried three-form interpreted as classical spin, and postulates a geometric transport law for spin tied solely to acceleration—without coupling constants—while distinguishing the framework from Newton–Cartan spacetime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reformulate Newtonian mechanics geometrically from first principles—without presupposing fixed spacetime structure—thereby unifying inertia, universality of free fall, and spin in a torsion-based framework that precedes gravity.

Method: Constructs spacetime geometry dynamically via a propagator and bilinear form; derives inertial motion as geodesic-like curves blind to scale and torsion; interprets totally antisymmetric torsion as a particle-carried three-form; imposes arc-length parametrisation to constrain torsion; defines classical spin via contraction of torsion with velocity; postulates an acceleration-driven spin transport law independent of coupling constants; proves all mathematical claims rigorously.

Result: Inertial motion emerges as a theorem; Newton’s Second Law holds for arbitrary torsion with an exact correction term; force-free motion fixes torsion to be totally antisymmetric; torsion contracts with velocity to annihilate it—satisfying spinning-particle constraints identically; spin is identified with this contraction; spin transport depends only on acceleration and curve parametrisation; spacetime is non-degenerate and distinct from Newton–Cartan.

Conclusion: Newtonian mechanics can be rebuilt geometrically without fixed background structure; inertia, universality of free fall, and classical spin arise coherently from propagator and bilinear form dynamics; torsion encodes intrinsic spin; the resulting framework is mathematically complete, physically motivated, and conceptually novel—offering a pre-gravitational geometric foundation for non-relativistic dynamics.

Abstract: Newtonian mechanics is usually written on a spacetime of fixed geometry. Here we build that geometry, introducing a propagator and a bilinear form only as the description demands. Within this reconstruction the Principle of Inertia emerges as a theorem rather than an independent postulate. Inertial motion is blind to the scale of the form and to the torsion the propagator defines, and that blindness is a geometric precursor of the \emph{universality of free fall}, before gravity enters. Newton's Second Law is a compatibility between two derivatives along a curve. The force exceeds the mass times the lowered acceleration by an exact term, and the law survives arbitrary torsion. Requiring force-free motion to be inertial along curves parametrised by arc length restricts the torsion to be totally antisymmetric. The remaining torsion is a three-form which we postulate the particle carries along its worldline. Its contraction with the velocity annihilates that velocity, so the supplementary condition of spinning-particle mechanics holds identically, and we propose that contraction as a classical spin. The algebra is familiar from relativistic spin hydrodynamics; the assignment, to the best of our knowledge, has not been proposed elsewhere. Since inertial motion resolves neither the mass nor the spin, the predictive content lies in the transport law we postulate, which turns the spin against a parallel-transported frame at a rate fixed by the acceleration alone and by no coupling constant. That rate becomes a laboratory number only once the arc-length parameter is identified with a clock, which we leave open. The dynamics asks non-degeneracy of the form, so this spacetime is not Newton--Cartan's Galilean one, and gravitation lies outside the construction. We prove every mathematical result, make three constitutive postulates and one physical conjecture.

</details>


### [2] [Finite-term recurrences in a generalized Bochner--Krall family](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21802)
*L. M. Anguas,D. Barrios Rolanía,B. Shapiro,M. Tater*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper classifies certain differential operators of the form $T = z^j \partial_z^j + z^m \partial_z^\ell$ whose monic eigenpolynomials satisfy finite-term recurrence relations; it proves such recurrences exist iff $j=1$ and $k=\ell-m$ divides $\ell$, determines all recurrence coefficients explicitly, shows the associated difference operator has order $\ell$, and confirms two conjectures of Horozov–Shapiro–Tater.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize differential operators with polynomial eigenfunctions satisfying finite-term recurrence relations — a property linked to bispectrality and orthogonal polynomial theory — and verify conjectures in the bispectral framework of Horozov–Shapiro–Tater.

Method: Algebraic analysis of eigenpolynomial structure, recurrence relation derivation via coefficient comparison, explicit computation of recurrence coefficients, factorization of differential operators, and verification against Horozov–Shapiro–Tater’s classification conjectures.

Result: Necessary and sufficient condition: $j = 1$ and $k = \\ell - m \\mid \\ell$. Recurrence coefficients are given in closed form. Associated difference operator is of exact order $\\ell$. Every admissible $T$ admits an explicit factorization proving it is Type (2) per Conjecture 1.10; equality of differential and difference orders confirms Conjecture 1.9.

Conclusion: The paper fully resolves the classification problem for this family of operators, establishes structural links between differential order, recurrence length, and bispectral duality, and validates key conjectures in the theory of bispectral orthogonal polynomials.

Abstract: We classify the differential operators \(T=z^j\partial_z^j+z^m\partial_z^\ell\), where \(0\le m<\ell\) and \(1\le j<\ell\), whose monic eigenpolynomials satisfy a finite-term recurrence relation. Writing \(k=\ell-m\), such a recurrence exists if and only if \(j=1\) and \(k\mid\ell\). In that case we determine all recurrence coefficients in closed form and prove that the associated difference operator has order \(\ell\). We also give an explicit factorization showing that every admissible operator is of Type~(2) in Conjecture~1.10 of Horozov--Shapiro--Tater; the equality of the differential and difference orders is the conclusion predicted by their Conjecture~1.9.

</details>


### [3] [TENSKEL: A Combinatorial Observable Tensor for Structured Measurement and Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21999)
*Yvan Richard*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: TENSKEL is a combinatorial tensor framework that explicitly models how latent configurations are organized under structured measurement, using hierarchical tensor representations over discrete domains to encode observable partitions, multiplicities, shell structure, and reconstruction geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Imaging and inverse problems often neglect the explicit representation of observable organization induced by measurement processes; TENSKEL addresses this gap by providing a formal combinatorial framework to capture how latent structures become organized under observation.

Method: Constructs a hierarchy of tensors from a binary latent ensemble via aggregation and folding operations, coupling measurement contexts to a latent Pascal organization; introduces a tensor kernel encoding similarity via measurement-context responses and uses regularized inversion for structured reconstruction; extends naturally to multinomial (simplex-supported) latent representations.

Result: Explicit characterization of combinatorial multiplicities, shell organization, degeneracies, and induced reconstruction geometry; tensor kernel formulation linking latent coordinate similarity to observable responses; compressed (Pascal cellular automata) and multinomial (discrete color mapping) realizations demonstrated.

Conclusion: TENSKEL provides a foundational combinatorial basis for modeling observation-induced organization—enabling principled analysis and design in computational imaging, structured sensing, inverse problems, and quantum-inspired measurements.

Abstract: Many imaging problems seek to reconstruct underlying configurations from partial observable measurements. While reconstruction algorithms operate on these measurements, the observable organization induced by the measurement process is rarely represented explicitly.
  We introduce TENSKEL, a combinatorial observable framework for structured measurement and reconstruction based on tensor representations defined over discrete domains. Starting from a binary latent ensemble, the framework constructs a hierarchy of tensors coupling measurement contexts to a latent Pascal organization through successive aggregation and folding operations. Each measurement context induces an observable partition of the same latent ensemble, and the resulting tensor formulation makes explicit the associated combinatorial multiplicities, shell organization, degeneracies, and induced reconstruction geometry.
  Rather than introducing a new reconstruction algorithm, this framework provides a mathematical representation of how latent configurations become organized under observation. The induced tensor kernel characterizes similarities between latent coordinates through their measurement-context responses, while regularized inversion provides a structured reconstruction of the latent representation from observable measurements. The binary construction further admits a natural multinomial extension to discrete simplex-supported latent representations.
  Connections to Pascal cellular automata and structured discrete color mappings illustrate respectively compressed and multinomial realizations of the framework. More generally, TENSKEL provides a combinatorial basis for reasoning about the organization induced by observation, with potential relevance to computational imaging, inverse problems, structured sensing, and quantum-inspired measurement formulations.

</details>


### [4] [Interacting Lattice Bosons on the Concrete Buchholz Algebra -- All-Temperature Equilibrium States from Functional-Integral Local-Number Bounds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22362)
*Yoshitsugu Sekine*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper formulates interacting Bose--Hubbard dynamics on the Buchholz algebra in Fock space and establishes volume-uniform exponential bounds on local particle numbers—without assuming local moments—under repulsive finite-range interactions and low-activity conditions; these bounds support finite-volume approximations and KMS state construction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To remove the restrictive local-moment hypothesis in rigorous statistical mechanics of interacting bosons, enabling robust construction of equilibrium (KMS) states for the Bose--Hubbard model.

Method: Uses the concrete Buchholz algebra in Fock space to formulate the dynamics; derives volume-uniform exponential local-number bounds via a discrete functional integral under repulsive finite-range interactions and a low-activity gap.

Result: Proves exponential local-number bounds uniform in volume and valid at all temperatures; these imply finite-volume approximations and existence of KMS accumulation states (as referenced in arXiv:2505.13170).

Conclusion: The local-moment hypothesis is unnecessary for controlling local observables in the interacting Bose--Hubbard model; the Buchholz algebraic framework with discrete functional integrals provides a rigorous, thermodynamically scalable foundation for equilibrium quantum statistical mechanics of bosons.

Abstract: Interacting Bose--Hubbard dynamics is formulated on the concrete Buchholz algebra in Fock space. Under repulsive finite-range interactions and a low-activity gap, a discrete functional integral gives volume-uniform exponential local-number bounds at all temperatures. These bounds remove the local-moment hypothesis and yield finite-volume approximation and KMS accumulation states in arXiv:2505.13170.

</details>


### [5] [On the gauge invariant boundary condition in open XXZ chains: SoV bases, elementary blocks and overlaps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22365)
*G. Niccoli,V. Terras*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper solves the open XXZ spin chain with unparallel boundary fields under a special gauge-invariant boundary condition at one end, using a generalized Sklyanin’s Separation of Variables (SoV) approach parameterized by two gauge parameters α and β; it shows SoV bases depend only on α−β, constructs correlation function building blocks in the ungauged limit (α−β→∞), and computes overlaps between eigenstates under modified boundary conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To solve the open XXZ spin chain with unparallel boundary fields—a nontrivial integrable model lacking U(1) symmetry—under a tractable yet physically relevant boundary condition: namely, when the Vertex-IRF-transformed K-matrix at site 1 is diagonal and gauge-parameter-independent, enabling exact solvability via SoV while retaining flexibility for correlation and overlap computations.

Method: Employs the Vertex-IRF transformation to recast the boundary problem into an IRF-type setting; constructs a family of SoV bases parameterized by gauge parameters α (spectral shift) and β (dynamical parameter); proves α- and β-dependence reduces to α−β alone; takes the ungauged limit α−β→∞ to extract elementary correlation function building blocks; uses spectral and algebraic properties of the transfer matrix and boundary K-matrices to compute eigenstate overlaps across different boundary configurations.

Result: Demonstrates that the SoV bases are functionally dependent only on α−β; derives explicit expressions for all elementary correlation function building blocks in the ungauged limit; provides a systematic method to compute overlaps between eigenstates of models differing only in the boundary condition at site 1 (gauge-invariant vs. generic), enabling applications in quench dynamics and boundary entropy studies.

Conclusion: The gauge-invariant boundary condition enables full analytical control over the open XXZ chain via SoV, revealing hidden parameter redundancy (α−β), yielding computable correlation structures in the ungauged limit, and facilitating overlap calculations essential for non-equilibrium quantum integrability.

Abstract: We consider the open XXZ spin chain with unparallel boundary fields, in the special case in which the Vertex-IRF transform of the K-matrix labelling the boundary field at site 1 is diagonal and invariant under the value of the gauge parameters, the other K-matrix being arbitrary. We discuss here several properties related to this special gauge invariant boundary condition. Such a model can be solved in the Vertex-IRF framework with arbitrary gauge parameters: we can diagonalise the transfer matrix by means of generalised Sklyanin's Separation of Variable (SoV) approach in a family of SoV bases that depend on two arbitrary gauge parameters $α$ and $β$, $β$ being the so-called dynamical parameter and $α$ an arbitrary shift of the spectral parameter. We show here that such SoV bases depend actually on $α$ and $β$ through the difference $α-β$ only. Considering the ungauged limit in which $α-β$ tends to infinity, we show how to compute all elementary building blocks for the correlation functions of this model. We finally show how to compute overlaps between eigenstates of this model and eigenstates of a spin chain sharing the same boundary condition at site $N$ and in which the gauge invariant boundary condition at site 1 has been changed to a more general one.

</details>


### [6] [Quantitative Furstenberg Theory for Large Random Matrices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22543)
*Reuben Drogin*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper quantifies Lyapunov exponent theory for the 1D block Anderson model with GOE potential blocks, proving a quantitative limit theorem for the top Lyapunov exponent and a uniform lower bound $c/W$ on all adjacent gaps; as a consequence, it establishes an upper bound $CW^2$ on the localization length.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To make classical Lyapunov exponent theory quantitative for the block Anderson model—specifically, to obtain explicit bounds on Lyapunov exponent gaps and thereby derive a rigorous, polynomial upper bound on the localization length.

Method: Combines Furstenberg-type formulas for Lyapunov exponents with Malliavin calculus–style arguments on the symplectic group to establish smoothness of the density of long products of transfer matrices; the key technical tool is a least singular value estimate for a structured random matrix.

Result: 1) Quantitative limit theorem for the top Lyapunov exponent; 2) Uniform gap lower bound $\lambda_i - \lambda_{i+1} \geq c/W$ for all consecutive Lyapunov exponents; 3) Localization length bounded above by $CW^2$.

Conclusion: The spectral localization in the 1D block Anderson model with GOE blocks is rigorously controlled at polynomial scale: the inverse localization length (i.e., smallest positive Lyapunov exponent) is at least of order $1/W$, implying exponential decay of eigenfunctions with characteristic length $\lesssim W^2$.

Abstract: We consider the $2W\times 2W$ transfer matrices associated to the block Anderson model with GOE potential blocks. We make the classical Lyapunov exponent theory quantitative in two ways. First, we prove a quantitative limit theorem for the top Lyapunov exponent. Second, we prove every gap between Lyapunov exponents is at least $c/W$. As a corollary, this implies the localization length of this $1$d block Anderson model is at most $CW^2$. The proof uses Furstenberg type formulas for the Lyapunov exponents, and Malliavin calculus style arguments in the symplectic group to show the product of sufficiently many transfer matrices has a sufficiently smooth density. The main technical input for the latter is a least singular value estimate for a structured random matrix.

</details>


### [7] [Fractality of the Schrödinger Density for Rough Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22547)
*Masoud Ataei*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper generalizes the fractal nature of the Talbot effect's intensity pattern (density) for arbitrary real initial data of bounded variation with jumps, proving that its graph has upper box dimension 3/2 almost everywhere in time; the critical Sobolev mass diverges logarithmically at a rate determined solely by Wiener’s jump statistic—revealing a direct link between observed intensity spectra and grating discontinuities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the known fractality of Talbot carpets—previously established only for step functions with rational jumps—to arbitrary real initial data of bounded variation possessing at least one jump, and to quantify how spectral features encode jump structure.

Method: Rigorous analysis of the free Schrödinger evolution on the torus using tools from harmonic analysis, fractal geometry (upper box dimension), Sobolev space theory, and Wiener’s jump statistic; leverages derangetropy operators and rank calculus to connect probability laws to quantum carpets; includes comparison with Airy flow for dispersion-relation sensitivity.

Result: For generic initial data of bounded variation with jumps: (i) the intensity density is fractal almost surely in time with upper box dimension exactly 3/2; (ii) the critical Sobolev norm diverges logarithmically at a rate given in closed form by Wiener’s jump statistic—immune to jump positions/phases; (iii) the same rate scales as 1/2 along time traces and ×2 for Airy flow; (iv) Gauss-sum revivals occur on quantile cells at rational times; (v) spectral growth of a single intensity profile uniquely determines jump content.

Conclusion: The Talbot effect’s intensity fractality is universal beyond rational steps, governed by jump statistics via Wiener’s functional; the critical Sobolev mass serves as a dispersion-sensitive, experimentally accessible probe of grating singularities—enabling quantitative inference of discontinuity structure from optical or matter-wave interference measurements.

Abstract: The observable of the Talbot effect is the intensity behind the grating: the density of the evolving field, not the field itself. Its fractality was previously known only for step data with rational jumps. We prove the general case. For arbitrary real data of bounded variation with at least one jump, the density of the free Schrödinger evolution on the torus is fractal at almost every time, with graph of upper box dimension exactly three halves. Its critical Sobolev mass diverges logarithmically, at a rate given in closed form by Wiener's jump statistic of the datum and independent of the positions and phases of the jumps. The same rate is halved along time traces and doubled for the Airy flow, so the critical mass probes the dispersion relation. Transported by the rank calculus of derangetropy operators, the theory equips every probability law with a quantum carpet: Gauss-sum revivals on quantile cells at rational times, a universal fractal at almost every other. The spectral growth rate of a single measured intensity profile returns the jump content of the grating, a prediction open to test in optical and matter-wave interferometry.

</details>


### [8] [Correlation inequalities for transversal field models with application to quantum glasses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23177)
*Chokri Manai,Simone Warzel*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a general correlation inequality for transversal field quantum spin models using Gaussian convolution estimates and Brascamp-Lieb techniques, identifying distinct low- and high-field phases in quantum spin glasses (e.g., SK and EA models), and derives logarithmic Sobolev inequalities to prove overlap concentration and high-field pressure asymptotics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand phase structure—particularly the existence of replica-symmetric vs. replica-symmetry-broken regimes—in quantum spin systems with transversal fields, especially quantum spin glasses where quantum fluctuations compete with disorder and interactions.

Method: Extends the Ding-Song-Sun inequality and applies Brascamp-Lieb convexity to derive a Gaussian convolution-based correlation inequality; combines it with prior low-field results; uses derived logarithmic Sobolev inequalities to analyze concentration and asymptotics of overlaps and pressure.

Result: A general correlation inequality valid in the high-transversal-field regime; proof of a high-field replica-symmetric phase (no replica order) in quantum SK and EA models; concentration of self- and replica-overlaps; exact high-field asymptotics of the pressure in the quantum SK model.

Conclusion: Quantum spin glasses exhibit a sharp transition between low-field replica-symmetry-breaking and high-field replica-symmetric phases; the developed functional-analytic tools provide a rigorous framework for studying correlations and thermodynamic limits in transversal field models.

Abstract: We study the class of transversal field models with pair interactions, including the quantum Curie-Weiss and the transversal field Ising model, as well as quantum spin glasses based on the Sherrington-Kirkpatrick (SK) and Edwards-Anderson models. Our main result is a general correlation inequality based on a Gaussian convolution estimate, which is derived from an extension of the Ding-Song-Sun inequality and an application of Brascamp-Lieb convexity techniques. The results apply for sufficiently strong transversal fields and, for quantum spin glasses, yield a high-field regime without replica order. Combined with the previously established low-field replica-symmetry-breaking regime, this gives distinct low- and high-field overlap phases. As a corollary of the logarithmic Sobolev inequalities for the underlying functional integrals derived here, we show the concentration of the self- and replica-overlap, and determine the high-field asymptotics of the pressure in the quantum SK model.

</details>


### [9] [Universality of the conformal anomaly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23201)
*Sid Maibach,Eveliina Peltola*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a universal property for local real one-dimensional modular functors—central extensions of the sewing operation on Segal moduli spaces—characterized solely by central charge; it identifies the disk-disk cocycle with the universal Liouville action (loop Loewner energy), thereby mathematically explaining the role of central charge in CFT and SLE, and proving uniqueness of the Brownian loop measure as the canonical SLE restriction function.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous mathematical foundation for the appearance of central charge in conformal field theory (CFT) and Schramm–Loewner evolution (SLE), and to resolve the conjectured uniqueness of the Brownian loop measure as the canonical restriction function for SLE.

Method: Proof of a universal property for local real one-dimensional modular functors via real-valued cocycles on sewn surfaces; identification of the disk-disk cocycle with the universal Liouville action (loop Loewner energy); use of Segal’s infinite-dimensional moduli space framework and analogy to Mumford’s theorem in the complex setting.

Result: Modular functors are uniquely determined by their central charge; the disk-disk cocycle equals the universal Liouville action (loop Loewner energy); the real determinant line bundle—encoding CFT trace anomaly and SLE restriction function—is canonically associated; Brownian loop measure is proven unique as the canonical SLE restriction function.

Conclusion: The central charge emerges universally from the algebraic structure of real modular functors, unifying CFT and SLE through Liouville/Loewner geometry; this resolves foundational questions about uniqueness and geometric meaning of central charge in stochastic and quantum conformal settings.

Abstract: We prove a universal property of local real one-dimensional modular functors, which may be thought of as central extensions of the sewing operation on the (infinite-dimensional) Segal moduli spaces. Such modular functors are characterized by one real parameter: their central charge: Our result is an analogue of ``Mumford's theorem,'' that appeared in Segal's monograph [Seg88, Seg04] in the complex case. Algebraically, the modular functors are characterized by real-valued cocycles on pairs of surfaces under sewing. We identify the disk-disk cocycle as the universal Liouville action, also known as loop Loewner energy.
  The guiding example is the real determinant line bundle encoding the trace anomaly of conformal field theories (CFT) and the restriction function of Schramm-Loewner evolution (SLE) loop measures. Our result thus gives a mathematical explanation for the appearance of the central charge in CFT and SLE, and proves the conjectured uniqueness of Brownian loop measureas the canonical restriction function for SLE.

</details>


### [10] [Elements of Topology, Differential Geometry and General Relativity for Physicists: A Mathematica-based Tutorial Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23266)
*Balasubramanian Ananthanarayan,Souvik Bera,Subhasish Chakrabarty,Souradeep Das,Amitabha Lahiri,Suhas Sheikh,Sarthak Talukdar*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: A self-contained, tutorial textbook introducing topology, differential geometry, and general relativity using a Mathematica-based, computational approach with original, runnable notebooks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge abstract mathematical concepts in theoretical physics with hands-on computation, enabling students and researchers to verify, extend, and internalize topological, geometric, and relativistic constructions through executable code.

Method: Pedagogical exposition combined with original, author-written Mathematica notebooks; structured progression from point-set topology → differential geometry (manifolds, tensors, forms, connections) → general relativity; emphasis on coordinate-free formulations and their algorithmic realization.

Result: A comprehensive, computationally grounded resource featuring runnable notebooks for all key demonstrations—including homotopy, Lie derivatives, exterior calculus, gauge-theoretic geometry (fiber bundles, connections), and GR curvature/field equation computations for standard spacetimes.

Conclusion: The book successfully unifies mathematical rigor and reproducible computation, establishing a new standard for teaching advanced geometric physics by making abstract theory tangible, verifiable, and extensible via Mathematica.

Abstract: This book is a self-contained, tutorial introduction to topology, differential geometry, and general relativity for students and researchers in physics. Its distinguishing feature is a Mathematica-based approach that makes abstract constructions concrete through explicit, runnable notebooks and worked examples. A key contribution is a large collection of original Mathematica notebooks, written by the authors themselves, that let readers run, verify, and extend every demonstration. The first part covers point-set topology, topological spaces, continuity of maps, homotopy, and the fundamental group with applications that highlight the role of topology in modern physics. The second and largest part develops differential geometry from the ground up: manifolds, tangent and dual spaces, vector fields, pullbacks and pushforwards, Lie brackets and Lie algebras, local flows, and the Lie derivative. It then treats tensors, differential forms, the exterior derivative, volume forms, the metric tensor, and Hodge duality, emphasizing coordinate-free formulations and their computational realization. These tools are applied to Maxwell's equations in the language of forms and the generalized Stokes theorem, while Lie groups, fiber bundles, connections, and curvature bridge geometry and gauge-theoretic physics. The final part uses this framework to present general relativity, computing curvature tensors and field equations for standard spacetimes with dedicated Mathematica packages. Throughout, the book balances mathematical rigor with hands-on computation, enabling readers both to understand the theory and to reproduce every result themselves.

</details>


### [11] [Instantons in a Double-Well are Poisson Distributed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23342)
*Jacob Shapiro*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper rigorously derives the instanton-induced eigenvalue splitting in a semiclassical double-well Schrödinger operator via a probabilistic (Brownian bridge) decomposition of the heat-kernel trace, identifying the hopping coefficient ρ_λ and proving its exponential asymptotics yield the standard instanton formula for E₁ − E₀.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a mathematically rigorous foundation for the dilute instanton gas picture—commonly used heuristically in quantum mechanics and field theory—for eigenvalue splitting in symmetric double-well potentials, bridging semiclassical analysis and stochastic path integrals.

Method: Uses a localized Feynman–Kac formula to represent the heat-kernel trace; decomposes Brownian bridge paths by number of passages between shrinking neighborhoods of the two wells; defines and analyzes the hopping coefficient ρ_λ via boundary layer integrals involving Dirichlet eigenfunctions on half-spaces; employs large-deviation scaling (β = N/|ρ_λ|) to show Poisson convergence of passage counts; extracts asymptotics of |ρ_λ| via logarithmic equivalence to the Agmon distance S(d,−d).

Result: Proves that the ground-excited eigenvalue splitting satisfies E₁(λ) − E₀(λ) = 2|ρ_λ|(1 + o(1)), with −λ⁻¹ log|ρ_λ| → S(d,−d), thereby recovering the instanton prediction directly from a factorized heat-kernel trace without ad hoc approximations.

Conclusion: The dilute instanton picture is not merely heuristic: it emerges rigorously from a probabilistic decomposition of the heat kernel, where the hopping coefficient ρ_λ encodes the non-perturbative tunneling amplitude, and its exponential asymptotics are governed by the Agmon distance—establishing a precise link between stochastic analysis, spectral theory, and semiclassical instanton calculus.

Abstract: We give a rigorous realization of the dilute instanton picture for a semiclassical Schrödinger operator with a symmetric double-well potential on $\mathbb{R}^n$. Using a localized Feynman--Kac representation, we decompose the heat-kernel trace according to the number of passages made by a Brownian bridge between shrinking neighborhoods of the two wells. We identify the weight of one passage with a hopping coefficient $ρ_λ$,
  $\displaystyle ρ_λ=
\int_{\partialΩ} \left( \nabla\overline{\varphi_{λ,0}^Ω}\, \varphi_{λ,0}^{-Ω} -
\overline{\varphi_{λ,0}^Ω}\, \nabla\varphi_{λ,0}^{-Ω} \right)\cdotν. $
  On the exponentially long time scale $β=N/\lvertρ_λ\rvert$, the number of passages converges, for every fixed $N>0$, to a Poisson random variable of mean $N$. We identify $-\frac{1}λ\log\lvertρ_λ\rvert\to S(d,-d)$ and obtain
  $\displaystyle E_1(λ)-E_0(λ) =
2\lvertρ_λ\rvert\left(1+o(1)\right). $
  Thus the familiar instanton expansion of the double-well eigenvalue splitting emerges directly from a factorization of the heat-kernel trace.

</details>


### [12] [Bound States in Exactly Solvable Polaritonic Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23535)
*Sam Johar,Ryogo Katahira,Joseph Kraisler*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates bound states in a 1D scalar model of an effective-mass quasiparticle coupled to a continuum of two-level atoms (with ≤1 excitation), proving a general existence theorem for bound states under sign-definite, spatially localized density perturbations, and solving several exactly solvable cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish rigorous conditions for the existence of bound states in a quantum impurity model where a quasiparticle interacts with a structured atomic bath (two-level atoms with single-excitation constraint), bridging spectral theory and open quantum systems.

Method: Mathematical spectral analysis: derivation and proof of a general existence theorem for bound states under sign-definite, compactly supported perturbations of atomic density; exact analytical solutions for specific density profiles (e.g., step, delta, rectangular potentials) using integral equation techniques and dispersion relation analysis.

Result: A rigorous sufficient condition — sign-definiteness and spatial localization of the atomic density perturbation — guarantees at least one bound state below the continuous spectrum; explicit dispersion relations and bound-state energies are obtained for several solvable configurations.

Conclusion: Bound states robustly emerge from localized, sign-definite modulations of the atomic bath density, irrespective of fine details, confirming the generality of confinement mechanisms in such hybrid quasiparticle–atom systems.

Abstract: We study a one-dimensional scalar model for an effective-mass quasiparticle field coupled to a continuum of two-level atoms with at most one excitation present. We prove a general theorem on the existence of bound states under sign-definite spatially localized perturbations of constant atomic density and analyze the bound states for several exactly solvable examples.

</details>

### [13] [Calculation of neutron electric dipole moment from Lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22587)
*Thomas Blum,Fangcheng He,Taku Izubuchi,Luchang Jin,Hiroshi Ohki,Sergey Syritsyn*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes the neutron electric dipole moment (nEDM) sensitivity to the QCD theta parameter using lattice QCD with background electric fields and the Feynman-Hellmann theorem, yielding dn = −0.0050(4)(8) θ̄ e·fm; extrapolation to the physical point gives |θ̄| ≲ 10⁻¹¹, confirming the strong-CP problem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether experimental nEDM bounds imply the strong-CP problem — i.e., unnaturally small QCD vacuum angle θ̄ — by nonperturbatively computing the nEDM–θ relationship from first principles.

Method: Lattice QCD with 2+1 dynamical domain wall fermions; background electric field applied to induce spatial polarization; nucleon states with mixed spatial parity constructed via variational analysis; nEDM extracted from topological charge density matrix elements using the Feynman-Hellmann theorem.

Result: dn = −0.0050(4)(8) θ̄ e·fm at the physical point; combined statistical and systematic uncertainty; constraint |θ̄| ≲ 10⁻¹¹ from current experimental nEDM bound.

Conclusion: The result confirms the strong-CP problem exists in QCD, as θ̄ must be unnaturally small; the method provides a robust, systematically controlled framework for computing nEDM from local topological structure, extendable to other CP-violating operators.

Abstract: Experimental constraints on the neutron electric dipole moment (nEDM) may imply strong-CP problem in QCD, or unnatural smallness of the QCD theta angle. In this work, we present a novel determination of the neutron electric dipole moment (nEDM) $d_n$ sensitivity to theta term from nonperturbative QCD on a lattice with background electric field. Using Feynman-Hellmann theorem, we compute nEDM from the matrix element of local topological charge density between nucleon ground states spatially polarized by an electric field. These states have mixed spatial parity, and we construct them using variational analysis. We obtain statistically significant signal for the theta induced nEDM from lattices with 2+1 dynamical domain wall fermions corresponding to pion masses of 340, 420, and 576 MeV and lattice spacing $a\approx 0.11~\text{fm}$. After extrapolating to the physical point, we obtain $d_n=-0.0050(4)(8)\barθ$ e$\cdot$fm. Comparison with the current experimental bound on nEDM implies constraint $|\barθ|\lesssim 10^{-11}$, which confirms existence of the strong-CP problem in QCD. Our pioneering work demonstrates that neutron EDM can be reliably determined from the local density of topological charge with robust control of systematic effects, and can be directly extended to other CP-violating interactions.

</details>

### [14] [A Hydrogen-Like Model for Pion and Kaon Masses Based on Dirac Quark-Hamiltonians and Numerical Methods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21459)
*Zhenhua Ning,A. S. Davuluru,H. Raposo,Jong-Ping Hsu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a relativistic hydrogen-like model for meson mass spectra using a novel Yang-Mills symmetry and fourth-order field equation yielding linear confinement; it replaces QCD's three color charges with a single confining charge $f_c$, achieves ~18% relative error fit to 31 pion/kaon masses, and identifies $f_c$ as the fundamental length scale (~8.87 fm).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a simplified yet predictive relativistic model for meson mass spectra that avoids the complexity of full QCD while retaining essential confinement physics—specifically, replacing the three-color SU(3) structure with a minimal confining charge framework.

Method: Constructs a Lagrangian with a new general Yang-Mills symmetry leading to a fourth-order field equation; derives Dirac Hamiltonians incorporating two short-range confining potentials; numerically solves for energy eigenvalues; classifies mesons by isospin, G-parity, total angular momentum, and parity; fits parameters to experimental pion and kaon masses.

Result: Obtains energy eigenvalues in natural units; achieves robust fit to 31 pion and kaon masses (140–2300 MeV) with ≈18% mean relative percentage error; determines confining charge $f_c = 0.045\,\text{MeV}^{-1} = 8.87\,\text{fm}$ as the characteristic length scale.

Conclusion: A single confining charge $f_c$ suffices to reproduce meson mass spectra semi-quantitatively within a relativistic hydrogen-like framework, suggesting that color degrees of freedom may be effectively reduced in bound-state spectroscopy, and that $f_c$ sets the fundamental length scale of quark confinement.

Abstract: We consider a relativistic hydrogen-like model for meson mass spectra. The model is based on a Lagrangian with a new general Yang-Mills symmetry. The strong interactions involve a fourth-order field equation that generates a linear confining potential. Notably, this model for meson masses requires quarks to carry only a single `confining charge' $f_c$ rather than three color charges. We classify meson mass spectra into sub-spectra defined by isospin, G-parity, total angular momentum and parity. By applying a numerical solver to the Dirac Hamiltonians--incorporating two short-range confining potentials, we obtain energy eigenvalues in natural units. The model has three coupling constants and one parameter. It provides a robust fit of 31 pions and kaons masses (from $\approx 140 MeV$ to $\approx 2,300 MeV$) to within $\approx 18\%$ of relative percentage error. The model suggests that the confining charge $f_c$=0.045/MeV = 8.87 fm plays the role of the basic length in the quark interactions.

</details>


### [15] [Electroweak and Single Top-Quark Conspiracy in Current LHC Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21510)
*Christoph Englert,Roman Kogler,Michael Spannowsky*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes LHC electroweak top-quark and tH production anomalies using SMEFT, identifying dominant contributions from a weak top dipole and triple-gauge coupling deformation; it finds no single renormalisable weakly-coupled UV model (scalar/vector/vector-like quark) fully explains the pattern, proposing instead a multi-threshold vector-like quark sector (singlet, doublet, two triplets), with implications for future collider measurements and VLQ searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain recent LHC rate deviations in electroweak single top and tH production—while multiboson channels remain SM-like—via a unified SMEFT interpretation grounded in gauge symmetry, and to assess whether a consistent, gauge-invariant, weakly-coupled UV completion exists.

Method: SMEFT global fit combining LHC electroweak top-quark and tH production rates with Z-pole observables; identification of leading effective operators; systematic matching analysis of candidate renormalisable UV models (scalars, vectors, vector-like quarks) under gauge invariance and perturbativity; assessment of direct, indirect, and flavour constraints on a representative multi-threshold VLQ realisation.

Result: The data prefer a combination of a weak top dipole operator (cφQ), triple-gauge coupling deformation (cWWW), and positive left-handed top current (cφQ(3)); an acceptable fit persists even with cWWW = 0. No single scalar, vector, or vector-like quark multiplet reproduces the full operator pattern at tree level; a minimal viable UV completion requires at least four VLQ representations (singlet, doublet, two triplets), where tree-level currents arise but the dipole necessitates additional loop contributions.

Conclusion: The observed anomalies point to a correlated SMEFT pattern best accommodated by a multi-threshold vector-like quark sector; this scenario evades current constraints but makes concrete, testable predictions for precision electroweak top measurements, tH/ttH cross sections, and dedicated VLQ searches at the LHC and future colliders.

Abstract: Recent LHC measurements of electroweak single top-quark and top-quark associated Higgs boson production show a number of rate shifts, while related multiboson channels remain close to their Standard Model predictions. Gauge symmetry relates these processes such that deviations can be organised as the low-energy imprint of a common ultraviolet origin. A Standard Model Effective Field Theory (SMEFT) analysis of these rates together with $Z$-pole observables identifies the pattern: a weak top dipole and a triple-gauge deformation carry the largest pulls, accompanied by a positive left-handed top-quark current. An acceptable fit is also obtained with the pure triple-gauge coefficient set to zero. We then ask whether a gauge-invariant model can produce this set of operators and still pass the numerous constraints from experimental data. Within weakly coupled renormalisable matching, no single scalar, vector or vector-like-quark multiplet is sufficient to reproduce the full pattern. A minimal possibility is a multi-threshold vector-like-quark sector (a singlet, a doublet and two triplets) whose currents arise at tree level, while the dipole requires additional loop dynamics that the current sector does not fix. A representative realisation, confronted with direct, indirect and flavour constraints, points to correlated measurements of electroweak single top-quark production, improved $tH$ and $t \bar t H$ sensitivity, and updated searches for vector-like quarks.

</details>


### [16] [Know What You Don't Flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21509)
*Anja Butter,Sascha Diefenbacher,Tilman Plehn,Lorenz Vogel*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces calibrated uncertainty estimation for generative neural networks in LHC physics, using heteroscedastic and Bayesian normalizing flows to model statistical and systematic uncertainties—both with and without explicit likelihoods—and demonstrates it on top pair event generation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Calibrated learned uncertainties are essential for reliable inference and decision-making in high-energy physics, especially for generative models used in simulation and analysis at the LHC.

Method: The authors employ heteroscedastic normalizing flows and Bayesian normalizing flows to learn uncertainties in phase space density; for cases without explicit likelihood, they use classifier-reweighted approximate generative networks trained with heteroscedastic loss.

Result: Both flow-based methods successfully capture statistical and systematic uncertainties; the conditional heteroscedastic flow achieves calibrated uncertainty propagation across all phase space dimensions in top pair event generation.

Conclusion: Heteroscedastic and Bayesian normalizing flows provide a principled, likelihood-free framework for calibrated uncertainty quantification in generative modeling for collider physics.

Abstract: Calibrated learned uncertainties are a key requirement also for generative neural networks in LHC physics. For a toy model with an explicit likelihood we show how a heteroscedastic and a Bayesian normalizing flow learn the systematic and statistical uncertainties on the underlying phase space density. Without an explicit likelihood we train the heteroscedastic loss on a classifier-reweighted approximate generative network. We illustrate our comprehensive approach for top pair events and show how a conditional heteroscedastic flow propagates calibrated uncertainties to all phase space directions.

</details>


### [17] [$Δ$ resonance contributions to QED radiative corrections in neutron and inverse beta decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22845)
*Oleksandr Tomalak,Yi-Bo Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper incorporates the Δ(1232) resonance into pion-induced QED radiative corrections for neutron decay and inverse beta decay (IBD), using heavy-baryon chiral perturbation theory with explicit Δ degrees of freedom. It finds that the Δ resonance does not affect the vector charge renormalization but restores power counting and substantially reduces the QED correction to the g_A/(g^QCD_A g_V) ratio—bringing it to zero within uncertainties—and enhances IBD radiative corrections by 20–30%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical precision in neutron decay and IBD observables by consistently including the dominant baryonic resonance (Δ(1232)) in pion-induced QED radiative corrections, thereby restoring chiral power counting and reducing systematic uncertainties in the comparison of experimental and lattice-QCD determinations of g_A.

Method: Heavy-baryon chiral perturbation theory (HBChPT) with explicit Δ(1232) degrees of freedom; computation of pion-loop-mediated QED radiative corrections involving Δ intermediate states; analysis of renormalization effects on nucleon isovector vector (g_V) and axial-vector (g_A) charges; evaluation of corrections to IBD cross sections.

Result: Δ resonance leaves the vector charge g_V unrenormalized; restores chiral power counting for g_A; reduces QED correction to g_A/(g^QCD_A g_V) to zero within uncertainty; increases pion-induced QED corrections to IBD cross sections by a factor of 1.2–1.3.

Conclusion: Inclusion of the Δ(1232) resonance is essential for consistent, high-precision electroweak analyses of neutron decay and IBD; it resolves a power-counting inconsistency in HBChPT and significantly improves agreement between experiment and lattice QCD for g_A.

Abstract: We incorporate the $Δ(1232)$ resonance into pion-induced QED radiative corrections to neutron decay and inverse beta decay (IBD). Within the framework of heavy-baryon chiral perturbation theory with explicit $Δ$ degrees of freedom, we compute additional contributions and study their impact on IBD cross sections and on the renormalization of the nucleon isovector vector and axial-vector charges. $Δ$ resonance does not renormalize the vector charge. For the axial-vector charge, including the $Δ$ resonance restores power counting and significantly reduces the QED radiative correction to the experiment-over-lattice-QCD ratio $g_A/\left(g^\mathrm{QCD}_A g_V\right)$, bringing it to zero within radiative-correction uncertainties. $Δ$ resonance increases the pion-induced QED radiative corrections to IBD by a factor $1.2$-$1.3$.

</details>


### [18] [Probing Glueball content of X(2370) through heavy quarkonium radiative decays and electron-positron annihilation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23428)
*Ruilin Zhu,Qiang Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes pseudoscalar glueball light-cone distribution amplitudes, proposes an η–η′–G–η_c tetra-mixing scheme, extracts mixing parameters from J/ψ→P+γ decays, predicts ψ(2S)→P+γ rates consistent with data, identifies strong sensitivity of J/ψ and ψ(2S) radiative decays to the glueball–charmonium mixing angle φ_gc for X(2370), and computes electroproduction cross sections for pseudoscalar mesons—including predictions for X(2370)—testable at BESIII and Belle-II.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the nature of the X(2370) candidate pseudoscalar glueball by quantifying glueball–charmonium mixing effects in radiative charmonium decays and electroproduction, leveraging theoretical consistency with existing J/ψ and ψ(2S) decay data.

Method: Light-cone QCD sum rule analysis of pseudoscalar glueball distribution amplitudes; construction of an η–η′–G–η_c tetra-mixing framework; extraction of mixing parameters from measured J/ψ→P+γ branching ratios; prediction of ψ(2S)→P+γ and J/ψ,ψ(2S)→X(2370)+γ decays using mixing-angle-dependent amplitudes; calculation of electroproduction cross sections via handbag mechanism, excluding resonance contributions.

Result: Predicted ψ(2S)→P+γ agrees with experiment; destructive interference in J/ψ→X(2370)+γ occurs at φ_gc≈0.01, in ψ(2S)→X(2370)+γ at φ_gc≈0.11; constructive interference in J/ψ→X(2370)+γ emerges for φ_gc>0.02; electroproduction cross sections for known pseudoscalars match data within order-of-magnitude; first theoretical predictions given for X(2370) electroproduction.

Conclusion: The X(2370) exhibits significant glueball–charmonium mixing, and its radiative production patterns provide a clean probe of the mixing angle φ_gc; combined analysis of J/ψ and ψ(2S) decays breaks degeneracy in mixing parameter determination; upcoming BESIII and Belle-II measurements of X(2370) electroproduction and radiative decays will critically test its glueball content.

Abstract: In this paper, we first analyze the light-cone distribution amplitudes of the pseudoscalar Glueball and point out that the two-gluon and quark-antiquark pair contributions naturally mix at the quantum loop level. Based on the $η-η'-G-η_c$ tetra-mixing scheme and existing experimental results, we analyze the branching ratio of $J/ψ\to P+γ$ with pseudoscalar meson $P$ and extract the mixing parameters of the tetra-mixing scheme. We then successfully predict the branching ratio of $ψ(2S) \to P+γ$, which is found to be in good agreement with experimental results. We also find that the branching ratios of $J/ψ\to X(2370)+γ$ and $ψ(2S) \to X(2370)+γ$ are highly sensitive to the Glueball-charmonium mixing angle: the destructive mixing interference in the branching ratio of $J/ψ\to X(2370)+γ$ occurs at $φ_{gc}\approx0.01$, whereas that in $ψ(2S) \to X(2370)+γ$ occurs at $φ_{gc}\approx 0.11$. Moreover, the $J/ψ\to X(2370)+γ$ branching ratio exhibits constructive mixing interference when the mixing angle is larger than $φ_{gc}\approx 0.02$. Finally, we also study the electroproduction cross sections of pseudoscalar mesons. After excluding the resonance contributions, the theoretical calculations agree with the experimental measurements in order of magnitude, and we also present theoretical predictions for the electroproduction cross section of the $X(2370)$. These theoretical results can be readily tested by current BESIII and Belle-II experiments, and further experimental measurements will reveal the intrinsic nature of the $X(2370)$.

</details>


### [19] [Disentangling Charged-Current Scalar NSI from the Solar Sector at JUNO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21521)
*Adriano Cherchiglia,Pietro Chimenti,Christiane Frigerio Martins,Guilherme A. Nogueira,Pedro Pasquini,Orlando L. G. Peres*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives an analytical expression for electron antineutrino survival probability up to O(ε⁴) including scalar non-standard interactions (NSI), identifies spectral distortions enabling a large mixing angle solution for θ₁₂, and presents the first reactor-based, flavor-resolved NSI parameter constraints using JUNO data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To constrain charged-current scalar non-standard interactions (NSI) in neutrino oscillations using reactor antineutrinos within a quantum field theory framework, addressing limitations of prior flavor-summed NSI analyses.

Method: Analytical derivation of the electron antineutrino survival probability including scalar NSI up to O(ε⁴); spectral distortion analysis; Bayesian or frequentist statistical inference on JUNO’s 59.1-day and preliminary 207.2-day datasets; projection for one-year exposure with solar-sector sensitivity to break θ₁₂–NSI degeneracy.

Result: First flavor-individual NSI parameter constraints from reactor experiments (not flavor-summed); identification of spectral distortions enabling large-θ₁₂ solutions; projected 3σ degeneracy breaking between solar mixing angle θ₁₂ and NSI parameters in solar-sector analysis.

Conclusion: Scalar charged-current NSI can be probed individually using reactor antineutrinos; JUNO enables competitive, model-independent NSI constraints with spectral shape information, resolving key parameter degeneracies unattainable in previous reactor analyses.

Abstract: We constrain charged-current scalar non-standard interaction with reactor antineutrinos within a quantum field theory framework of neutrino oscillations. We derive an analytical expression up to $\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{4})$ for the electron antineutrino survival probability including scalar NSI contributions, and show that these effects induce spectral distortions that admit a large mixing angle solution for $θ_{12}$. Using the 59.1-day and preliminary 207.2-day JUNO datasets, we obtain the first neutrino-oscillation determination of NSI parameters individually, rather than in a flavor-summed combination, from reactor experiments. Assuming one year of data taken, we show that the only solar sector analysis breaks the degeneracy with the solar mixing angle at $3σ$ of C.L.

</details>


### [20] [Radiative decays of the isoscalar $S$-wave $D\bar D$ molecule](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23332)
*Yuan-Jun Gao,Gang Li,Shi-Dong Liu,Pan-Pan Shi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates radiative decays of a hypothetical isoscalar S-wave D-Dbar molecule X₀ (J^PC = 0^{++}) into γρ⁰ and γω within an effective Lagrangian framework with charmed-meson loops, finding a cutoff-insensitive ratio Γ(X₀→γρ⁰)/Γ(X₀→γω) ≈ 2.26 — a model-independent signature of its molecular nature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the hadronic molecular interpretation of a hypothetical isoscalar 0^{++} D\bar{D} bound state X₀ — the spin-0 partner of X(3872) — by identifying robust, model-insensitive observables in its radiative decays.

Method: Effective Lagrangian approach with intermediate charmed-meson (D, D*, D₁) loops; electromagnetic gauge invariance enforced; partial widths computed as functions of X₀ mass and UV cutoff Λ; ratio Γ(X₀→γρ⁰)/Γ(X₀→γω) analyzed for cutoff stability.

Result: Individual partial widths Γ(X₀→γρ⁰) and Γ(X₀→γω) depend strongly on the cutoff Λ, but their ratio is remarkably stable at ≈ 2.26 across physically reasonable Λ variations.

Conclusion: The robust ratio Γ(X₀→γρ⁰)/Γ(X₀→γω) ≈ 2.26 serves as a model-insensitive diagnostic for the D\bar{D} molecular structure of X₀, distinguishable from compact tetraquark or quark-model predictions.

Abstract: In the hadronic molecular picture, an isoscalar $S$-wave $D\bar{D}$ molecule is naturally expected as the spin-0 partner of $X(3872)$. Assuming this state, denoted by $X_0$, to be a pure $D\bar D$ molecule with quantum numbers $J^{PC}=0^{++}$, we investigate its radiative decays $X_0\toγV (V=ρ^0,ω)$ within an effective Lagrangian framework. The decay amplitudes are generated by intermediate charmed-meson loops, with electromagnetic gauge invariance consistently maintained throughout the calculation. We evaluate the partial decay widths and examine their dependence on the $X_0$ mass and the cutoff. Although the individual decay widths exhibit a sizable dependence on the cutoff, their ratio is remarkably stable. In particular, we obtain that the ratio for the decays $X_0\toγρ^0$ and $X_0\toγω$ is approximately 2.26, which is insensitive to the variation of the cutoff. This robust ratio provides a useful model-insensitive signature of the molecular structure of $X_0$.

</details>


### [21] [Photon production from gluon splitting and fusion induced by a magnetic field of arbitrary strength in relativistic heavy-ion collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21600)
*Alejandro Ayala,Santiago Bernal-Langarica,José Jorge Medina-Serna,Ana Julia Mizher*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes photon yield and elliptic flow from pre-equilibrium gluon fusion/splitting in heavy-ion collisions under magnetic fields, using glasma-inspired and anisotropic gluon distributions; results agree well with PHENIX data, particularly for v₂, advancing resolution of the photon puzzle.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the 'photon puzzle'—the longstanding discrepancy between theoretical predictions and experimental measurements of direct photon elliptic flow (v₂) in relativistic heavy-ion collisions—by investigating photon production during the poorly constrained pre-equilibrium stage, especially under strong magnetic fields.

Method: Computes photon yield and elliptic flow coefficient (v₂) from magnetic-field-induced gluon fusion (gg→γg) and splitting (g→γg) processes during the pre-equilibrium stage; employs two models for the initial gluon distribution: (i) a Glasma-inspired Bose-Einstein occupation factor, and (ii) an anisotropic distribution incorporating rapid longitudinal expansion; calculations are performed for arbitrary magnetic field strength.

Result: Both gluon distribution models yield photon v₂ magnitude and p_T dependence in very good agreement with PHENIX experimental data; the computed photon yield is also consistent with measurements; the magnetic-field-driven mechanisms naturally generate significant early-time v₂ without requiring late-stage hydrodynamic flow.

Conclusion: Magnetic-field-mediated pre-equilibrium photon production via gluon fusion and splitting robustly explains the observed v₂, suggesting that the photon puzzle may be resolved by properly accounting for early-time, non-hydrodynamic, field-coupled dynamics—thus challenging the assumption that large v₂ necessitates thermalization and hydrodynamic evolution.

Abstract: We compute the yield and elliptic flow coefficient for photons produced during the pre-equilibrium stage of semi-central relativistic heavy-ion collisions from the gluon fusion and splitting processes induced by the presence of a magnetic field. The calculation is performed for arbitrary values of the field strength. The pre-equilibrium gluon distribution is modeled with a glasma-inspired Bose-Einstein occupation factor, as well as with an anisotropic distribution that accounts for the rapid initial expansion along the beam axis. In both cases, we find a very good agreement between the calculation and the experimental data from PHENIX. Most notably, the shape and strength of the elliptic flow coefficient are described quite well, representing a step towards solving the outstanding photon puzzle.

</details>


### [22] [Textures of dimension-six operators in the SMEFT with non-invertible selection rules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21707)
*Tatsuo Kobayashi,Hajime Otsuka,Morimitsu Tanimoto,Kei Yamamoto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper classifies flavor textures of baryon-number-conserving dimension-six SMEFT operators under non-invertible selection rules, deriving analytic constraints on Wilson coefficients and showing deviations from Minimal Flavor Violation—yielding distinct predictions for B-meson and lepton-flavor-violating observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically characterize flavor structures of dimension-six SMEFT operators beyond conventional flavor symmetries, especially under non-invertible selection rules that go beyond standard assumptions like Minimal Flavor Violation.

Method: Systematic classification of flavor textures of all baryon-number-conserving dimension-six SMEFT operators using non-invertible selection rules; analytical derivation of allowed tensor structures and parameter reductions for Wilson coefficients.

Result: Full classification of flavor textures and associated zero patterns; explicit analytic parameterizations of Wilson coefficients with reduced independent parameters; identification of flavor–chirality patterns inconsistent with Minimal Flavor Violation and conventional flavor symmetries.

Conclusion: Non-invertible selection rules generate novel, predictive flavor structures in SMEFT that differ fundamentally from MFV, leading to distinctive signatures in B-physics and charged-lepton-flavor-violating decays.

Abstract: We investigate the flavor structures of dimension-six operators in the Standard Model Effective Field Theory (SMEFT) subject to non-invertible selection rules. In particular, we classify the flavor textures of all baryon-number-conserving dimension-six SMEFT operators and determine the resulting constraints on their Wilson coefficients. The selection rules determine not only the texture zeros but also the allowed tensor structures of the Wilson coefficients, which can be expressed analytically in terms of a reduced number of independent parameters. We also find that the flavor structures of higher-dimensional operators are not necessarily aligned with those of the Yukawa couplings, in contrast to the Minimal Flavor Violation hypothesis, in which the Yukawa couplings govern the flavor structure of higher-dimensional operators. It turns out that the resulting flavor and chirality patterns differ from those typically obtained in SMEFT with conventional flavor symmetries, providing characteristic predictions for $B$-meson observables and charged-lepton-flavor-violating radiative decays.

</details>


### [23] [Imprints of nuclear shell structure in exclusive vector meson production](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23445)
*Arpita Mondal,Arjun Kumar,Debojit Sarkar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that exclusive vector meson production at small x within the saturation framework is sensitive to nuclear shell structure, with coherent J/ψ production serving as a clean probe—especially for intermediate-mass nuclei like calcium isotopes—while φ production requires explicit inclusion of shell structure before isolating saturation effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe nuclear shell structure via small-x exclusive vector meson production in the color-glass condensate/saturation framework, distinguishing mean-field single-particle effects from many-body correlations.

Method: Uses self-consistent nuclear densities from occupied single-particle orbitals in the quark-meson coupling (QMC) model, embedded in the saturation framework, to compute coherent |t|-differential cross sections for J/ψ and φ production on light, intermediate-mass, and heavy nuclei.

Result: Shell structure modifies coherent |t|-differential cross sections: enhances secondary diffractive lobes for light nuclei; displaces higher-order minima for intermediate-mass nuclei (e.g., Ca isotopes); induces weaker, larger-|t| shifts for heavy nuclei. J/ψ (small dipole) is insensitive to saturation and thus cleanly reflects shell structure; φ (larger dipole) requires shell-aware initial states to disentangle saturation.

Conclusion: Exclusive vector meson production—particularly coherent J/ψ—is established as a novel probe of nuclear mean-field, single-particle structure at small x; provides a baseline for isolating residual many-body correlations at future facilities like the EIC.

Abstract: We report for the first time that, within the saturation framework, exclusive vector meson production at small $x$ is sensitive to the shell structure of the target nucleus. Self-consistent nuclear densities from occupied single-particle orbitals in the quark-meson coupling (QMC) model modify the coherent $|t|$-differential cross section, enhancing the secondary diffractive lobes for light nuclei and displacing the higher-order minima in $|t|$ for intermediate-mass nuclei, whereas for heavy targets the shifts are weaker and appear at larger $|t|$. Because the small $J/ψ$ dipole is insensitive to saturation, these features make coherent $J/ψ$ production a clean probe of nuclear shell structure, most favorably for intermediate-mass nuclei such as the calcium isotopes. For the larger $φ$ dipole, shell structure must be included in the nuclear initial state before saturation effects can be isolated in differential observables at the EIC. Our results establish exclusive vector meson production as a probe of the mean-field, single-particle structure of nuclei at small $x$, and provide a baseline for isolating residual many-body correlations.

</details>


### [24] [Thermal width shift of $Δ^{++}$ in a pion gas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21720)
*Ying Zhang,Peng-Yu Niu,Xin-yue Hu,Kai-Jia Sun,Qian Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the thermal width shift of the Δ^{++} resonance in a pion gas using a nonrelativistic effective field theory, finds a temperature-dependent broadening that peaks near 140 MeV when chiral restoration effects are included, and shows that STAR data require T ≈ 300 MeV—far exceeding hadronic-phase temperatures—implying dominant hot-medium and collective-flow contributions beyond simple pion-gas collisions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify how the Δ^{++} resonance width is modified in hot hadronic matter, disentangle pion-gas collisional broadening from genuine QGP or collective medium effects, and interpret anomalous width enhancements observed in heavy-ion experiments (e.g., STAR d+Au).

Method: Nonrelativistic effective field theory; evaluation of Δ^{++} self-energy via pion-forward-scattering diagrams with intermediate proton and Δ states; thermal weighting by Bose-Einstein pion distribution; analytical extraction of ImΣ → δΓ(T); incorporation of NJL-model temperature-dependent N and Δ masses to model chiral restoration.

Result: δΓ(T) increases with T, reaching ~6 MeV at T ≈ 160 MeV; with chiral mass evolution, δΓ(T) becomes non-monotonic, peaking near T ≈ 140 MeV; application to STAR d+Au data yields T ≈ 300 MeV at p_t = 900 MeV—well above the hadronic phase boundary (~155 MeV).

Conclusion: The observed Δ^{++} width enhancement in d+Au collisions cannot be explained by pion-gas interactions alone; it requires substantial contributions from a hot deconfined medium and/or collective flow—providing evidence for early-stage QGP formation or strong pre-hadronic dynamics.

Abstract: We compute the thermal width shift of the $Δ^{++}$ resonance induced by a pion gas within a nonrelativistic effective field theory framework. The $Δ^{++}$ self-energy is evaluated from pion-forward-scattering diagrams with intermediate proton and $Δ$ states, weighted by the thermal pion distribution. Analytical expressions for the imaginary part of the self-energy yield the temperature-dependent width correction $δΓ(T)$. The width increases with temperature, reaching approximately $6$~MeV at $T \approx 160$~MeV. When the temperature-dependent $Δ$ and nucleon masses from an NJL-model chiral restoration scenario are incorporated, the width shift becomes non-monotonic, peaking near $T \approx 140$~MeV---a consequence of the competition between collisional broadening and the shrinking $Δ\to Nπ$ phase space as the $N$--$Δ$ mass gap closes. Applying our formalism to STAR data for $Δ^{++}$ in d+Au collisions at $\sqrt{s_{NN}} = 200$~GeV, we extract a temperature $T \approx 300$~MeV at $p_t = 900$~MeV, consistent with the experimental extraction within errors. This value significantly exceeds the hadronic-phase temperature, explicitly demonstrating that the observed $Δ^{++}$ width shift is not solely of pion-gas origin---genuine hot-medium and collective-flow contributions must be substantial.

</details>


### [25] [Direct renormalization of flavor-changing currents in vNRQCD for $B_c^{(*)}$ decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21771)
*Jichen Pan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes two-loop anomalous dimensions of flavor-changing nonrelativistic currents for Bc and Bc* meson leptonic decays using direct vNRQCD, bypassing conventional infrared-pole extraction; spin dependence is unified via S(S+1); ultrasoft mixing operators are shown irrelevant at the matching scale ν=1 but important for subsequent running.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain precise theoretical predictions for Bc and Bc* leptonic decays, higher-order QCD corrections—particularly two-loop anomalous dimensions of NRQCD currents—are required. Conventional methods extract these from infrared poles in full-QCD matching; this work instead performs a direct vNRQCD calculation to avoid ambiguities and clarify operator mixing structure.

Method: Direct dimensional-regularized vNRQCD calculation at matching scale ν = 1; renormalization constants for leading flavor-changing currents are computed explicitly as UV counterterms canceling residual IR poles; spin dependence encoded via S(S+1); ultrasoft mixing operators are systematically analyzed for their role in unequal-mass vNRQCD.

Conclusion: Direct vNRQCD computation provides a robust, pole-free route to current anomalous dimensions; the spin-unified formalism simplifies treatment across pseudoscalar and vector channels; ultrasoft mixing is dynamically relevant only below ν = 1 and does not impact the reported fixed-order results.

Abstract: We present a direct vNRQCD calculation of the two-loop anomalous dimensions of the leading flavor-changing nonrelativistic currents that govern the leptonic decays of the $B_c$ and $B_c^*$ mesons. The calculation is performed at the matching scale $ν=1$, where the anomalous dimensions are the ultraviolet counterterms required to cancel the residual infrared poles of the full-QCD short-distance coefficients. In contrast to the conventional extraction from those infrared poles, we determine the current renormalization constants directly in the effective theory. The pseudoscalar and vector channels are obtained from one spin-unified formula, with the spin dependence entering through $S(S+1)$. We also clarify the role of ultrasoft-induced mixing operators in the unequal-mass theory: they are essential for the full velocity running below $ν=1$, but their Wilson coefficients vanish at the matching point and do not alter the fixed-order current anomalous dimensions reported here. The results agree with the known matching calculations and reduce smoothly to the equal-mass quarkonium limits.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the observed dark matter relic abundance without fine-tuned mass degeneracies or strong interactions, leveraging the known baryon asymmetry as a source for dark matter asymmetry.

Abstract: We propose a mechanism in which a primordial baryon asymmetry determines the relic abundance of self-conjugate dark matter through a heavier partner sector. In a simplified model with a Majorana fermion $χ$ and a colored scalar partner $\tilde q$, QCD annihilations deplete the symmetric partner population while a residual asymmetric component survives as a temporary reservoir. For feeble portal couplings, this asymmetry is transferred to $χ$ at late times, after dark matter freeze-out. The final relic abundance is controlled by the amount of surviving asymmetry, the timing of its transfer, and the subsequent dark matter annihilation. Solving the coupled Boltzmann equations with the relevant chemical-potential effects, we find that an intermediate range of portal couplings can reproduce the observed relic abundance without requiring a compressed mass spectrum. The qualitative mechanism remains robust against variations in the mass hierarchy, dark matter annihilation mode, and lepton asymmetry. The small portal couplings also make the colored partner potentially long lived, allowing LHC searches for heavy long-lived particles to constrain part of the viable parameter space.

### [27] [Phase-constrained $Σ^*$ spectroscopy in the pure-$I=1$ reactions $K_Lp\toπ^+Σ^0$ and $K_Lp\toπ^+Λ$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21824)

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional charged-kaon reactions ($K^-p$) mix $I=0$ and $I=1$ amplitudes, obscuring isospin-separated baryon spectroscopy; the neutral $K_L p$ reaction is isospin-selective ($I=1$ only), offering a clean probe of the $I=1$ hyperon spectrum.

Conclusion: Multichannel, phase-constrained analyses of pure-$I=1$ reactions are essential for robust strange-baryon spectroscopy; results provide critical input for the JLab KLF program and enable subsequent extraction of the $I=0$ amplitude from $K^-p$ data, facilitating quantitative study of the $\Lambda^*$ spectrum.

Abstract: Low-energy $\bar K N$ scattering provides direct access to strange-baryon spectroscopy, but conventional charged-kaon reactions mix the $I=0$ and $I=1$ amplitudes. Motivated by the isospin-selective nature of the $K_Lp$ reactions, we present, to our knowledge, the first simultaneous analysis of the pure-$I=1$ reactions $K_Lp\toπ^+Σ^0$ and $K_Lp\toπ^+Λ$ in which a common set of resonance parameters and a fixed relative-phase convention are imposed on both final states. Within an effective-Lagrangian model, a joint fit of available differential cross sections and recoil polarizations yields $χ^2/\mathrm{d.o.f.}=1.604$. We find clear channel complementarity: the $t$-channel $K^*$ exchange is more important in $K_L p\to π^+Λ$, especially at forward angles, whereas $K_L p\to π^+Σ^0$ is more sensitive to the contribution of $Σ(1620)\,1/2^-$. At the same time, $Σ(1660)\,1/2^+$ remains important through interference effects in both channels. These results demonstrate the importance of multichannel, phase-constrained analyses for establishing the $I=1$ hyperon spectrum and provide timely phenomenological input for future high-precision measurements by the KLF program at JLab. Once the pure $I=1$ amplitudes are reliably determined, they will also enable the separation of the $I=0$ component in the much more abundant $K^- p\to π^\pmΣ^\mp$ data, opening a path toward a more quantitative study of the $Λ^*$ spectrum.
