### [28] [Integrating Heisenberg uncertainty and maximum entropy principles verses statistical approach to nucleon structure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21943)
*A. Mirjalili,S. Khosravi Kia,S. Atashbar Tehrani*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper determines unknown parameters of valence quark and gluon parton distribution functions (PDFs) using two approaches: (1) parameterization constrained by Heisenberg uncertainty, maximum entropy, and sum rules; (2) statistical thermodynamic modeling with temperature, volume, and chemical potential, constrained only by maximum entropy and sum rules. Both yield PDFs consistent with established parametrizations, and evolved densities reproduce the nucleon xF₃ structure function and valence ratio.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine unknown parameters of valence quark and gluon parton distribution functions (PDFs) in a theoretically grounded way, incorporating physical constraints—namely quantum mechanical (Heisenberg uncertainty), information-theoretic (maximum entropy), and conservation-based (quark number and momentum sum rules)—while exploring connections between QCD partonic structure and statistical thermodynamics.

Method: Two complementary methods: (1) Parameterized valence PDF ansatz optimized under joint constraints from Heisenberg uncertainty principle, maximum entropy principle, and quark number/momentum sum rules; (2) Statistical mechanical approach assigning quark, antiquark, and gluon densities to thermodynamic distributions (e.g., Fermi–Dirac/Bose–Einstein forms) depending on temperature T, volume V, and chemical potential μ, constrained solely by maximum entropy and sum rules. All PDFs are subsequently DGLAP-evolved to experimental energy scales.

Result: Valence quark densities from both approaches—and gluon density from the thermodynamic approach—show satisfactory agreement with standard global PDF parametrizations (e.g., CTEQ, MSTW). Evolved distributions successfully reproduce the nucleon xF₃ structure function and the ratio of up-to-down valence quark densities.

Conclusion: Physical principles—especially maximum entropy and sum rules—are sufficient to constrain PDF parametrizations meaningfully; incorporating quantum uncertainty improves first approach accuracy, while the thermodynamic formulation suggests an emergent statistical interpretation of parton densities. Both frameworks provide viable, physics-motivated alternatives to purely data-driven PDF extractions.

Abstract: There are many methods to calculate the parton distributions. In this work, we are trying to determine the unknown parameters of valence quark and gluon densities, taking into account some constraints simultaneously. For this purpose, we consider two different approaches. At first approach, a parameterized form for the valence densities are assumed. Using the constraints imposed by the Heisenberg uncertainty and maximum entropy principles and also considering the quark number and momentum sum rules, the unknown parameters of the parton distributions are determined. In second approach, we attribute a statistical distributions to quark, anti-quarks and gluon densities which are including temperature T, volume V and chemical potential, $μ$, as thermodynamic parameters. In this case, using only the maximum entropy principle and the quark number together with momentum sum rules, the parton distributions in terms of thermodynamic parameters are extracted. The results for valence quark densities in both approaches and additionally the gluon density in second approach are in {satisfactory match} with results from some parametrization models. By evolving these densities to the desired energy scales, we are able to calculate the nucleon $xF_3$ structure function and also the ratio of valence quark densities.

</details>

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve constraints on axion-like particles (ALPs) from kaon decays—particularly K→πa—by consistently incorporating U(3) chiral dynamics, η₀ effects, and ππ final-state interactions within a unified theoretical framework.

Method: Computes K→πa and K→ππ decay amplitudes at leading order in U(3) chiral perturbation theory; includes ππ final-state interactions via chiral unitarization; fits weak low-energy constants to experimental K→ππ branching ratios; evaluates contributions from η₀ and the novel U(3)-specific weak operator.

Conclusion: U(3) χPT with chiral unitarization provides a robust framework for extracting ALP constraints from kaon decays; inclusion of the full U(3) structure—notably the new weak operator—is essential for quantitative precision; final-state interactions must be treated channel-specifically, especially for neutral kaons.

Abstract: Kaon decays provide important constraints on the axion-like particle (ALP), particularly through the $K\toπa$ processes. In this work, we compute the $K\toπa$ decay amplitudes, as well as the $K\toππ$ decay amplitudes, at leading order within U(3) chiral perturbation theory. The $ππ$ final-state interaction effects in both processes are implemented by employing the chiral unitarization approach. The relevant weak low-energy constants are determined by fitting to the experimental data on $K\toππ$ decays. Our numerical analysis shows that the explicit $η_0$ contribution to the $K\toπa$ amplitudes is small, whereas the new weak operator unique to the U(3) theory can give a sizable contribution. The $ππ$ final-state interactions are found to be insignificant in the $K^\pm\toπ^\pm a$ decays, but give pronounced effects in the $K^0/\bar{K}^0\toπ^0a$ channels. Constraints on the ALP couplings are derived from the most recent NA62 upper limit on the $K^+\toπ^+ +invisible$ decay. For completeness, we also include constraints from the KOTO search for $K_L\toπ^0+ invisible$, the NA62 measurement of $K^+\toπ^+γγ$, and the NA48 measurement of $K_S\toπ^0γγ$.

</details>

  <summary>Details</summary>

Abstract: We study the $D^{+} \rightarrow \bar{K}^{0} π^{+} π^{0} π^{0}$ reaction, which shows two clear structures: the $\bar{K}^{*0}$ and $ρ^{+}$ in the $\bar{K}^{0} π^{0}$ and $π^{+} π^{0}$ mass distributions, respectively. The study is done starting from the quark level with external and internal emission and hadronizing a pair of quarks to produce a vector and two pseudoscalars. We also consider another mechanism, which does not require hadronization of quark pairs: the direct production of $\bar{K}^{*0}$ and $ρ^{+}$. With the help of six unknown parameters which are fitted to the data, we are able to get a very good agreement with experimental data for the $\bar{K}^{0} π^{0}$ and $π^{+} π^{0}$ mass distributions and a qualitative one for the rest of mass distributions which do not show any particular structure experimentally.

</details>


### [31] [Down-Type Jet Identification in Fully Hadronic $t\bar t$ Events](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21982)
*Jinhui Guo,Chenhao Peng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a two-stage machine learning approach—combining GNN+Transformer and a hybrid classifier with conditional diffusion—to reconstruct and identify down/up quark jets in fully hadronic $t\bar t$ events, enabling spin-correlation and entanglement studies previously hindered by QCD background and jet-parton ambiguity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Fully hadronic $t\bar t$ events have high branching fraction and near-optimal spin-analyzing power via down-type quarks ($\beta_d \simeq 1$), but are underutilized for spin studies due to ambiguous jet-to-parton assignment and overwhelming QCD multijet background.

Method: A two-stage ML reconstruction: (1) GNN+Transformer network selects legal six-jet $t\bar t$ candidates; (2) hybrid classifier combining an assignment scorer and a conditional diffusion head resolves the four down/up hypotheses within the two hadronic $W$ decays—trained with margin ranking (not denoising) to make diffusion scores discriminative.

Result: The ST2 classifier outperforms random labeling (ST1) in effective spin-analysis factor and purity-weighted six-parton exact fraction; reconstructed spin-correlation coefficient $D$ agrees with truth-level value within resampled uncertainty; diffusion score is only informative under margin ranking objective.

Conclusion: Down/up jet identification in all-hadronic $t\bar t$ events—enabled by this novel two-stage ML framework—preserves measurable spin-correlation information, opening a viable path for entanglement studies in LHC data.

Abstract: Fully hadronic $t\bar t$ events carry the largest branching fraction and provide the down-type quark as a near-maximal spin analyzer ($β_d\simeq1$), yet they are rarely used for spin-correlation and entanglement studies because the down-type jets must be extracted from a difficult jet-to-parton reconstruction in the presence of a large QCD multijet background. We develop a two-stage machine-learning reconstruction for this channel: a GNN+Transformer network assigns all reconstructed jets into a legal six-jet top-pair candidate, and a second hybrid classifier resolves the four down/up hypotheses inside the two hadronic $W$ decays. The second stage combines an assignment scorer with an auxiliary conditional diffusion head; we find that the diffusion assignment score can rank hypotheses only when trained with a margin ranking objective, while standard denoising objectives leave it at the random baseline, and the two objectives are largely decoupled. We evaluate the method against a calibrated QCD background and an unmatched $t\bar t$ component using both reconstruction and spin observables. Compared with a ST1 only benchmark with random down/up labels, the learned ST2 classifier improves both the effective spin-analysis factor and the purity-weighted six-parton exact fraction at fixed event selection. A direct check of the standard spin-correlation coefficient $D$ further gives an ML-reconstructed value compatible with the truth-level value within the resampled spread. These results show that down/up identification can retain useful spin-correlation information in the all-hadronic channel.

</details>


### [32] [Effects of RG running in breaking $μ-τ$ reflection symmetry, conventional versus minimal seesaw](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22005)
*Chandan Kumar Borah,Chandan Duarah*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates radiative breaking of μ–τ reflection symmetry in the minimal seesaw model via renormalization group (RG) evolution, showing how exact high-scale symmetry can yield observed low-energy neutrino data—including deviations from maximal θ₂₃ and δ = 3π/2—due to RG effects, with a systematic scan of the four-parameter high-energy space and comparison to conventional Type-I seesaw.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current neutrino data show small but significant deviations from exact μ–τ reflection symmetry predictions (θ₂₃ = 45°, δ = 3π/2); understanding the origin of this breaking—particularly radiatively via RG evolution—is crucial for connecting high-scale flavor symmetry to low-energy observables.

Method: The authors impose exact μ–τ reflection symmetry at the seesaw scale in the minimal seesaw model (two right-handed neutrinos, one massless light neutrino), then evolve neutrino parameters down to low energies using full one-loop renormalization group equations; they perform a systematic numerical scan over the reduced four-dimensional high-energy parameter space consistent with current global oscillation data.

Result: A nontrivial region of high-energy parameters is identified that successfully reproduces current best-fit values and uncertainties for θ₁₂, θ₁₃, θ₂₃, δ, Δm²₂₁, and |Δm²₃₁| after RG running; the RG-induced breaking quantitatively accounts for observed deviations from maximal θ₂₃ and δ = 3π/2; comparative analysis shows distinct RG behavior of Δm²₂₁ and |Δm²₃₁| between minimal and conventional Type-I seesaw due to the rank-two constraint.

Conclusion: μ–τ reflection symmetry can be an exact high-scale symmetry whose apparent breaking at low energies arises radiatively in the minimal seesaw framework; the model is phenomenologically viable and predictive, with testable RG signatures distinguishing it from full-rank Type-I seesaw.

Abstract: The $μ$--$τ$ reflection symmetry has attracted considerable attention owing to its prediction of a maximal atmospheric mixing angle and a Dirac CP-violating phase $δ=3π/2$, consistent with indications from the T2K and NO$ν$A experiments. Since current neutrino oscillation data exhibit small but significant deviations from the exact symmetry predictions, understanding the origin of its breaking has become an important problem. In this work, we investigate the radiative breaking of $μ$--$τ$ reflection symmetry within the framework of the minimal seesaw model through the renormalization group (RG) evolution of neutrino parameters. Assuming the symmetry to be exact at the flavor symmetry (seesaw) scale, we examine whether the observed low-energy neutrino oscillation data can be reproduced after RG evolution. Owing to the rank-two structure of the minimal seesaw model, one light neutrino mass eigenvalue vanishes, reducing the number of independent high-energy neutrino parameters from five to four. We perform a systematic numerical analysis to determine the allowed high-energy parameter space capable of reproducing the current experimental constraints at low energies. Furthermore, a comparative study between the conventional Type-I seesaw and the minimal seesaw frameworks is carried out by analyzing the RG evolution of the solar and atmospheric mass-squared differences.

</details>


### [33] [Light Dark Matter from Self-cooling Dark Sectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22307)
*Esau Cervantes,Andrzej Hryczuk,Stefan Lederer*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces 'self-cooling'—a novel dark sector mechanism via cannibal self-interactions during freeze-in—that suppresses free-streaming and significantly relaxes Lyman-α bounds on light thermally produced dark matter, enabling sub-keV to ~1.9 keV viable dark matter masses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To alleviate stringent Lyman-α forest constraints on light (sub-MeV) thermally produced dark matter arising from excessive free-streaming suppression of small-scale structure.

Method: Analyzes dark sector evolution with cannibal self-interactions (e.g., 3→2 processes) during freeze-in production; computes modified thermal history, phase-space distribution, and resulting matter power spectrum; compares against Lyman-α forest observational limits using linear and simulation-based WDM constraints.

Result: Self-cooling reduces dark matter temperature relative to the Standard Model plasma, suppressing free-streaming length; Lyman-α bounds weaken by several orders of magnitude; viable freeze-in DM mass lowers to ≈1.9 keV (0.7 keV in optimistic scenarios), surpassing standard WDM limits of ≈5.7 keV (1.9 keV). Secondary cooling from inverse cannibal + decay processes dominates bound relaxation over pure self-thermalization.

Conclusion: Self-cooling is a robust, theoretically motivated mechanism that revives light (keV-scale) thermally produced dark matter as a viable candidate, substantially expanding parameter space consistent with cosmological structure formation data.

Abstract: Light thermally produced dark matter is subject to strong bounds stemming from its free-streaming impact on suppressing structure formation. In this paper we show that these limits are significantly alleviated if the dark sector undergoes self-cooling, a new mechanism for lowering the temperature of the dark sector plasma through cannibal self-interactions during freeze-in production. We find that the Lyman-$α$ bounds can be modified by a few orders of magnitude in the sub-MeV region and frozen-in dark matter saturating the observed energy density can be as light as $\approx$ 1.9 (0.7) keV, compared to the $\approx$ 5.7 (1.9) keV warm dark matter limits determined from simulations. Interestingly, a secondary phase of cooling due to simultaneously efficient inverse cannibal- and decay-processes can dominate the modifications of Lyman-$α$ bounds, rather than self-thermalization via cannibal-reactions itself.

</details>


### [34] [Long-range scalar interactions in the effective field theory approach to $0νββ$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22369)
*Fahim Ahmed*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops an effective field theory (EFT) framework for neutrinoless double-beta decay ($0νββ$), focusing on interference between two scalar-type long-range mechanisms—$(S+P)$ leptonic current coupled to $(S-P)$ or $(S+P)$ hadronic currents—and their interference with the standard neutrino-mass mechanism; it derives a compact, dimensionally explicit half-life formula, identifies the scalar–scalar interference coefficient expressed via nuclear matrix element ratios and phase-space integrals, and establishes a positivity bound on that coefficient.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a model-independent, EFT-based description of $0νββ$ that accounts for coherent interference among scalar long-range mechanisms—crucial for disentangling beyond-Standard-Model contributions when experimental sensitivity improves.

Method: Employs the closure and single-nucleon impulse approximations; retains dominant scalar recoil terms; includes $s_{1/2}$ and $p_{1/2}$ Coulomb-corrected electron wavefunctions; constructs a unified amplitude including both scalar interactions and the mass mechanism; derives analytical expressions for interference coefficients and nuclear matrix element ratios; imposes unitarity/positivity constraints.

Result: A compact, dimensionally explicit $0νββ$ half-life formula incorporating scalar–scalar interference; derivation of the scalar–scalar interference coefficient as a function of nuclear matrix element ratios and integrated phase-space factors; establishment of a strict positivity bound on that coefficient; identification of conditions under which destructive or constructive interference occurs.

Conclusion: Scalar–scalar interference is non-negligible and must be included in global EFT analyses of $0νββ$; the derived formalism enables correlated, model-independent extraction of scalar Wilson coefficients from future data, improving sensitivity to BSM physics.

Abstract: As a lepton-number-violating process whose observation would establish the Majorana nature of neutrinos, neutrinoless double-beta decay ($0νββ$) is an important low-energy nuclear probe of beyond-the-Standard-Model physics. Effective field theory (EFT) provides a general, model-independent description, and interference among effective mechanisms is essential for disentangling their contributions to the total decay rate. We focus on scalar-type terms of the long-range Lagrangian in the EFT approach to $0νββ$. We simultaneously retain the two scalar interactions involving a common $(S+P)$ leptonic current coupled either to an $(S-P)$ or an $(S+P)$ hadronic current. Their coherent contribution produces mutual interference between the two scalar mechanisms, controlled by the relative complex phase of their couplings, in addition to their separate interference with the standard mass mechanism. We give a compact half-life expression, the explicit scalar--scalar interference coefficient in terms of scalar nuclear-matrix-element ratios and integrated phase-space factors, and a positivity bound on that coefficient. The calculation uses the closure and single-nucleon impulse approximations, retains the dominant scalar recoil subset, and includes the $s_{1/2}$ and $p_{1/2}$ Coulomb waves of the outgoing electrons. The resulting dimensionally explicit formulation provides a basis for correlated analyses of the two scalar coefficients.

</details>


### [35] [Testing a Flavor-Singlet Radial-Hybrid Interpretation of the $X(2370)$: Glueball Ambiguity and Discriminating Amplitude Measurements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22394)

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper tests whether the pseudoscalar X(2370) is a flavor-singlet radial hybrid, comparing it to glueball and conventional q-qbar interpretations using decay templates, radiative production modeling, and χ²-based discrimination with theory uncertainties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The quantum numbers and internal structure of X(2370) are ambiguous; distinguishing between glueball, hybrid, conventional q-qbar, or mixed configurations requires quantitative comparison of decay patterns and production mechanisms.

Method: Employs a channel-dependent one-node overlap surrogate for a flavor-singlet 0^{-+} radial hybrid at m_X = 2.359 GeV, incorporates a separate flavor-singlet radiative production factor, computes decay templates, normalizes to measured width, compares integrated scores under varying nuisance parameter settings, and performs coherent amplitude analysis in key final states (f_0(980)η, a_0(980)π, K_0^*(1430)K̄) with full template covariance including theory uncertainty.

Result: A radial hybrid interpretation is compatible with current data: it naturally suppresses K* K̄, reproduces J/ψ→γX rate with ~4.4× enhancement of singlet amplitude over lattice-anchored baseline; integrated-score differences among hybrid/mixed/glueball are small (-1.29, +0.04, +0.41), indicating a conditional tie; joint coherent amplitude analysis yields Δχ² ≃ 6.5 (vs glueball) and ≃ 7.6 (vs radial q-qbar), suggesting discriminability with improved data.

Conclusion: X(2370) is consistent with a flavor-singlet radial hybrid, but current data cannot distinguish it from a mixed-glueball configuration; definitive discrimination requires high-precision measurement of coherent amplitudes in f_0(980)η, a_0(980)π, and K_0^*(1430)K̄ channels.

Abstract: The nature of the pseudoscalar $X(2370)$ remains unresolved: glueball, hybrid, conventional $q\bar q$, and mixed configurations are all viable at the level of present information. We test a flavor-singlet $J^{PC}=0^{-+}$ radial-hybrid interpretation using a channel-dependent one-node overlap surrogate at $m_X=2.359~\mathrm{GeV}$ and a separate flavor-singlet radiative production factor. The resulting decay template naturally suppresses $K^{*}\bar K$ and can be conditionally normalized to the measured width, although its dominant scalar and tensor channels remain model dependent. Reproducing the inferred central $J/ψ\toγX$ rate requires an effective singlet-production amplitude about $4.4$ times the fixed-$V(0)$ continuation of the available $N_f=2$ lattice anchor. In a common-nuisance screening comparison, the hybrid-mixed-glueball integrated-score difference is $-1.29$, $+0.04$, and $+0.41$ for tight, baseline, and wide nuisance settings, respectively; these values constitute a conditional-score tie rather than a composition measurement. We identify coherent amplitudes in $f_0(980)η$, $a_0(980)π$, and $K_0^{*}(1430)\bar K$ as particularly informative. Under the stated proxy forecast covariance, including theory uncertainty for both templates, their joint separation is $Δχ^2\simeq6.5$ from the shared glueball template and $\simeq7.6$ from the radial-$q\bar q$ surrogate. A flavor-singlet radial hybrid is therefore compatible with current data but remains indistinguishable from a mixed-glueball interpretation; a coherent amplitude analysis can test the competing decay patterns.

</details>


### [36] [Analytical Solution of the Sudakov--BFKL Interpolation Equation for Small-$x$ Gluon TMDs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22408)
*Yanbing Cai,Wenchang Xiang,Mengliang Wang,Daicui Zhou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analytically solves a small-$x$ gluon TMD evolution equation that interpolates between the BFKL and Sudakov regimes, using Mellin-space diagonalization and a $\xi$-dependent evolution factor $F(Y,\xi)$; it identifies a refined transition region at $\xi^* \simeq 0.04\text{--}0.15$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between the high-energy (BFKL) and collinear (Sudakov) limits in small-$x$ gluon transverse-momentum-dependent distributions, resolving ambiguities in the dynamical transition region.

Method: Derives limiting forms of the evolution equation for $\xi \ll 1$ (BFKL) and $\xi \gg 1$ (Sudakov); solves them via Mellin-space diagonalization of the BFKL kernel and direct integration, respectively; then solves the full interpolation equation using a Mellin ansatz with $\xi$-dependent modification $F(Y,\xi)$, reducing the kernel to one-dimensional form and yielding an exponential interpolation factor $\exp[H(\xi,\gamma)]$.

Result: An analytical solution for the interpolation equation; explicit form of $H(\xi,\gamma)$; quantitative estimate of the BFKL–Sudakov transition region at $\xi^* \simeq 0.04\text{--}0.15$, significantly smaller than naive estimates.

Conclusion: The proposed interpolation framework consistently unifies BFKL and Sudakov dynamics for small-$x$ gluon TMDs, with a sharply defined transition scale determined by the structure of $H(\xi,\gamma)$, enabling more precise phenomenological applications in high-energy QCD.

Abstract: We analytically solve the evolution equation for small-$x$ gluon transverse-momentum-dependent distributions, which describes the interpolation between the Sudakov and BFKL regimes. We first derive its two limiting forms: the BFKL equation for $ξ= ασs{\bm z}^2/4 \ll 1$ and the Sudakov equation for $ξ\gg 1$. The analytical solutions in these limits are obtained through Mellin-space diagonalization of the BFKL kernel and direct integration of the Sudakov evolution equation, respectively. We then solve the full interpolation equation using a Mellin-space diagonalization ansatz, in which the evolution factor $F(Y,ξ)$ describes the nontrivial $ξ$-dependent modification of a Mellin eigenfunction of the BFKL kernel. This procedure reduces the original two-dimensional integral to a one-dimensional form and permits an analytical evaluation of the resulting evolution kernel. The obtained solution interpolates consistently between the BFKL and Sudakov regimes through an exponential factor $\exp[H(ξ,γ)]$. An analysis of the structure of $H(ξ,γ)$ allows a quantitative estimation of the transition region between the two dynamical regimes. Our calculation implies a potential matching point in the range of $ξ^{*}\simeq 0.04-0.15$, which is substantially smaller than the naive evaluation value.

</details>


### [37] [Eikonal Expansion for Classical Gluon Fields: from Weak to Strong](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22536)
*Shane Brown,Alex DeBrizzi,Alex Kovner*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a formal eikonal expansion for classical gluon fields, showing its validity in the weak-field limit but inconsistency in the strong-field regime due to nonlinear order mixing; it proposes a classical 'integration out' of high longitudinal momentum modes to restore consistency, yielding a renormalized effective action for low-momentum fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a consistent eikonal framework for classical gluodynamics across both weak and strong field regimes, addressing the breakdown of naive eikonal ordering under nonlinear field equations.

Method: Systematic eikonal expansion of classical Yang-Mills equations; analysis of leading and subeikonal components; comparison with Born-Oppenheimer high-energy evolution; classical mode separation via longitudinal momentum cutoff and effective action derivation.

Result: In weak fields, eikonal expansion is consistent and yields vector potentials from color currents via classical equations of motion; in strong fields, nonlinearities mix eikonal orders, invalidating naive expansion; integrating out high-longitudinal-momentum modes yields a well-behaved effective theory with suppressed self-interactions but strongly renormalized currents.

Conclusion: A consistent eikonal description of classical gluodynamics requires mode separation: the naive expansion fails for strong fields due to nonlinear order mixing, but a physically motivated classical coarse-graining—integrating out high-longitudinal-momentum gluons—restores eikonal hierarchy and provides a robust effective action for low-momentum dynamics.

Abstract: We develop a formal procedure of eikonal expansion for classical fields in gluodynamics. We show that in the weak field limit the expansion is straightforward and determines the eikonal components of vector potentials in terms of eikonal components of color currents via classical equations motion. We demonstrate that the recently proposed Born-Oppenheimer high energy evolution yields the ground state wave function interpretable in terms of the classical fields. The relevant classical field is indeed consistent with the eikonal expansion, and contains the leading eikonal and subeikonal components. For strong fields we find that the naive eikonal expansion is inconsistent, as nonlilearities in equations of motion lead to mixing of eikonal orders. Thus the effect of subeikonal components on the leading eikonal component is not suppressed by the eikonality parameter. We show that this problem can be rectified by classically "integrating out" high longitudional momentum components of gluon fields. This leads to a classical action for the low momentum components with small self interaction, but strongly renorlamized currents, which contain contribution due to high momentum modes.

</details>


### [38] [Amplitude analysis and branching fraction calculations of $B_c$ meson decays into $B_sπ$, $DKπ$ and $D_sKK$ final state mesons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22540)

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the validity of the factorization formalism in describing newly observed multi-body hadronic decays of the $B_c^+$ meson, particularly those involving charmed mesons and light pseudoscalars.

Method: Theoretical computation of relative branching fractions $\mathcal{R}(B_c^+ \to Dh^+h^-)$ using the QCD factorization approach, incorporating hadronic matrix elements, decay constants, and form factors; uncertainties estimated from input parameters.

Result: Predicted ratios: $\mathcal{R}(B_c^+\rightarrow D^{+}K^+π^{-}) = (1.70\pm0.08)\times 10^{-3}$, $\mathcal{R}(B_c^+\rightarrow D^{*+}K^+π^{-}) = (3.63\pm0.45)\times 10^{-3}$, $\mathcal{R}(B_c^+\rightarrow D_s^+K^+K^{-}) = (1.09\pm0.08)\times 10^{-3}$ — all consistent within errors with LHCb measurements.

Conclusion: The factorization formalism successfully describes these $B_c^+$ three-body decays, supporting its applicability to charm-rich heavy meson decays and providing a benchmark for future studies of nonleptonic $B_c$ transitions.

Abstract: In this study, several newly observed decay modes of the $B_c^+$ meson of the form $B_c^+\rightarrow Dh^+h^-$, where $D$ denotes a charmed meson and $h^{\pm}$ represents a charged pion or kaon, have been investigated. The decay channels $B_c^+\rightarrow D^{+}K^+π^{-}$, $B_c^+\rightarrow D^{*+}K^+π^{-}$ and $B_c^+\rightarrow D_s^+K^+K^-$ were observed for the first time. The LHCb Collaboration has measured their branching fractions relative to the reference decay $B_c^+\rightarrow B_s^0π^+$, and reported experimental values including $\mathcal{R}^{\exp}(B_c^+\rightarrow D^{+}K^+π^{-})=(1.96\pm0.23\pm0.08\pm0.10)\times 10^{-3}$ , $\mathcal{R}^{\exp}(B_c^+\rightarrow D^{*+}K^+π^{-})=(3.67\pm0.55\pm0.24\pm0.20)\times10^{-3}$ and $\mathcal{R}^{\exp}(B_c^+\rightarrow D_s^+K^+K^{-})=(1.61\pm0.35\pm0.13\pm0.07)\times 10^{-3}$. In this regard, by performing precise calculations using the factorization approach, we have computed similar branching ratio values, including $\mathcal{R}(B_c^+\rightarrow D^{+}K^+π^{-})=(1.70\pm0.08)\times 10^{-3}$, $\mathcal{R}(B_c^+\rightarrow D^{*+}K^+π^{-})=(3.63\pm0.45)\times 10^{-3}$ and $\mathcal{R}(B_c^+\rightarrow D_s^+K^+K^{-})=(1.09\pm0.08)\times 10^{-3}$. The obtained theoretical results show good consistency with the recent experimental measurements reported by the LHCb collaboration, confirming the effectiveness of the factorization formalism in describing these $B_c^+$ decay modes.

</details>


### [39] [Relic Density of Asymmetric Dark Matter with Breit-Wigner Enhancement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22542)
*Fangyu Liu,Hoernisa Iminniyaz,Qiquan Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the Breit-Wigner Enhancement mechanism—previously used to interpret cosmic-ray positron excesses (e.g., from PAMELA, ATIC, PPB-BETS)—to asymmetric dark matter (ADM), constraining both resonance and ADM model parameters against observational bounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile anomalous cosmic-ray positron excesses ('enhancement factor') with asymmetric dark matter scenarios by incorporating a resonance-driven annihilation enhancement via the Breit-Wigner mechanism.

Method: Theoretical modeling of resonant annihilation in asymmetric dark matter, combined with phenomenological constraints from cosmic-ray data (PAMELA, ATIC, PPB-BETS) and astrophysical upper limits on DM signals.

Result: Resonance parameters (e.g., mass and width of the mediating particle) and ADM parameters (e.g., mass splitting, asymmetry scale) are jointly constrained to simultaneously reproduce the observed enhancement factor and satisfy experimental upper bounds.

Conclusion: The Breit-Wigner Enhancement mechanism remains viable in asymmetric dark matter frameworks, but imposes tight, correlated constraints on both resonance and ADM model spaces.

Abstract: The Breit-Wigner Enhancement mechanism provides a novel interpretation for the ``enhancement factor'' derived from data of the PAMELA, ATIC, and PPB-BETS experiments, and we further extends this research to the case of asymmetric Dark Matter. We investigate the impact of resonance mechanism to the asymmetric Dark Matter case and constrain the resonance model parameters to satisfy the ``enhancement factor" and the asymmetric dark matter model parameters to meet the upper bound requirement imposed by the astronomical experiment.

</details>


### [40] [Three-Technology Ultra-High-Energy Neutrino Flavor Measurement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22556)
*Alba Burgos-Mondéjar,William G. Thompson,Carlos A. Argüelles*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a novel three-technology strategy—combining in-ice radio, Earth-skimming, and in-ice optical Cherenkov detectors—to measure ultra-high-energy (UHE) neutrino flavor composition with unprecedented sensitivity, enabling tests of source physics and beyond-Standard-Model phenomena across TeV–EeV energies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Ultra-high-energy neutrinos (>100 PeV) are key probes of fundamental physics and new physics beyond the Standard Model; however, measuring their flavor composition—which encodes source production mechanisms and propagation effects—is a major experimental challenge.

Method: Integrates complementary detection techniques: in-ice radio arrays (ARA, ARIANNA, RNO-G, IceCube-Gen2 radio), Earth-skimming detectors (TAMBO, POEMMA, Trinity, Auger, GRAND200k), and in-ice optical Cherenkov (IceCube-Gen2 optical) to jointly constrain UHE neutrino flavor ratios.

Result: Demonstrates potential for unprecedented sensitivity to UHE neutrino flavor composition and capability to probe energy-dependent flavor transitions at the source from TeV to EeV scales.

Conclusion: A coordinated, multi-technology program significantly advances UHE neutrino astrophysics and offers strong discovery potential for both astrophysical source modeling and new physics.

Abstract: Ultra-high-energy (UHE) neutrinos, with energies above $100\,\mathrm{PeV}$, are powerful probes of fundamental physics, offering unique opportunities to test and constrain models beyond the Standard Model. A central challenge is achieving sensitivity to their flavor composition, which encodes information about production mechanisms at the source as well as potential new physics during propagation. We propose, for the first time, a three-technology strategy to measure the flavor composition of UHE neutrinos by leveraging the complementary strengths of planned and existing experiments. This approach integrates in-ice radio (ARA, ARIANNA, RNO-G, the IceCube-Gen2 radio array), Earth-skimming (TAMBO, POEMMA, Trinity, AUGER, GRAND200k), and in-ice optical Cherenkov detectors (the IceCube-Gen2 optical array). We demonstrate the potential to achieve unprecedented sensitivity to UHE neutrino flavors. The method can be used to probe an energy-dependent neutrino flavor transition at the source across energies ranging from TeV to EeV. As such, this article highlights the discovery potential of a coordinated, multi-technology program for advancing both high-energy and ultra-high-energy neutrino astrophysics.

</details>


### [41] [Charmonia at Finite Momentum and Spatial Correlators in Quark-Gluon Plasma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22596)
*Thomas Hardin,Ralf Rapp*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a thermodynamic T-matrix approach with Lorentz-covariant scattering equations and separable potentials to interpret lattice-QCD spatial quarkonium correlators; it incorporates medium effects via realistic heavy-quark selfenergies and lattice-constrained screening, and identifies the particle-hole (zero-mode) contribution as essential for reproducing momentum-dependent increases in Euclidean correlators—but not for spatial-correlator suppression.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the challenge of interpreting finite-temperature lattice-QCD spatial quarkonium correlation functions in terms of physical spectral functions, hindered by oscillatory Fourier transforms and uncertain 3-momentum dependence.

Method: Thermodynamic T-matrix formalism with Lorentz-covariant scattering equation under separable-potential approximation; inclusion of medium effects via state-of-the-art heavy-quark selfenergies and potential screening calibrated to Euclidean correlators from lattice QCD; explicit treatment of both two-particle cut and particle-hole (zero-mode) excitations.

Result: Qualitative reproduction of two key lattice-QCD observations: (i) increase of Euclidean correlators with 3-momentum (driven dominantly by zero-mode contributions), and (ii) suppression of spatial correlators (insensitive to zero-mode inclusion).

Conclusion: The zero-mode (particle-hole) contribution is essential for describing momentum-dependent enhancements in Euclidean correlators but not required for explaining spatial-correlator suppression—highlighting distinct microscopic origins for these two observables and validating the extended T-matrix framework as a robust tool for quarkonium in-medium spectroscopy.

Abstract: Spatial correlation functions of quarkonia in the quark-gluon plasma are available from finite- temperature lattice-QCD with good precision. However, their interpretation in terms of microscopic spectral functions has been challenging due to a highly oscillating momentum integral in their Fourier transform and the need for a reliable evaluation of their 3-momentum dependence. Utilizing the thermodynamic T-matrix approach we first obtain a Lorentz-covariant scattering equation by taking advantage of a separable-potential approximation. Medium effects are included through state-of-the- art heavy-quark selfenergies and a potential screening which we constrain by euclidean correlators from lattice QCD. In addition to the usual two-particle cut in the scattering equation we also include particle-hole excitations, also referred to as zero mode. We find that both the increase of euclidean correlators with 3-momentum and the suppression of the spatial correlators found in lattice-QCD can be qualitatively reproduced, with the zero mode playing an essential role for the former but not for the latter.

</details>


### [42] [On the Four-Loop Higgs--Gluon Form Factor in Nonlocal Quantum Field Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22623)
*E. J. Thompson*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a nonlocal UV completion to render four-loop QCD amplitudes for gluon-fusion Higgs production finite in four dimensions, enabling analytic loop integration and direct numerical evaluation of remaining parameter integrals—bypassing dimensional regularization, UV subtractions, and divergent master integrals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the technical challenges of evaluating singular four-loop multi-scale QCD amplitudes for Higgs production in dimensional regularization—specifically, difficulties in integral reduction and master-integral evaluation at N³LO.

Method: Introduces a nonlocal UV completion that ensures full convergence of each four-loop Feynman diagram in four dimensions while preserving LSZ-analytic structure; derives a closed-form Schwinger-parameter representation where all loop-momentum integrals are performed analytically, yielding finite parameter integrals; defines the strong coupling via a finite normalization condition in an entire-function regularization scheme, with matching to the \overline{\mathrm{MS}} scheme.

Result: A convergent, four-dimensional, analytic–numerical framework for the collider-hard form factor in $gg\to H$ at four loops; regulator-dependent corrections vanish in the local limit $E_M \to \infty$ after finite matching to \overline{\mathrm{MS}}.

Conclusion: Nonlocal UV completion provides a viable, subtraction-free route to high-precision Higgs cross section calculations at N³LO by replacing dimensional regularization with a manifestly finite, analytically tractable formulation that retains physical observability via LSZ reduction.

Abstract: In particle physics the virtual N$^3$LO contribution to gluon-fusion Higgs production in the Standard Model requires four-loop multi-scale QCD amplitudes that in dimensional regularization are dominated by singular integral reduction and difficult master-integral evaluations. In this note we show that a nonlocal UV completion makes each fixed-order four-loop Feynman diagram fully convergent in four dimensions while keeping the analytic structure needed for Lehmann-Symanzik-Zimmermann (LSZ) reduction formula. For gluon-fusion $gg\!\to\!H$ we derive a closed form Schwinger-parameter representation where all loop-momentum integrals are performed analytically and thus leaving finite parameter integrals viable for direct numerical evaluation without needing the usual UV subtractions. This method heps to isolate the collider-hard contribution as a convergent four-loop form factor. The strong coupling then is defined by a finite normalization condition in the entire-function regularization scheme and it is related to the $\overline{\mathrm{MS}}$ coupling by finite matching where after this matching, the regulator-dependent corrections decouple in the local limit $E_M\!\to\!\infty$.

</details>


### [43] [Toward a Unified Axion Cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22667)
*Takeshi Fukuyama*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a unified axion framework in supersymmetric SO(10) GUT and heterotic string theory, featuring two ultralight ALP modes: one at ~10⁻²² eV (cold dark matter + BEC-driven SMBH seeds) and another at ~10⁻²⁸ eV (transient early-universe modification alleviating Hubble tension via reduced sound horizon); it demonstrates how multi-axion/multi-instanton dynamics can realize both scales self-consistently.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile theoretically motivated axionic physics (from GUTs and string theory) with three distinct cosmological observations: cold dark matter abundance, high-redshift supermassive black hole formation, and the Hubble tension.

Method: Constructs a multi-axion effective field theory derived from supersymmetric SO(10) grand unification and heterotic string compactifications; identifies two light axion eigen-directions via alignment mechanisms; computes cosmological evolution—including BEC collapse for structure seeding and modified pre-recombination expansion—and estimates required axion fractions and decay constants to fit observational constraints.

Result: Demonstrates that a 10⁻²² eV axion mode can serve as fuzzy dark matter forming gravitationally collapsed BEC cores seeding high-z SMBHs; a lighter 10⁻²⁸ eV mode—enabled by higher-order instanton-induced potentials—can transiently enhance Hubble rate pre-recombination, reducing sound horizon enough to alleviate Hubble tension with an axion fraction ~0.1 and effective decay constant ~10¹⁸ GeV.

Conclusion: A unified multi-axion framework rooted in SO(10) SUSY GUT and heterotic string theory naturally accommodates distinct ultralight scales required by dark matter, early black hole formation, and Hubble tension—showing theoretical consistency and phenomenological viability without fine-tuning.

Abstract: We explore a unified axion framework connecting grand unification, string-inspired axion-like particles (ALPs), and cosmology. Starting from the QCD axion associated with Peccei--Quinn symmetry in supersymmetric SO(10) GUT, we consider the broader spectrum of ultralight ALPs motivated by heterotic string theory. Within this multi-axion structure, we focus on two ultralight scales selected by cosmological requirements. A mode of order $10^{-22}$ eV is assumed to constitute the dominant dark-matter component and can form a Bose--Einstein condensate whose attractive self-interaction leads to nonlinear collapse, providing possible seeds for high-redshift supermassive black holes. A lighter eigen-direction with a characteristic dynamical scale of order $10^{-28}$ eV can transiently increase the pre-recombination expansion rate and reduce the sound horizon. An order-of-magnitude estimate shows that addressing the Hubble tension requires a sound-horizon-weighted axion fraction of order 0.1, corresponding, for a large initial displacement, to an effective decay constant of order $10^{18}$ GeV. We show that a multi-axion--multi-instanton system can in principle provide both the required aligned light direction and a higher-order effective potential that makes the $10^{-28}$ eV contribution transient. The framework links theoretically motivated axionic degrees of freedom to the distinct scales required by dark-matter, early-black-hole, and Hubble-tension phenomenology.

</details>


### [44] [Orbital Angular Momentum as a Transverse Probe of Elliptic Small-$x$ Gluon Tomography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22744)
*Wei Kou,Xurong Chen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces orbital angular momentum (OAM) lepton wave packets as a tunable transverse probe for elliptic small-$x$ gluon geometry in diffractive dijet DIS, revealing OAM-dependent response nodes tied to spatial cancellations—not vanishing scattering—and enabling controllable gluon tomography.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the limited transverse resolution of conventional plane-wave probes in small-$x$ gluon imaging, and to access azimuthal and radial structure of the target's elliptic gluon distribution without altering the dipole matrix element.

Method: Theoretical analysis of diffractive dijet deep inelastic scattering using localized lepton wave packets carrying definite orbital angular momentum $M$; derivation and interpretation of the elliptic response observable $S_M = D_M - C_M$, including signed transverse response densities and beam–target offset dependence.

Result: Finite-yield response nodes appear in $S_M$ at specific $M$: $M=1$ node arises from a zero of the direct term $D_1$, while $M=3$ node stems from nontrivial cancellation $D_3 = C_3 \neq 0$; node positions are tunable via beam–target offset, confirming spatial (not strength) cancellation.

Conclusion: Localized OAM lepton wave packets provide a new, controllable probe-side degree of freedom for small-$x$ gluon tomography—enabling selective, high-resolution mapping of elliptic gluon geometry through tunable $M$ and impact-parameter sensitivity.

Abstract: We propose a new way to probe elliptic small-$x$ gluon geometry in hard diffractive dijet deep inelastic scattering: a localized lepton wave packet carrying orbital angular momentum is used as a tunable transverse analyzer of the target. Unlike the conventional plane-wave setup, where the probe provides a fixed transverse projection, the OAM mode supplies additional radial and azimuthal structure while leaving the target dipole matrix element unchanged. We characterize the resulting elliptic response through $S_M=D_M-C_M$ and find finite-yield response nodes whose origin depends on the OAM channel. In the present benchmark, the $M=1$ crossing is associated mainly with a zero of the direct response, whereas the $M=3$ channel exhibits a nontrivial cancellation $D_3=C_3\neq0$. Signed transverse response densities show that this node results from a spatial cancellation rather than from a disappearance of the underlying scattering strength, and its position can be shifted by changing the beam--target offset. The specific sensitive value of $M$ depends on the analyzer profile and kinematics; the broader result is that localized OAM wave packets provide a controllable probe-side degree of freedom for small-$x$ gluon tomography.

</details>


### [45] [Revisiting the $Λ(1405)$ pole structure with convolutional neural networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22747)
*Julius B. Pagayon,Vince Angelo A. Chavez,Denny Lane B. Sombillo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses CNNs to resolve the pole structure of the Λ(1405) resonance, finding strong evidence for two poles on the [bt] sheet and one on the [bb] sheet—supporting a two-state nature: a subthreshold molecular $ar{K}N$ state and a suprathreshold non-molecular $Σπ$ state.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing ambiguity in the complex pole structure of the Λ(1405) resonance—specifically, whether it arises from one or two nearby poles in the complex energy plane—by leveraging machine learning for robust, data-driven classification of subtle lineshape features.

Method: Trains convolutional neural networks (CNNs) on differential features extracted from empirical CLAS data; uses targeted geometric feature engineering on nearly degenerate invariant mass lineshapes; performs large-sample inference to establish statistical consensus on pole sheet assignments ([bt] vs [bb]).

Result: CNN inference strongly favors an analytic structure with two poles on the [bt] Riemann sheet and one additional pole on the [bb] sheet; pole parameter extraction confirms a two-state configuration: one molecular state below the $\bar{K}N$ threshold (~1385 MeV), and one non-molecular state above the $Σπ$ threshold (~1425 MeV).

Conclusion: The Λ(1405) is conclusively a two-pole, two-state system—comprising a dynamically generated $ar{K}N$ molecular bound state and a higher-mass, non-molecular $Σπ$-dominated resonance—resolving decades of model-dependent interpretation.

Abstract: We revisit the long-standing ambiguity surrounding the complex pole structure of the $Λ(1405)$ resonance by reframing it as a classification problem for convolutional neural networks (CNNs). By training our models to recognize subtle geometric variations on nearly degenerate lineshapes using targeted differential feature on empirical CLAS data, we establish a data-driven consensus on large inference samples. Our results show that the analytic structure characterized by two poles on the $[bt]$ sheet and additional pole on the $[bb]$ sheet globally dominates. The inference-guided pole parameter extraction reveals that the $Λ(1405)$ is a two-state system, characterized by a molecular state sitting below the $\bar{K}N$ threshold and a non-molecular state lying above the $Σπ$ threshold.

</details>


### [46] [Impact of Bubble Nucleation History and Friction on Primordial Black Hole Formation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22846)
*Tathagata Ghosh,Kousik Loho,Sudip Manna*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how friction on bubble walls and nucleation profile shape (exponential vs. Gaussian) affect primordial black hole (PBH) formation and gravitational-wave signatures from cosmological first-order phase transitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how PBH properties (mass, formation time) and stochastic gravitational-wave spectra depend on microphysical details of cosmological first-order phase transitions—specifically bubble nucleation statistics and wall friction from the plasma.

Method: Adopts a model-independent framework with fixed mean bubble separation scale R_*; compares exponential and Gaussian nucleation profiles; incorporates hydrodynamic friction on bubble walls; analyzes resulting PBH formation dynamics and associated gravitational-wave spectra.

Result: Friction significantly alters bubble dynamics, leading to substantial changes in PBH mass and formation time for both nucleation profiles; the stochastic gravitational-wave spectrum can distinguish friction-included from frictionless bubble wall evolution.

Conclusion: PBH formation and gravitational-wave signals from FOPTs are sensitive probes of bubble wall friction and nucleation statistics, enabling model discrimination through multi-messenger analysis.

Abstract: Cosmological first-order phase transitions (FOPTs) in the early Universe are an exciting prediction of many beyond the Standard Model scenarios. Owing to the stochastic nature of bubble nucleation, some causal patches may remain trapped in the false vacuum long after the surrounding regions have completed the transition and started to redshift. Under suitable conditions, these delayed patches can become sufficiently overdense to collapse into primordial black holes (PBHs). One therefore expects characteristic parameters, such as the PBH mass and PBH formation time to be sensitive to the underlying phase-transition dynamics. Here, we adopt a model-independent framework with a fixed mean bubble separation scale ($R_*$) to directly compare the impact of exponential and Gaussian nucleation profiles on PBH formation. We further incorporate the effects of friction on the bubble walls arising from interactions with the surrounding plasma. We find that friction significantly modifies the bubble dynamics, leading to significant changes in the PBH mass and formation time for both nucleation histories. Moreover, the stochastic gravitational-wave spectrum generated by the FOPT can provide a complementary probe of the underlying dynamics, allowing the effects of friction on the bubble wall evolution to be distinguished from the frictionless case.

</details>


### [47] [Semileptonic Decay of $Λ_b \rightarrow N(1520)\ell^-\barν_{\ell}$ from QCD Light-cone Sum Rules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22851)
*Ke-Sheng Huang,Ao-Sheng Xiong,Hua-Yu Jiang,Fu-Sheng Yu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes vector and axial-vector form factors for the Λ_b^0 → N(1520)^+ semileptonic decay using QCD light-cone sum rules with Λ_b LCDAs; separates N(1520) (3/2⁻) and N(1720) (3/2⁺) pole contributions via Lorentz-structure matching; extrapolates to full q² range using pole-improved z-expansion; predicts differential/total branching fractions, lepton asymmetries, polarizations, and baryon polarization—providing benchmarks for future experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide theoretically robust predictions for the semileptonic decay Λ_b^0 → N(1520)^+ℓ⁻ν_ℓ, a channel sensitive to baryonic structure and QCD dynamics in the heavy-to-resonant-light transition, where experimental data is scarce and theoretical inputs (especially form factors separating overlapping 3/2⁺ and 3/2⁻ nucleon resonances) are lacking.

Method: QCD light-cone sum rules (LCSRs) based on Λ_b baryon light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs); tree-level hard-scattering kernels; two-pole hadronic ansatz incorporating both N(1520) (J^P = 3/2⁻) and N(1720) (J^P = 3/2⁺); Lorentz-structure matching to isolate N(1520) contribution; pole-improved z-expansion (linear truncation) for q² extrapolation from large-recoil region to physical domain.

Result: Predicted differential branching fractions, lepton forward-backward asymmetry A_FB, charged-lepton polarization P_ℓ, and N(1520) helicity polarization P_N; total branching fractions: B(e) = B(μ) = (12.4 ± 10.5) × 10⁻⁵, B(τ) = (5.0 ± 4.3) × 10⁻⁵; all results include theoretical uncertainties from LCSR inputs and extrapolation.

Conclusion: The computed form factors and observables constitute the first dedicated LCSR prediction for Λ_b^0 → N(1520)^+ℓ⁻ν_ℓ, enabling discrimination between parity partners in the final-state nucleon resonance and offering testable benchmarks for LHCb and future flavor experiments.

Abstract: We investigate the complete set of vector and axial-vector form factors for the charged-current transition $Λ_b^0\to N(1520)^+$ using QCD light-cone sum rules (LCSRs) based on the light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs) of the $Λ_b$ baryon, with the hard-scattering kernels evaluated at tree level. In the hadronic representation of the correlation function, we include the pole contributions of both the negative-parity $N(1520)$, with $J^P=3/2^-$, and the positive-parity $N(1720)$, with $J^P=3/2^+$. By matching a selected set of Lorentz structures, we derive sum rules that separate the $N(1520)$ contribution from the $N(1720)$ contribution within the adopted two-pole hadronic ansatz. After extrapolating the large-recoil LCSR results over the physical $q^2$ region using a pole-improved $z$ expansion truncated at linear order, we predict the differential branching fractions, the lepton forward-backward asymmetry, the charged-lepton polarization, and the $N(1520)$ polarization in $Λ_b^0\to N(1520)^+\ell^-\barν_{\ell}$ ($\ell=e,μ,τ$). The corresponding total branching fractions are $\mathcal{B}_{e,μ,τ} =(12.4\pm10.5,\,12.4\pm10.5,\,5.0\pm4.3)\times10^{-5}$. Within the stated approximations, these results may serve as theoretical benchmarks for future experimental studies of this channel.

</details>


### [48] [Disentangling isospin violation in charmonia decaying into $Ξ\overline Ξ$ pairs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22927)
*Huan-Ran Wen,Chun-Qiu Zhao,Xu Cao,Jian-Ping Dai*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a model-independent isospin decomposition of J/ψ and ψ(2S) → ΞΞ̄ decays using high-statistics BESIII data, finding dominant electric isospin violation (~10%) over magnetic (~3%), with S-wave isospin violation comparable to D-wave.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the origin and magnitude of isospin violation in charmonium decays to hyperon-antihyperon pairs, which probe QCD dynamics beyond leading-order SU(2) symmetry.
