Result: Electric isospin-violating amplitudes dominate (~10%) over magnetic ones (~3%); S-wave isospin violation is found to be comparable in magnitude to the D-wave contribution.

Conclusion: Isospin violation in these decays is primarily electromagnetic (electric couplings), not strong-interaction–driven, and exhibits non-negligible S-wave component—challenging simple theoretical expectations and informing hadronic structure models.

Abstract: Based on a model-independent isospin decomposition, we perform a data-driven analysis of $J/ψ$ and $ψ(2S)$ decays into $Ξ\barΞ$ pairs by leveraging the full set of observables from high-statistics BESIII data. We find that the isospin-violating amplitudes are predominantly driven by the electric couplings, with magnitudes reaching approximately 10%, contrasting with the smaller magnetic contributions of about 3%. A partial-wave analysis further reveals that $S$-wave isospin violation is comparable to the $D$-wave contribution.

</details>


### [49] [Neural Boltzmann Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23022)
*Jonas Spinner,Jack Shergold*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces Neural Boltzmann Equations (NBEs) — a physics-informed neural framework combining neural distribution functions, Monte Carlo importance sampling, and natural gradient evolution — to efficiently solve high-dimensional Boltzmann equations for early-universe particle dynamics, enabling precision calculation of N_eff.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classical Boltzmann solvers suffer from poor scalability in high-dimensional phase-space integrals and fixed-grid discretization, hindering complex process modeling and parameter scans in early-universe cosmology.

Method: NBEs integrate (1) physics-inspired neural distribution functions with learnable parameters, (2) Monte Carlo integration with collider-physics-derived importance sampling, and (3) natural gradient descent for time evolution of the distribution.

Result: Demonstrates improved scalability and accuracy over classical methods; achieves precision computation of the effective number of relativistic neutrino degrees of freedom (N_eff) in the early universe.

Conclusion: NBEs provide a scalable, differentiable, and physically grounded alternative to traditional Boltzmann solvers, enabling high-fidelity, parameter-efficient modeling of non-equilibrium particle dynamics in cosmology.

Abstract: The dynamics of particles in the early universe are described by Boltzmann equations, which involve high-dimensional phase-space integrals. Classical approaches use quadrature integration and evolve the system on a fixed momentum grid, which scales poorly to complicated systems and parameter scans, severely limiting the complexity of processes that can be studied. We introduce Neural Boltzmann Equations (NBEs), which combine three coupled concepts to overcome these limitations. First, particle properties are encoded in physics-inspired neural distribution functions, with parameters that can be predicted using neural networks, enabling efficient parameter scans. Second, phase-space integrals are evaluated with Monte Carlo, using importance sampling tools from collider physics. Third, we use the natural gradient method to evolve the system. After demonstrating the individual benefits of NBEs, we use the framework to perform a precision calculation of the effective number of relativistic neutrino degrees of freedom in the early universe.

</details>


### [50] [Integration-by-parts identities in the presence of measurement delta functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23171)
*Saimeng Zhou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces a new integration-by-parts (IBP) identity framework for dimensionally regulated loop integrals with measurement constraints encoded by a delta function δ(φ(ℓ)), enabling systematic derivation of differential cross sections—especially for transverse-momentum (q_T) distributions—within reverse unitarity, validated in tt̄H (LO) and tt̄ (NLO) QCD processes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard IBP identities fail to handle measurement constraints (e.g., q_T = |ℓ_T|) encoded via delta functions δ(φ(ℓ)) when φ is non-linear; existing approaches require distributional derivatives that complicate automation and introduce spurious surface terms. A geometric, constraint-respecting IBP formalism is needed for robust differential predictions in reverse unitarity.

Method: Constructs IBP identities using vector fields tangent to the constraint hypersurface φ(ℓ) = 0; such vectors annihilate δ(φ(ℓ)), preserving it as an overall factor and eliminating derivatives of the delta. The method leverages differential geometry on the loop-momentum space and integrates seamlessly with reverse unitarity for extracting differential cross sections dσ/dφ.

Result: Derives a manifestly constraint-preserving IBP system applicable to arbitrary regular non-linear φ(ℓ); implements and validates it for q_T spectra in tt̄H (LO) and tt̄ (NLO) production, confirming consistency with known analytic and numerical results and enabling fully automated differential reduction.

Conclusion: The tangent-vector-based IBP formalism resolves long-standing technical obstacles in computing differential observables with non-linear measurements in dimensional regularization; it provides a rigorous, automatable foundation for high-precision q_T and related resummation-aware calculations in the Standard Model.

Abstract: We derive a convenient form of integration-by-parts (IBP) identities for dimensionally regulated loop integrals that include a measurement constraint imposed by a delta distribution $δ(φ(\ell))$, where $φ$ is a regular and generally non-linear function of the loop momentum. Non-trivial IBP relations can be generated directly by vectors tangent to the hypersurface $φ=0$, leaving the measurement delta as an overall factor and avoiding derivatives of distributions. This construction provides a systematic route to differential distributions within reverse unitarity for non-linear measurement functions, in particular, for transverse-momentum distributions. We implement the construction and validate it for transverse-momentum distributions in $t\bar tH$ production at leading order and $t\bar t$ production at next-to-leading order in QCD.

</details>


### [51] [Search for Light Scalars in the Two Real Singlet Model at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23183)
*Aman Desai,Kristin Lohwasser,Mohamed Ouchemhou,Tania Robens,Prasenjit Sanyal*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies exotic decays h2 → h1h1 in the Two Real Singlet Model (TRSM) with light CP-even scalars, proposing associated production pp → Vh2 (V = W,Z) as a clean probe; it evaluates sensitivity to the 4b+ℓνℓ final state at LHC Run 3 and HL-LHC, finding up to ~12σ significance for Wh2 production.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a complementary and experimentally clean probe of light scalar cascades in the TRSM, beyond conventional gluon-gluon or vector-boson fusion production mechanisms.

Method: Analyzes associated production pp → Vh2 (V = W,Z) followed by h2 → h1h1 → 4b and leptonic W decay, computing signal significance for the 4b+ℓνℓ channel at √s = 13.6 TeV for LHC Run 3 (300 fb⁻¹) and HL-LHC (3000 fb⁻¹).

Result: At 300 fb⁻¹, Wh2 yields ~4σ (W⁺) and ~3σ (W⁻); at 3000 fb⁻¹, significances rise to ~12σ and ~8σ, respectively — demonstrating strong discovery potential for light-scalar associated production.

Conclusion: Associated production pp → Vh2 provides a highly sensitive and experimentally clean channel to probe exotic scalar decays h2 → h1h1 in the TRSM, especially for light h1/h2 in the 20–120 GeV mass range, with compelling reach at HL-LHC.

Abstract: We investigate exotic scalar decays $h_2 \to h_1 h_1$ in the Two Real Singlet Model (TRSM), focusing on light CP-even scalars with $20 \le M_{1} \le 60$~GeV and $20 \le M_{2} \le 120$~GeV. Unlike previous studies focused on gluon--gluon fusion or vector-boson fusion production, we explore associated production with electroweak gauge bosons, $pp \to Vh_2$ {\sl($V = W,Z$)}, as a complementary and experimentally clean probe of light scalar cascades in the TRSM. The subsequent decay $h_1 \to b\bar{b}$ leads to final states containing four $b$-jets accompanied by a charged lepton and missing transverse energy. We evaluate the sensitivity of the $4b+\ellν_\ell$ channel at $\sqrt{s}=13.6$~\TeV for the LHC Run~3 and the HL-LHC.
  For an integrated luminosity of $300~\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$, the $W^+h_2$ channel provides the strongest sensitivity, reaching a significance of approximately $4σ$, while the $W^-h_2$ channel reaches approximately $3σ$. At the HL-LHC with $3000~\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$, the corresponding significances increase to approximately $12σ$ and $8σ$, respectively, highlighting the strong discovery potential of associated light-scalar production in the TRSM.

</details>


### [52] [Resonance-continuum interference with a possible tensor $X(6900)$ in radiative double-charmonium production at $\sqrt{s}=10.58~{\rm GeV}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23408)
*Meng-Kun Jia,Yi-Jie Li,Guang-Zhi Xu,Kui-Yong Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies radiative double-charmonium production e+e−→HHγ (H=J/ψ, ηc) at √s=10.58 GeV, incorporating coherent interference between nonresonant (color-singlet NRQCD) and resonant (X(6900), J^PC=2^++) amplitudes; it finds strong phase-dependent resonance structure in J/ψJ/ψγ (~0.038–0.111 fb near M_HH ≈ M_X), but only weak interference and negligible rate in η_cη_cγ, highlighting channel-specific dynamics and necessity of amplitude-level treatment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the nature and coupling of the fully charmed tetraquark candidate X(6900) (J^PC = 2^++) via coherent interference with nonresonant double-charmonium radiative production, distinguishing its impact across different final states (J/ψJ/ψγ vs η_cη_cγ).

Method: Computes nonresonant e+e−→HHγ amplitudes in color-singlet non-relativistic QCD (NRQCD); incorporates X(6900) as a narrow 2^++ tensor resonance added coherently to the continuum amplitude; analyzes invariant-mass spectra and cross sections with full interference.

Result: Strong, phase-sensitive resonant enhancement in J/ψJ/ψγ near M_HH ≈ 6.9 GeV, yielding σ ≈ 0.038–0.111 fb; η_cη_cγ is continuum-dominated with only tiny localized interference and highly suppressed rate (<10^−3 fb), making experimental detection impractical.

Conclusion: The X(6900) contribution must be treated at the amplitude level—not just as an incoherent bump—due to pronounced channel-dependent interference effects; J/ψJ/ψγ is the optimal channel for X(6900) spectroscopy in double-charmonium radiative production.

Abstract: We study radiative double-charmonium production, $e^+e^-\to HHγ$ with $H=J/ψ$ and $η_c$, at $\sqrt{s}=10.58~{\rm GeV}$, including a possible tensor contribution from the fully charmed state $X(6900)$. The nonresonant amplitudes are calculated within the color-singlet non-relativistic QCD framework, while the $J^{PC}=2^{++}$ resonance is combined coherently with the continuum. We find that continuum--resonance interference significantly modifies the invariant-mass distributions near $M_{HH}\simeq M_X$. The $J/ψJ/ψγ$ channel exhibits a pronounced phase-dependent resonant structure, with a near-resonance cross section of $0.038$--$0.111~{\rm fb}$. In contrast, the $η_cη_cγ$ channel is continuum dominated and shows only a localized interference effect with an extremely suppressed production rate, rendering its experimental observation extremely challenging. These channel-dependent features arise from the different effective decay structures and demonstrate the importance of an amplitude-level treatment of the $X(6900)$ contribution.

</details>


### [53] [Next-to-leading-order QCD corrections to $S$- and $P$-wave heavy quarkonium decay to $l^{+}l^{-} γ$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23439)
*Zhi-Guo He,Xu-Dong Huang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes QCD next-to-leading order (NLO) radiative Dalitz decays H → l⁺l⁻γ for S-wave (η_c, η_b) and P-wave (χ_{cJ}, χ_{bJ}, J=0,1,2) quarkonia, analyzing total/differential widths with realistic photon energy cuts; it identifies characteristic singularities in the lepton-pair invariant mass spectrum tied to threshold and soft-photon regions, reveals J-dependent peak structures, quantifies sensitivity of χ_{QJ} decay rates to soft-photon cuts (χ_{Q1} > χ_{Q2} > χ_{Q0}), and provides NLO predictions for BESIII tests.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide precise theoretical predictions for radiative Dalitz decays of heavy quarkonia (H → l⁺l⁻γ) at QCD NLO within NRQCD factorization, enabling discrimination among quarkonium multiplets and probing the γ* → l⁺l⁻ form factor—especially under realistic experimental photon energy thresholds.

Method: NRQCD factorization-based QCD next-to-leading order (NLO) calculation of total and differential decay widths for H → l⁺l⁻γ, including S-wave (η_c, η_b) and P-wave (χ_{cJ}, χ_{bJ}, J = 0,1,2) states; systematic implementation of kinematic cuts on photon energy; analysis of lepton-pair invariant mass distribution and regularization of singularities via lepton mass and soft-photon region treatment.

Result: Distinct singularity structures in dΓ/dm_{ll}: S-wave shows one peak near m_{ll} ≈ 2m_l; χ_{Q0}, χ_{Q2} show peaks in both threshold and soft-photon regions; χ_{Q1} only in soft-photon region. Hierarchical sensitivity to soft-photon cut: χ_{Q1} > χ_{Q2} > χ_{Q0}. For χ_{cJ} → e⁺e⁻γ, NLO rate reductions from E_γ^cut = 100 MeV to 500 MeV are 6% (J=0), 66% (J=1), 21% (J=2); for μ⁺μ⁻γ: 17%, 66%, 39% respectively.

Conclusion: The J-dependent spectral features and cut sensitivity provide powerful discriminants among P-wave quarkonium states and serve as sensitive probes of the virtual photon’s electromagnetic form factor; the high-precision NLO predictions are tailored for upcoming BESIII measurements, enhancing validation of perturbative QCD and NRQCD in exclusive quarkonium decays.

Abstract: In this work, we comprehensively study the total and differential decay widths of the radiative Dalitz decays $H \to l^+l^-γ$ up to QCD next-to-leading order (NLO) accuracy within the framework of NRQCD factorization. Our calculation includes the decays of $S$-wave states ($η_c, η_b$) and the $P$-wave triplets ($χ_{cJ}, χ_{bJ}$ for $J=0,1,2$) to both electron ($l=e$) and muon ($l=μ$) final states. To match realistic experimental detection thresholds, systematic kinematic cuts are implemented on the final-state photon energy. Our analysis of the lepton-pair invariant mass distribution shows distinct singular behaviors across the multiplets originating from the lepton-pair threshold region, which is regularized by the lepton mass, and the soft-photon region, respectively. For the $S$-wave states, there is only one peak near the lepton-pair threshold region, while for the $J=0$ and $J=2$ $P$-wave states, the peaks show up in both regions. However, for the $J=1$ $P$-wave states, the peak only appears in the soft-photon region. Such features provide a rich venue to probe the $γ^{\ast}\to l^{+}l^{-}$ form factor in heavy quarkonium decay. Integrating over the bounded phase spaces reveals a distinct hierarchy among the $χ_{QJ}$ states in the sensitivity of our theoretical predictions to the soft-photon energy cuts, ordered as $χ_{Q1} >χ_{Q2}>χ_{Q0}$. In the $χ_{cJ}\to l^{+}l^{-}γ$ cases, as the energy cut increases from 100 $\mathrm{MeV}$, to 500 $\mathrm{MeV}$ the theoretical predictions at QCD NLO are reduced by $6\%(χ_{c0})$,$66\%(χ_{c1})$, and $21\%(χ_{c2})$ for $l=e$, and by $17\%(χ_{c0})$,$66\%(χ_{c1})$, and $39\%(χ_{c2})$ for $l=μ$. Comparing our predictions with the upcoming high-precision experimental tests at BESIII will definitely deepen our understanding of the predictive power of perturbative calculations.

</details>


### [54] [Generalized Chiral $U(1)_{B-L}$: Towards a Less Constrained $Z'$ and Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23521)
*Ranjeet Kumar,Hemant Kumar Prajapati*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a generalized chiral U(1)_X extension of the Standard Model that relaxes LHC Z' dilepton bounds via adjustable anomaly-free charge assignments, accommodates Dirac neutrino masses and scalar dark matter, and resolves relic density–direct detection tension by including scalar-mediated DM annihilation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To relax stringent LHC dilepton resonance constraints on Z' bosons in U(1)_X extensions while simultaneously accommodating Dirac neutrino masses and viable scalar dark matter without ad hoc symmetries.

Method: Constructs a two-parameter family of anomaly-free U(1)_X charge assignments generalizing (B−L); incorporates a Dirac type-I seesaw for neutrino masses; introduces a stable singlet scalar DM candidate; computes Z' branching ratios, DM relic abundance (including Z' and scalar-mediated channels), direct detection cross sections, and confronts with LHC, electroweak precision, and cosmological data.

Result: Dilepton bounds on Z' mass are significantly weakened due to suppressed Z'→ℓ⁺ℓ⁻ branching fractions; viable DM parameter space spans 60 GeV–10 TeV when scalar-mediated annihilation is included; full model satisfies relic density, XENONnT/LZ direct detection limits, LHC dilepton and diboson searches, and electroweak precision tests.

Conclusion: A generalized chiral U(1)_X structure reconciles a less constrained Z' sector, Dirac neutrino masses, and thermally produced scalar dark matter—demonstrating that charge flexibility, rather than minimal (B−L), enables broader phenomenological viability.

Abstract: Extensions of the Standard Model by a $U(1)_{X}$ gauge symmetry predict a new $Z'$ boson, whose mass is severely constrained by high-mass dilepton resonance searches at the LHC. We show that these bounds can be relaxed by generalizing the chiral $(B-L)$ charge assignment while preserving gauge anomaly cancellation. The resulting $U(1)_X$ charges are parameterized by two independent parameters, and an appropriate choice of these parameters suppresses the $Z'$ branching fraction into charged leptons, substantially weakening the dilepton constraints. The framework accommodates Dirac neutrino masses through a Dirac type-I seesaw mechanism and a stable singlet scalar DM candidate without requiring an additional discrete symmetry. We find that the modified charge assignment, although beneficial for collider phenomenology, introduces a tension between obtaining the observed relic abundance and satisfying direct detection limits when DM annihilation proceeds solely through the $Z'$ portal. The inclusion of scalar-mediated annihilation channels resolves this tension and opens up a broad viable parameter space, with DM masses ranging from $M_{\rm DM}\simeq 60~{\rm GeV}$ to $10~{\rm TeV}$. The resulting parameter space is consistent with the observed relic density, direct detection, collider, and electroweak precision constraints, demonstrating that a generalized chiral charge structure can simultaneously accommodate a less constrained $Z'$ sector, neutrino masses, and viable dark matter phenomenology.

</details>


### [55] [Two-loop renormalisation and Higgs phenomenology in the five-dimensional MSSM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23555)
*Ammar Abdalgabar,Alan S. Cornell,Aldo Deandrea,Howeida M. Esmaeil,Mohammed Omer Khojali*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a comprehensive two-loop renormalisation group analysis of the 5D MSSM, confirms perturbative consistency via cancellation of dangerous two-loop UV contributions due to N=2 supersymmetry, computes the radiatively corrected Higgs potential with full KK resummation, and demonstrates that the model remains phenomenologically viable—reproducing the 125 GeV Higgs mass and satisfying electroweak precision and LHC constraints—with a natural (not overly heavy) SUSY spectrum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the 'little hierarchy problem' in the MSSM by leveraging power-law RG running in 5D to generate large stop trilinear couplings and thus accommodate the observed 125 GeV Higgs mass without multi-TeV superpartners; and to rigorously assess the perturbative validity and phenomenological consistency of the 5D MSSM at two-loop order.

Method: Derivation of complete two-loop renormalisation group equations for gauge, Yukawa, and soft SUSY-breaking parameters in the 5D MSSM; analytical demonstration of UV-safe perturbative expansion via cancellation of leading two-loop divergences using N=2 supersymmetry; computation of the Higgs effective potential including full Kaluza-Klein tower via KK resummation; numerical scanning and global fit to Higgs mass, electroweak precision observables (S, T), Higgs coupling modifiers (κb, κτ, κγ, etc.), and LHC signal strengths.

Result: Two-loop RG evolution preserves power-law scaling; dangerous two-loop UV contributions cancel exactly due to N=2 SUSY; full KK-resummed Higgs potential yields m_h ≈ 125 GeV for sub-TeV stops and compactification scales M_c ∼ 10–30 TeV; EWPO and Higgs coupling constraints restrict M_c > ~15 TeV and favour moderate tanβ (~5–10) and light gauginos; viable parameter space remains natural and testable at future colliders.

Conclusion: The 5D MSSM is perturbatively consistent to two loops and phenomenologically viable: it reconciles the measured Higgs mass with a relatively light supersymmetric spectrum, satisfies all current collider and precision constraints, and retains predictive power for KK excitations and SUSY partners.

Abstract: The five-dimensional Minimal Supersymmetric Standard Model (5D MSSM) provides an attractive framework in which power-law renormalisation group evolution naturally generates a sizeable trilinear stop coupling, allowing the observed Higgs boson mass to be reproduced without requiring too heavy a supersymmetric spectra. In this work we present a comprehensive two-loop analysis of the 5D MSSM, deriving the complete renormalisation group equations governing the gauge, Yukawa and soft supersymmetry-breaking sectors and examining the perturbative consistency of the theory. Particular attention is given to the ultraviolet behaviour of the model, where we demonstrate that the potentially dangerous leading two-loop contributions cancel as a consequence of the underlying $\mathcal{N}=2$ supersymmetry, leaving a well-behaved perturbative expansion. Building upon this framework, we compute the radiatively corrected Higgs effective potential including the complete Kaluza-Klein (KK) contributions through KK resummation, and investigate the resulting implications for the Higgs sector. The phenomenological viability of the model is subsequently explored through detailed numerical studies of the Higgs boson mass, electroweak precision observables, Higgs coupling modifiers and signal strengths, allowing constraints on the compactification scale and supersymmetric parameter space to be established. We find that the inclusion of the two-loop corrections preserves the characteristic power-law behaviour of the five-dimensional theory while yielding a phenomenologically viable parameter space consistent with current collider and precision measurements.

</details>

### [56] [String stars, small black holes, and the supersymmetric index](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21511)
*Erez Y. Urbach*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explores the black hole–string transition near the supersymmetric index βΩ = 2πi, where standard high-temperature effective descriptions break down. It identifies distinct near-index regimes for Heterotic and Type II string theories—Higgsed supergravity with magnetic-field-induced W-boson condensation (Heterotic) and light winding strings enabling T-dual dimensional emergence (Type II)—both supporting decoupling string star solutions. Charged versions interpolate between black holes and free strings but fully decouple at the index, challenging smooth transitions and highlighting tension with BPS two-charge black hole existence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the breakdown of the Atick-Witten high-temperature effective description near the supersymmetric index βΩ = 2πi, and to characterize the emergent physics governing the black hole–string transition in that regime.

Method: Analytical study of near-supersymmetric-index limits in Heterotic and Type II string theories; construction and analysis of string star solutions; examination of charged configurations and their thermodynamic/phase behavior; use of dualities (e.g., T-duality) and effective supergravity descriptions (including Higgsing and magnetic backgrounds).

Result: 1) Near βΩ = 2πi, Heterotic theory admits a Higgsed SU(2)_L × U(1)_R supergravity description with magnetic-field-driven Ambjorn-Olesen W-boson condensation (Hagedorn instability); 2) Type II theory exhibits a light winding-string tower, opening a new dimension in the T-dual picture; 3) Both yield decoupling string star solutions analogous to Horowitz-Polchinski but vanishing at the index; 4) Charged string stars interpolate between black holes and free strings, yet all charged phases decouple precisely at βΩ = 2πi.

Conclusion: A smooth black hole–string transition is incompatible with the existence of a stable, stringy BPS two-charge black hole at the supersymmetric index, due to universal decoupling of both black hole and string star phases at βΩ = 2πi.

Abstract: We discuss aspects of the black hole-string transition as the angular velocity approaches the supersymmetric index $βΩ= 2πi$. In this limit, the high-temperature Atick-Witten effective description breaks down. For Heterotic string theory, a new high-temperature description emerges near the index in terms of a Higgsed $SU(2)_L\times U(1)_R$ supergravity with a magnetic field profile. The Hagedorn instability manifests in the theory as Ambjorn-Olesen W boson condensation, due to the background magnetic field. For type II, a tower of winding strings becomes light near the index, which opens up a new dimension in the T-dual picture. In both cases, a string star solution is found, similar to the Horowitz-Polchinski solution, but decouples at the index $βΩ= 2πi$.
  Considering charged solutions, the string star appears to interpolate between charged black holes and the free string phase. At the index, both the charged black hole and the charged string star phases decouple. This suggests a tension between a smooth black hole-string transition and the existence of a stringy BPS two-charge black hole.

</details>


### [57] [What Neural Network Field Theory Can and Cannot Realise on a Computer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21523)
*Thomas R. Harvey*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves a no-go theorem showing that standard neural network ensembles cannot consistently realize quantum field theories (QFTs) due to failure of reflection positivity; effective field theory (EFT) interpretations also lack scale separation, and only smeared correlators are numerically controllable in the infinite-width limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the feasibility of implementing quantum or effective field theories via neural network ensembles—treating the network ensemble itself as the field theory—and determine under which conditions such a 'neural network field theory' is mathematically and computationally viable.

Method: Rigorous analysis of reflection positivity, scale separation, and correlator computability across four variants of neural network field theory (finite/infinite width × QFT/EFT); employs functional analysis and statistical mechanics tools to examine consistency conditions for field-theoretic interpretations.

Result: A no-go theorem: finite-width ensembles violate reflection positivity (disqualifying QFT interpretation) and lack scale separation (undermining EFT validity); infinite-width limits permit full simulation only for one variant, while another yields only controllably approximated smeared correlators; 1D is exceptional but still fails reflection positivity at finite width for cosine networks.

Conclusion: Standard neural network architectures cannot serve as consistent, numerically controlled realizations of QFTs or EFTs unless either pointwise finite variance or exact rotation invariance is relaxed—suggesting fundamental limitations for neural network field theory as currently formulated.

Abstract: One aim of neural network field theory is to put a quantum or effective field theory on a computer, with the network ensemble itself as the theory. We ask how far that aim can be pushed for a function class regular enough to be computed with. Our main result is a no-go theorem with assumptions that hold for standard network architectures. We use it to separate four versions of neural network field theory, according to whether the defining object is the finite width ensemble or its infinite width limit, and whether the target we want to compute is a quantum or an effective field theory. Neither finite width interpretation is straightforwardly consistent. For finite width ensembles with finite variance at each point, the QFT interpretation fails reflection positivity, while the EFT interpretation establishes no scale separation by which the positivity violation can be placed outside its domain of validity. Of the two limit versions, one can be simulated in full and the other only in part, as only its smeared correlators are computable with a controlled error. As such, at the level of a controlled numerical computation, the QFT and EFT versions cannot be distinguished. One dimension escapes the obstruction, yet reflection positivity is shown to still fail there at every finite width for the cosine network. Two escapes from the theorem remain, giving up either finite variance at a point or exact rotation invariance, and we discuss both of these possibilities.

</details>


### [58] [On flat space limits of $AdS_4$ black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21518)
*Pedro Vicente Marto,Shradha Ramakrishnan,Stefan Vandoren*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the flat space limit of asymptotically AdS₄ black holes in N=2 gauged supergravity to probe flat space holography; it analyzes the on-shell action via holographic renormalization, identifies divergences linked to thermodynamic volume, matches small black hole partition functions to flat-space counterparts, maps BPS/extremal/non-extremal solutions across limits, and derives a charged BPS flat-space black hole from M-theory, whose entropy is reproduced by a Carrollian-like limit of the ABJM partition function on S¹×S².


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand flat space holography by systematically taking the flat space limit of asymptotically AdS₄ black holes in N=2 gauged supergravity, bridging bulk gravitational physics and boundary field theory descriptions.

Method: Holographic renormalization of the on-shell gravitational action; careful analysis of the flat space (Λ→0) limit for various black hole families (large/small, BPS, extremal, rotating, charged); mapping between bulk solutions and dual field theory states; derivation of a flat-space BPS solution from M-theory; Carrollian-like scaling limit of the ABJM partition function on S¹×S² at large N.

Result: Large black holes induce a thermodynamic volume divergence in the partition function; small black holes semiclassically reproduce flat-space partition functions; explicit mappings established between BPS/extremal/non-extremal AdS and flat black holes; a new charged BPS flat-space black hole is obtained; its entropy is precisely matched by a Carrollian limit of the large-N ABJM partition function.

Conclusion: The flat space limit of AdS₄ supergravity black holes provides a controlled framework for flat space holography, with consistent thermodynamic and partition function behavior across scales and supersymmetry classes, and the ABJM theory admits a Carrollian limit encoding flat-space BPS black hole entropy.

Abstract: With a view towards understanding flat space holography, we study the flat space limit of asymptotically $AdS_4$ black holes in gauged $\mathcal{N}=2$ supergravity. Using holographic renormalisation, we carefully analyse the flat space limit of the on-shell action of various black holes in this setting and make explicit the states that contribute to the partition function in this limit. We find several features: large black holes lead to a divergence in the partition function which can be understood as a thermodynamic volume divergence. Meanwhile, semiclassically, small black holes reproduce the partition function of their flat space counterparts, and we clarify the mapping between BPS, (near-)extremal or non-extremal black holes on both sides for charged and rotating solutions. Finally, by studying a suitable flat limit of the BPS Kerr-Newman-$AdS_4$ black hole in M-theory, we obtain a charged BPS solution in flat space. On the dual side, we determine a Carrollian-like limit on the known ABJM partition function on $S^1\times S^2$ at large $N$, and show that it reproduces the correct entropy of the BPS charged black hole in asymptotically flat space.

</details>


### [59] [Neural Network Field Theory at Finite Width](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21588)
*Christian Ferko,Aaron Mutchler*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper shows that while quantum theories can be represented by neural network ensembles with countably infinite random parameters, finite-parameter neural networks (e.g., finite-width feedforward nets) generically fail to satisfy key axiomatic properties of Euclidean QFTs—specifically reflection positivity or cluster decomposition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the representational limits and physical fidelity of neural network parameterizations of quantum theories, especially whether finite-capacity models can preserve foundational axiomatic features of QM/QFT.

Method: Analytical derivation and structural comparison between neural network representations (NN-QM/NN-QFT) and axiomatic Euclidean QFT frameworks; examination of reflection positivity, cluster decomposition, and other defining properties under finite-parameter constraints.

Result: Finite-parameter NN-QM and NN-QFT models generically violate at least one core Euclidean QFT axiom—namely reflection positivity or cluster decomposition—under mild assumptions.

Conclusion: Neural networks with countably infinite random parameters can faithfully represent QM/QFT, but finite-parameter approximations inevitably break essential physical consistency conditions, revealing a fundamental trade-off between model capacity and axiomatic compliance.

Abstract: Under mild assumptions, any quantum mechanical (QM) model or quantum field theory (QFT) admits a representation in terms of an ensemble of neural networks with countably many random parameters. We investigate the features of NN-QM and NN-FT models with finitely many parameters, such as a feedforward network of width $N < \infty$. We find that, generically, such models must violate one of the properties of conventional Euclidean QFTs, such as reflection positivity or cluster decomposition. We present several complementary ways of understanding which features can and cannot be preserved at finite $N$, both in QM and in QFT.

</details>


### [60] [One-Loop Quantum Correction to the Kink Mass in a Lorentz-Violating Scalar Field Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21613)
*Reza Moazzemi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the one-loop radiative correction to the mass of a (1+1)D λφ⁴ kink in the presence of a specific Lorentz-violating interaction, requiring both mass and field-strength renormalization; it finds a finite, numerically negative Lorentz-violating contribution to the kink mass.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how irreducible Lorentz violation affects topological soliton properties—specifically the kink mass—in a controlled, renormalizable 2D scalar field theory, beyond what can be absorbed by coordinate or field redefinitions.

Method: One-loop quantum field theoretic calculation: (i) introduces the Lorentz-violating operator −½cφ²(u·∂φ)² with spacelike u^μ=(0,1); (ii) performs full one-loop renormalization, identifying necessary mass and field-strength counterterms; (iii) solves the modified fluctuation (stability) problem around the kink background, analyzing bound/continuum modes and phase shift; (iv) computes vacuum-subtracted zero-point energy and adds counterterm Hamiltonian contribution to obtain the corrected kink mass to O(c).

Result: The Lorentz-violating theory requires a nonzero field-strength counterterm—absent in the Lorentz-invariant case—and yields modified fluctuation spectrum (bound and continuum modes), though the O(c) continuum phase shift remains unchanged under the adopted boundary-condition prescription; the resulting one-loop kink mass correction contains a finite, numerically negative O(c) term.

Conclusion: Irreducible Lorentz violation induces qualitatively new renormalization structure and quantitatively shifts the kink mass at one loop; the negative sign of the O(c) contribution suggests Lorentz violation tends to reduce topological soliton mass in this model.

Abstract: We study the radiative correction to the mass of the $(1+1)$-dimensional $λφ^4$ kink in the presence of an irreducible, spacelike Lorentz-violating interaction. The Lorentz-violating operator is chosen as $-\frac12 cφ^2(u\!\cdot\!\partialφ)^2$ with $u^μ=(0,1)$, which is the lowest-order single-scalar operator considered in this class of models that produces effects that cannot be removed by a linear redefinition of coordinates or the field. We perform the one-loop renormalization of the underlying quantum field theory. In contrast with Lorentz-invariant $λφ^4$ theory in two dimensions, the Lorentz-violating theory requires, at this order, both a modified mass counterterm and a nonzero field-strength counterterm. The corrected stability problem contains modified bound and continuum modes, while the continuum phase shift remains unchanged at $O(c)$ with the boundary-condition prescription adopted here. Finally, using the vacuum-subtracted zero-point energy and the counterterm Hamiltonian, we obtain the one-loop kink mass through first order in $c$. We give the finite coefficient in the subtraction prescription implemented in the accompanying thesis calculation, for which the Lorentz-violating contribution is numerically negative.

</details>


### [61] [Heterotic string couplings at order $α'^3$ in NS-NS sector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21798)
*Alireza Pahlavan,Mehdi Ameri,Mohammad R. Garousi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the eight-derivative effective action of heterotic string theory in the NS-NS sector using T-duality constraints, dimensional reduction, and field redefinitions; it fixes couplings relative to type II and reproduces known S-matrix results in the Meissner scheme, unifying gravity and Yang-Mills terms as (Tr(R²−F²))².


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the complete, T-duality-invariant eight-derivative effective action for heterotic string theory in the NS-NS sector — a long-standing problem requiring consistency with both Buscher duality and known scattering amplitudes.

Method: Standard T-duality procedure applied to heterotic string theory: (1) Start from minimal basis of 872 even- + 477 odd-parity eight-derivative couplings in D dimensions; (2) Dimensionally reduce on S¹; (3) Impose invariance under Buscher rules extended to higher derivatives; (4) Fix couplings by matching to type II theory and α′-coefficients; (5) Apply field redefinitions to achieve canonical dilaton factor e⁻²Φ; (6) Compare results in Metsaev-Tseytlin and Meissner schemes.

Result: T-duality uniquely fixes part of the couplings to match type II up to overall factor; remaining couplings expressed in terms of α′-coefficients. In Meissner scheme, pure gravity sector matches S-matrix predictions. All pure gravity eight-derivative terms are (Tr(R²))²; combined with Yang-Mills (Tr(F²))², they unify into (Tr(R²−F²))².

Conclusion: The eight-derivative NS-NS effective action of heterotic string theory is fully constrained by T-duality and field redefinition freedom; its structure confirms the anticipated unification of curvature and gauge-field squared invariants, validating the (Tr(R²−F²))² form and establishing consistency between worldsheet, S-matrix, and duality-based approaches.

Abstract: We utilize the standard T-duality procedure to derive the classical effective action of heterotic string theory at the eight-derivative order within the NS-NS sector, which comprises the metric, the \(B\)-field, and the dilaton. Starting from the minimal basis at this order, consisting of 872 even-parity and 477 odd-parity couplings, we perform a dimensional reduction on a circle and impose invariance under T-duality transformations, specifically the Buscher rules supplemented by higher-derivative corrections. This invariance uniquely fixes a subset of the couplings to match those of type II theory up to an overall factor, while the remaining couplings are determined in terms of the coefficients at order \(α'\). For these latter couplings, we adopt both the Metsaev-Tseytlin and Meissner schemes. Subsequently, through field redefinitions, we recast the action into a canonical form in which the dilaton appears solely through the overall factor \(e^{-2Φ}\). In the Meissner scheme, the pure gravity sector precisely reproduces the known S-matrix results. In both schemes, the pure gravity terms can be expressed as the double trace \((\Tr(R^2))^2\), and when combined with the corresponding Yang-Mills terms \((\Tr(F^2))^2\), they take the unified form \((\Tr(R^2-F^2))^2\), as anticipated in the literature.

</details>


### [62] [Characteristic-cone running from on-shell residues: Identifiability on the physical EFT quotient](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21865)
*Renat Gafarov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a data-completeness test for extracting low-energy characteristic-cone running from flat-space on-shell renormalization-group residues in 4D parity-even Einstein–scalar–Maxwell EFT up to four derivatives and four fields; it computes specific beta-function coefficients for vector–scalar residues, identifies incompleteness via operator-direction-dependent response splittings (e.g., $F^4$ and $\mathcal{C}FF$), and shows that the scalar–photon contact residue fixes only one diagonal cone contribution—not the full characteristic response.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether on-shell renormalization-group residues fully determine the low-energy characteristic-cone evolution in gravitational–electromagnetic–scalar effective field theory, i.e., whether amplitude data is 'complete' for reconstructing physical RG flow in the massless root sector.

Method: Formulates a data-completeness test using on-shell renormalization-group residues; places Wilson directions on the physical EFT quotient; models background propagation via traceless response on the massless root sector; identifies response from amplitude coordinates via kernel membership; computes residues using amplitude kernels and frame translations; applies Schur reduction and Maxwell Ward identity for contact-term analysis; uses Petrov classification and magnetic/Weyl background probes for operator-direction incompleteness tests.

Result: Computes $\\beta_\\mu C_{\\gamma\\phi}^{(2)} = -55/(96\\pi^2)$ and $\\beta_\\mu(r_T - r_\\gamma) = -55/(48\\pi^2 M_{\\rm P}^4)$; finds finite operator registry reveals rank-two incompleteness—$F^4$ splits photon polarizations on magnetic backgrounds, $\\mathcal{C}FF$ induces analytic splitting on Petrov-I Einstein–scalar backgrounds; scalar–photon contact yields nonzero raw mixing but vanishes off-diagonally at first order due to Maxwell Ward identity.

Conclusion: The opposite-helicity vector–scalar residue fixes a single diagonal contribution to the characteristic cone but fails to determine the term-complete characteristic response over the admissible coefficient domain—demonstrating intrinsic data incompleteness in the on-shell RG residue framework.

Abstract: We formulate a data-completeness test for extracting low-energy characteristic-cone running from flat-space on-shell renormalization-group residues. In four-dimensional, parity-even Einstein--scalar--Maxwell effective field theory through four derivatives and four external fields, Wilson directions are first placed on the physical EFT quotient and background propagation is represented by a traceless response on the full massless root sector. The response is identifiable from chosen amplitude coordinates precisely when the amplitude kernel lies in the response kernel. For the published opposite-helicity vector--scalar residue we find $β_μC_{γφ}^{(2)}=-55/(96π^2)$ and, after an order-reduced frame translation, the contribution $β_μ(r_T-r_γ)=-55/(48π^2M_{\rm P}^4)$. A finite operator registry gives explicit rank-two incompleteness tests: independent $F^4$ directions split photon polarizations on a magnetic background, while $\mathcal CFF$ produces an analytic splitting along a principal Weyl axis on a Petrov-I Einstein--scalar background. For the isolated scalar--photon contact, direct Schur reduction finds nonzero raw mixing but no first-order physical off-diagonal entry, by the Maxwell Ward identity. Thus the residue fixes one diagonal cone contribution but does not determine the term-complete characteristic response over the declared admissible coefficient domain.

</details>


### [63] [Perturbative Reconstruction of Self-Adjoint Generators from Bosonic Canonical Commutation Relations: Application to the Null-Surface Formulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21947)
*C. N. Kozameh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves a constructive integrability theorem for reconstructing a self-adjoint generator—order-by-order—from a perturbative bosonic outgoing map preserving canonical commutation relations (CCRs); it applies the result to graviton scattering in the null-surface formulation, yielding a formal perturbative unitary implementation and canonical decomposition of scattering amplitudes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous, constructive link between perturbative S-maps preserving CCRs and self-adjoint generators implementing them unitarily via adjoint conjugation—particularly in quantum gravity contexts where unitarity and algebraic consistency are subtle.

Method: Working within the polynomial bosonic CCR algebra, the authors use recursive reconstruction: at each perturbative order, they subtract Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff contributions from previously determined generators and solve the remaining homogeneous CCR conditions explicitly for the next generator. Applied to NSF graviton scattering with Minkowski background, they impose CCR preservation up to O(ε²) (i.e., δa₃) and fix operator ordering via scalar CCR constraints.

Result: A constructive proof that CCR preservation suffices to uniquely reconstruct formal self-adjoint generators δT₁ and δT₂; identification of symmetric ordering (c = 1) as uniquely selected by scalar CCRs; cancellation of double-contraction terms via linear contraction from the same cubic kernel; and canonical separation of the 2→2 amplitude into irreducible-loop, 1PR, and factorized sectors.

Conclusion: Order-by-order CCR preservation is not only necessary but sufficient for formal unitary implementability of perturbative bosonic outgoing maps; this yields a well-defined, algebraically consistent framework for perturbative quantum gravity scattering, exemplified in the null-surface formulation.

Abstract: We study the inverse adjoint problem for a perturbative bosonic outgoing map. Within the polynomial bosonic canonical-commutation-relation (CCR) algebra, we prove a constructive integrability theorem: order-by-order preservation of the CCRs is sufficient to reconstruct recursively a formal self-adjoint generator whose exponential implements the outgoing map by adjoint conjugation. At each order, the Baker--Campbell--Hausdorff contribution determined by previously reconstructed generators is subtracted from the outgoing coefficient, and the remaining homogeneous CCR conditions determine the next generator explicitly.
  We apply the construction to graviton scattering in the null-surface formulation (NSF), specialized to the flat solution-space sector with Minkowski metric, null generators, affine parameter, and cone measure. The NSF outgoing map preserves the Ashtekar radiative CCRs through (δa_3), corresponding to order (\varepsilon^2). At this order, the scalar CCR sector uniquely selects the symmetric ordering (c=1) for the nontrivial interacting kernel. The associated linear contraction, generated by the same cubic kernel, cancels the double-contraction contribution from ([δa_2,δa_2^\dagger]). The theorem then reconstructs the self-adjoint generators (δT_1) and (δT_2), providing a formal perturbative unitary implementation. The resulting canonical organization also separates the direct (2\to2) contribution into irreducible-loop, one-particle-reducible, and factorized sectors.

</details>


### [64] [Holographic SU(3) color superconductivity at finite baryon chemical potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22013)
*Xin Zhao,Zhang-Yu Nie,Ya-Peng Hu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a holographic SU(3) color superconductivity model featuring spontaneous global color symmetry breaking via a diquark condensate at finite baryon chemical potential μ_b; it identifies an S1+iS2 order parameter, shows μ_b enhances its T_c while suppressing p-wave, and predicts a dominant balanced S+ phase (S1=S2) at high μ_b — offering a non-Abelian holographic framework for dense QCD and neutron-star physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a concrete holographic description of global SU(3) color symmetry breaking relevant for color-superconducting phases in dense QCD—particularly in neutron-star interiors—where conventional perturbative methods fail and non-Abelian dynamics dominate.

Method: Constructs a bottom-up holographic model with a bulk scalar field dual to a diquark operator, implements finite baryon chemical potential μ_b via a background gauge field, performs systematic linear stability analysis over all SU(3) diquark channels, incorporates gravitational backreaction to assess phase competition, and computes critical temperatures and phase boundaries.

Result: At μ_b = 0, the model recovers the known SU(2) s+p competition; at finite μ_b, the s-wave order generalizes to a complex S1+iS2 structure; μ_b increases the critical temperature of the S1+iS2 phase while suppressing the p-wave phase (which vanishes beyond a critical μ_b when backreaction is included); the balanced S+ phase (S1 = S2) dominates the entire color-superconducting region at sufficiently large μ_b.

Conclusion: The holographic SU(3) CSC model demonstrates that finite baryon density fundamentally reshapes the symmetry-breaking pattern and phase structure—favoring a globally symmetric, complex diquark condensate—thereby providing a non-perturbative, geometrically realized framework for studying color superconductivity in ultra-dense matter.

Abstract: We present a holographic model of SU(3) color superconductivity (CSC) where a global color symmetry is spontaneously broken by a diquark condensate at finite baryon chemical potential. At $μ_b=0$, a systematic stability analysis over all SU(3) channels recovers the known SU(2) s+p competition. Based on that, the finite $μ_b$ further generalizes the s-wave order to an S1+iS2 structure. We find that $μ_b$ enhances the critical temperature of the S1+iS2 phase while suppressing the p-wave -- the latter vanishes beyond a certain $μ_b$ when backreaction is included. This opposite trend reshapes the phase diagram: with increasing $μ_b$, the balanced S+ phase (S1=S2) takes over the entire color-superconducting region. Our non-Abelian framework provides a concrete holographic description of global SU(3) symmetry breaking, relevant for understanding color-superconducting phases in dense QCD and may offer new insights into the physics of neutron-star interiors.

</details>


### [65] [Thermal propagator of the bosons and fermions fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22469)
*M. A. A. Ahmed,H. Zainuddin,N. M. Shah*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs scalar and fermion propagators in thermal field theory using a real-time formalism with an arbitrary parameter σ, enabling analysis of particle propagation in momentum and mixed space at finite temperature and zero chemical potential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To distinguish thermal field theory from classical statistical mechanics by rigorously examining quantum propagators—specifically scalar and fermion propagators—in a finite-temperature, zero-chemical-potential setting using real-time formalism.

Method: Introduces an arbitrary parameter σ to formulate path integrals in the real-time formalism (RTF); constructs scalar and fermion propagators explicitly for finite-temperature systems without chemical potential; analyzes propagation in both momentum space and mixed (coordinate–momentum) space.

Result: Explicit expressions for scalar and fermion propagators in RTF at finite temperature (μ = 0), revealing how quantum particles propagate between spacetime points in both momentum and mixed representations.

Conclusion: The σ-parameterized RTF framework successfully generalizes propagator structure beyond classical statistical analogs, confirming TFT’s inherently quantum, time-dependent nature even in equilibrium thermodynamic settings.

Abstract: In a thermodynamic environment, thermal field theory (TFT) describes a large ensemble of interacting particles. This may appear to be the same as in classical statistical mechanics. Therefore, we study the scalar propagator and fermion propagator by considering an arbitrary parameter $σ$ that allows for a path integral description in real-time formalism (RTF). We constructed those propagators which allowed us to know how particles moved from one point to another point in momentum space as well as in mixed space, at finite temperature without a chemical potential.

</details>


### [66] [A second rotational Killing field on gauged D=5 vector multiplet horizons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22509)
*Usman Kayani*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper classifies supersymmetric near-horizon geometries in 5D gauged supergravity with vector multiplets, under the assumption that the canonical rotational Killing vector on the horizon section is non-vanishing. It proves existence of a second rotational Killing field on compact horizons, distinguishes two moduli sub-branches (constant vs. varying), recovers known geometries as consequences of supersymmetry+compactness, constructs explicit solutions, and excludes hidden Killing-tensor symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify supersymmetric near-horizon geometries in D=5 gauged supergravity with vector multiplets—beyond minimal supergravity—without assuming global rotational symmetry, and to understand the role of moduli dynamics and isometry enhancement on compact horizons.

Method: Analysis of the first-order Killing spinor equations and near-horizon geometry constraints; use of $SU(2)$-structure formalism; study of critical points of $\|η_-\|^2$; investigation of closure conditions ($dP = 0$) and integrability of associated flat rank-two systems; explicit construction and numerical verification of horizon metrics.

Result: 1) A second rotational Killing field always exists on the open set where $Z$ and $W$ are linearly independent and extends globally under mild conditions. 2) Two disjoint sub-branches emerge: $P \equiv 0$ (constant moduli, homogeneous horizons, recovering Kunduri-Lucietti) and $P \not\equiv 0$ (varying moduli, solved explicitly via $U = \|η_-\|^2(h_Y N - h_N Y)$). 3) $K = Q_{IJ} C^I C^J$ is either identically zero or strictly positive; hidden Killing tensors are excluded. 4) Both sub-branches are realized by known and newly constructed compact horizons, including a non-static Kunduri-Lucietti example verifying the extension hypothesis numerically.

Conclusion: Supersymmetry and compactness alone enforce enhanced isometries in 5D gauged supergravity near-horizons—even without ansatz—leading to a clean dichotomy between constant and dynamical moduli sectors. The analysis confirms structural rigidity of such geometries and refines the classification beyond minimal theory.

Abstract: We study supersymmetric near-horizon geometries of gauged D=5 supergravity coupled to vector multiplets, on the branch where the canonical rotational Killing vector $\tilde V$ of the horizon section $\mathcal{S}$ is non-vanishing. No rotational symmetry is assumed. On a compact, connected $\mathcal{S}$ without boundary, a second rotational Killing field, independent of $\tilde V$, always exists on the open set where $Z$ and $W$ are linearly independent, and extends to an isometry of all of $\mathcal{S}$, unconditionally if the moduli are constant and under a checkable extension hypothesis otherwise.
  With $K=Q_{IJ}C^IC^J$ vanishing exactly in the minimal theory, $K\equiv0$ recovers the known result of Grover, Gutowski, Papadopoulos and Sabra. When $K>0$, $P=Z+W$ vanishes at every critical point of $\|η_-\|^2$, and two sub-branches occur, both realised. On the first, $P\equiv0$, the moduli are constant and the isometry follows by homogeneity, recovering Kunduri and Lucietti local geometries as a consequence of supersymmetry and compactness rather than an ansatz. On the second, $P\not\equiv0$, the moduli genuinely vary; closure of the first-order system ($dP=0$) turns the Killing equation for a vector orthogonal to $P$ into a flat rank-two system transported along $P$, solved explicitly by $U=\|η_-\|^2(h_YN-h_NY)$.
  Both sub-branches are non-empty on compact $\mathcal{S}$: known $U(1)^3$ black holes populate the first, and an explicit compact horizon from the non-static Kunduri-Lucietti family, with $P$ vanishing at two points, populates the second and verifies the extension hypothesis numerically. We also prove $K$ is identically zero or everywhere positive, exclude a hidden Killing-tensor symmetry, and leave the $S^1\times S^2$ black-ring window $\tfrac13Φ^2<χ^2K\leq\tfrac43Φ^2$ unexcluded on either sub-branch.

</details>


### [67] [Genus-Two Contributions to Late-Time Correlators in JT Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22609)
*Masayoshi Sato*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the genus-two contribution to the late-time two-point correlation function in JT gravity by modeling two baby universes via a Hartle–Hawking state amplitude, reducing it to a Weil–Petersson moduli integral, decomposing $V_{0,3}$ using Kontsevich graphs, enforcing the no-shortcut condition on genus-two strip geometries, and evaluating explicit strip contributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend beyond the single-baby-universe (genus-one) analysis and systematically compute higher-genus (specifically genus-two) contributions to late-time correlators in JT gravity, which encode multi-wormhole physics and quantum gravitational effects in low-dimensional holography.

Method: Constructs a Hartle–Hawking amplitude for emission/absorption of two baby universes; reduces the genus-two correlator to a moduli-space integral over $\mathcal{M}_{0,3}$ weighted by the Weil–Petersson volume $V_{0,3}$; applies Kontsevich graph decomposition of $V_{0,3}$; imposes the no-shortcut condition on genus-two strip geometries; uses the strip approximation to convert the integral into a finite sum over distinct geometric configurations; evaluates explicit strip-geometry contributions analytically.

Result: Explicit analytic evaluation of the genus-two late-time two-point correlation function arising from two baby universes; demonstration that the strip approximation + no-shortcut condition yields a tractable, finite-sum representation for multi-wormhole amplitudes in JT gravity.

Conclusion: The combined framework of strip approximation and no-shortcut condition provides a robust and computationally effective analytic tool for studying higher-genus, multi-wormhole geometries in Jackiw–Teitelboim gravity, paving the way for systematic exploration of quantum gravitational correlations beyond genus one.

Abstract: In this work, we analyze the late-time two-point correlation function in Jackiw--Teitelboim gravity, focusing on the genus-two ($g=2$) contribution associated with two baby universes. Extending the analysis of the single-baby-universe ($g=1$) case, we formulate an amplitude describing the emission and absorption of two baby universes during the evolution of the Hartle--Hawking state. This formulation reduces the problem to a moduli-space integral involving the Weil--Petersson volume $V_{0,3}$. To evaluate this integral, we decompose $V_{0,3}$ into contributions from Kontsevich graphs and extend the no-shortcut condition to the relevant genus-two strip geometries. Combined with the strip approximation, this decomposition reorganizes the moduli-space integral into a finite sum of contributions associated with distinct geometric configurations. We then explicitly evaluate the strip-geometry contributions corresponding to the emission of two baby universes and determine their contribution to the genus-two late-time two-point correlation function. Our results suggest that the strip approximation, together with the no-shortcut condition, provides a useful analytic framework for studying higher-genus geometries containing multiple wormholes.

</details>


### [68] [Higher-spin charges and the $L_Λ w_{1 + \infty}$ algebra in (A)dS$_4$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22625)
*Lorenzo Di Giacomo,Maitá Micol,Daniele Pranzetti,Ana-Maria Raclariu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs an infinite tower of higher-spin charges in (3+1)-dimensional asymptotically locally (A)dS spacetimes, showing they realize a Λ-deformed w_{1+∞} algebra; it also defines curved-space conformally soft gravitons as boundary primaries and reproduces the proposed Λ-deformed celestial OPE, thereby extending the celestial holography dictionary to (A)dS₄.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the celestial holography framework—previously established for asymptotically flat gravity—to asymptotically (A)dS₄ spacetimes, and to uncover the bulk gravitational origin of the recently proposed Λ-deformed w_{1+∞} symmetry and celestial operator product expansion.

Method: Perturbative asymptotic expansion of the Einstein equations in the cosmological constant Λ; construction of higher-spin charges as solutions to a hierarchy of evolution equations; canonical analysis on a restricted gravitational phase space; definition of conformally soft gravitons via spacetime data and verification of their primary properties under sl(2,R) ⊂ isometry algebra; computation of Poisson brackets with quadratic higher-spin charges.

Result: 1) An infinite tower of conserved higher-spin charges realizing the Λ-deformed w_{1+∞} algebra (L_Λ w_{1+∞}) canonically on a restricted phase space; 2) Curved-space conformally soft gravitons identified as sl(2,R) primaries; 3) Their Poisson brackets with quadratic charges reproduce the Taylor–Zhu Λ-deformed celestial OPE; 4) Full bulk gravitational realization of L_Λ w_{1+∞} and extension of the celestial bulk-boundary dictionary to (A)dS₄.

Conclusion: The L_Λ w_{1+∞} symmetry has a concrete bulk gravitational origin in asymptotically (A)dS₄ gravity, and the celestial holography program—including soft theorems, symmetry algebras, and OPEs—admits a natural, nontrivial generalization to nonzero cosmological constant.

Abstract: We consider $(3+1)$-dimensional asymptotically locally (anti-)de Sitter ((A)dS$_4$) spacetimes with boundary conditions allowing for gravitational flux. We construct an infinite tower of higher-spin charges as perturbative solutions in the cosmological constant $Λ$ to a hierarchy of evolution equations resulting from an asymptotic expansion of the Einstein equations. We show that these charges canonically realize the $Λ$-deformed $w_{1+\infty}$ algebra ($L_Λ w_{1+\infty}$) on a restricted gravitational phase space, thereby extending the higher-spin symmetry structure of asymptotically flat spacetimes to asymptotically (A)dS spacetimes. We further construct the curved-space counterparts of conformally soft gravitons directly in terms of spacetime data. We show that they are primaries with respect to an $\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{R})$ subalgebra of the (A)dS$_4$ isometry algebra and that their Poisson brackets with the quadratic higher-spin charges reproduce the $Λ$-deformed celestial operator product expansion proposed by Taylor and Zhu. Our results establish the bulk gravitational origin of the $L_Λw_{1+\infty}$ symmetry and extend the celestial bulk-boundary dictionary to asymptotically (A)dS$_4$ gravity.

</details>


### [69] [Towards Perturbative Unimodular Poincaré Gauge Theories with Propagating Torsion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22669)
*Arthur F. Vieira,Antonio D. Pereira,Reinhard Alkofer*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the one-loop equivalence between Poincaré gauge gravity with propagating torsion and its unimodular counterpart, showing identical logarithmic divergences and physical determinants on various symmetric and flat torsionful backgrounds—indicating no modification of local torsion dynamics by the unimodular constraint under these conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether imposing the unimodular constraint alters quantum (one-loop) torsion dynamics in Poincaré gauge theories of gravity, especially given potential differences in diffeomorphism invariance and background dependence.

Method: Computes and compares one-loop effective actions and functional determinants in both diffeomorphism-invariant and genuine unimodular formulations, focusing on two quadratic torsion sectors (axial and vector components of hook-antisymmetric torsion) over maximally symmetric metric backgrounds (with vanishing torsion) and flat backgrounds with homogeneous axial/vector torsion; analyzes metric-torsion mixing and determinant structure.

Result: Identical one-loop determinants and local logarithmic divergences in both formulations across all considered backgrounds; torsion-dependent effective actions governed by the same physical determinant in unimodular gauge and genuine unimodular theory; equivalence holds only for restricted backgrounds, breaking down generically on curved torsionful backgrounds.

Conclusion: The unimodular constraint does not affect local one-loop torsion dynamics for the studied actions and backgrounds, but the equivalence is not universal—it fails for generic curved, torsionful spacetimes, necessitating extensions beyond current setup.

Abstract: We study the one-loop relation between an effective-field-theory extension of Poincaré gauge theories of gravity with propagating torsion and its unimodular counterpart. In the Einstein representation, we consider two representative quadratic torsion subsectors, namely totally antisymmetric (axial) torsion and the vector component of hook-antisymmetric torsion. On maximally symmetric metric backgrounds with vanishing background torsion, the diffeomorphism-invariant and genuine unimodular theories yield identical one-loop determinants in both sectors, reproducing the same local logarithmic divergences. We further examine flat backgrounds with homogeneous axial or vector torsion, where metric-torsion mixing is present. In each case, the torsion-dependent one-loop effective action in unimodular gauge and in the genuine unimodular formulation is controlled by the same physical determinant. Thus, for the actions and backgrounds considered, the unimodular constraint does not modify local one-loop torsion dynamics. We also delineate the limitations of this equivalence and the extensions needed for generic torsionful curved backgrounds.

</details>


### [70] [Renormalization, cutoff, and gluing for a quartic model with boundary](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23069)
*A. V. Ivanov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies renormalization of a 3D quartic model on a compact Riemannian manifold with boundary, showing that boundary singular contributions necessitate extending the classical action and renormalizing a boundary operator.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum field theory renormalization must be modified when the spacetime manifold has a boundary, particularly whether standard mass renormalization suffices or if new boundary-localized counterterms are required.

Method: Perturbative renormalization analysis of a scalar quartic (φ⁴) model on a compact 3D Riemannian manifold with boundary, incorporating geometric and boundary operator constraints, especially consistency under manifold gluing.

Result: Boundary contributions with singular coefficients arise beyond mass shift; consistency under gluing forces renormalization of a boundary operator, implying extension of the classical action.

Conclusion: Standard renormalization is insufficient for manifolds with boundary; boundary-localized counterterms and operator renormalization are mandatory, demanding a generalized renormalization framework.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the renormalization of a three-dimensional quartic model on a compact connected Riemannian manifold with a boundary. We show that, in addition to the standard shift of the mass parameter, it is necessary to take into account boundary contributions involving singular coefficients. This indicates the need to extend the classical action within the framework of standard renormalization theory. The requirements of consistency regarding the gluing of manifolds lead to renormalization of an operator on the boundary.

</details>


### [71] [Bouncing shellworld embedded in charged AdS spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22855)
*Karma P. Sherpa,Rishi Pokhrel,Indra K. P. Chettri,Tanay K. Dey*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies cosmological evolution of a spherical brane (shellworld) in a 5D charged anti-de Sitter (AdS) bulk, showing that charge-induced nonsingular bounces occur—cyclic for small brane cosmological constant, eternal expansion for large values—and resolve Cauchy horizon instability; scalar perturbations remain regular across the bounce, and inclusion of bulk long strings preserves these features.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve singularity and Cauchy horizon instability issues in standard braneworld cosmologies by exploring nonsingular bouncing dynamics in a charged AdS bulk with a spherical brane (shellworld).

Method: Analytical investigation of Friedmann-like equations for a spherical brane embedded in a five-dimensional charged AdS spacetime; analysis of bounce conditions, horizon structure, linear scalar perturbations across the bounce, and extension to include uniformly distributed long strings in the bulk.

Result: Nonsingular bouncing cosmology emerges due to bulk charge; cyclic behavior for small brane Λ, eternal expansion for large Λ; bounce can occur outside bulk horizons, eliminating Cauchy horizon instability; scalar perturbations are regular and non-divergent; bounce persists even with bulk long strings.

Conclusion: The charged AdS shellworld scenario provides a robust, singularity-free, and horizon-stable alternative to standard braneworld cosmologies, with resilience against perturbations and additional bulk matter (long strings).

Abstract: We investigate the cosmological evolution of a spherical brane within the shellworld (dark bubble) scenario embedded in a five-dimensional charged AdS bulk spacetime. For zero or small value of the brane cosmological constant, the shellworld universe has a nonsingular bouncing and cyclic nature with the bounce induced by the charge parameter of the bulk spacetime. On large value of the brane cosmological constant, the shellworld undergoes a nonsingular bounce and expands eternally. Under certain conditions, the brane bounces outside the bulk horizons, leading to a geometrical resolution of the Cauchy horizon instability present in standard braneworld models. Furthermore, analysis of linear scalar perturbations reveals that test scalar field modes remain regular and non-divergent across the bounce. Finally, we incorporate uniformly distributed long strings in the bulk and demonstrate that the bounce still persists while avoiding the horizon instability.

</details>


### [72] [A pedagogical introduction to invariant theory and finite-$N$ holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22952)
*Robert de Mello Koch,Minkyoo Kim,Augustine Larweh Mahu,Anik Rudra*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper provides a pedagogical introduction to finite-N AdS/CFT physics, using invariant theory to solve trace relations and classify gauge-invariant operators into primary (perturbative gravitational) and secondary (non-perturbative gravitational) invariants.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand finite-N corrections in AdS/CFT by resolving redundancies among gauge-invariant operators via trace relations and invariant theory.

Method: Application of classical invariant theory—particularly the theory of generators and relations for rings of invariants—to characterize the space of multi-trace operators in U(N) or SU(N) gauge theories, solving trace identities exactly at finite N.

Result: A complete, non-redundant basis of gauge-invariant operators is constructed; it splits into primary invariants (freely generating a polynomial subring) and secondary invariants (finite module generators over that subring); this algebraic structure maps to perturbative vs. non-perturbative sectors of the dual gravity theory.

Conclusion: The trace relation structure at finite N admits an exact algebraic solution, revealing a sharp operator-algebraic distinction between perturbative (primary) and non-perturbative (secondary) gravitational physics in AdS/CFT.

Abstract: These lectures provide a pedagogical introduction to the study of finite-$N$ physics of the AdS/CFT correspondence. More specifically we develop the consequences of the trace relations for the space of gauge invariant operators, using powerful methods from invariant theory. Our key conclusions are \begin{itemize} \item that the complete set of trace relations can be solved, leaving a non-redundant set of gauge invariant operators, \item that this non-redundant set can be generated using two types of generators, called primary and secondary invariants, and \item that the primary invariants acquire a natural interpretation as perturbative gravitational degrees of freedom, while the secondary algebra encodes non-perturbative effects of the dual gravitational theory. \end{itemize}

</details>


### [73] [Perturbative Anomaly Inflow on Orbifolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23326)
*Hao Y. Zhang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes perturbative anomaly inflow for 6D orbi-instanton theories from M5-branes on C²/Zₖ near an M9-brane, using Donnelly’s equivariant APS theorem; it matches the full unflavored tensor-branch anomaly polynomial—including Kac-label-dependent terms—and extends to flavored anomalies via E₈ centralizers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve and geometrically interpret the complete anomaly polynomial—including previously conjectured Kac-label-dependent remainder terms—for 6D A-type orbi-instanton theories arising from M5-branes on C²/Zₖ at an M9-brane, within M-theory corner-inflow framework.

Method: Applies Donnelly's equivariant Atiyah–Patodi–Singer (APS) theorem to compute fixed-point densities on orbifold fixed loci; extracts degree-(d+2) component as d-dimensional anomaly polynomial; evaluates non-identity ALE fixed-point class on the M9 wall with flat E₈ connection ρ: Zₖ → E₈; combines with known M5/Hořava–Witten and ALE/Hořava–Witten contributions.

Result: The degree-eight component of the non-identity ALE fixed-point class matches the full conjectured Kac-label-dependent remainder of the 6D anomaly polynomial, including SU(2)_R and tangent-bundle curvature dependence; together with established terms, it reproduces the total unflavored tensor-branch anomaly polynomial; flavor symmetries are incorporated via centralizers of ρ in E₈.

Conclusion: Perturbative anomaly inflow on orbifold fixed loci fully accounts for the 6D orbi-instanton anomaly polynomial, supporting a local M-theory corner-inflow mechanism; the equivariant APS framework provides a unified, geometric derivation that extends naturally to flavored anomalies.

Abstract: We study perturbative anomaly inflow for effective theories supported on orbifold fixed loci of the internal geometry. Donnelly's equivariant APS theorem gives a fixed-point density, whose degree-\((d+2)\) component is identified as the anomaly polynomial of the \(d\)-dimensional effective theory. We apply the construction to six-dimensional A-type orbi-instanton theories engineered by \(N\) M5-branes probing a transverse \(\mathbb{C}^2/\mathbb{Z}_k\) singularity at an end-of-the-world M9-brane. For a flat \(E_8\) connection specified by \(ρ:\mathbb{Z}_k\to E_8\), we compute the non-identity ALE fixed-point class on the M9 wall. Its degree-eight component agrees with the complete Kac-label-dependent remainder conjectured in \cite{MOTZ}, including the \(SU(2)_R\) and tangent-bundle curvatures. Together with the known M5/Hořava--Witten and ALE/Hořava--Witten terms, this reproduces the unflavored tensor-branch anomaly polynomial. This local equality further suggests a complete M-theory corner-inflow interpretation. The same prescription also be extended to include flavor symmetries as the centralizer of \(E_8\).

</details>


### [74] [A Combinatorial Origin of Locality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23008)
*Laurentiu Rodina,Giuseppe Sellaroli,Jonathan Valenzuela*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves that locality in tree-level scattering amplitudes of Tr(φ³) theory emerges from hidden zeros—on-shell kinematic conditions where the amplitude vanishes—combined with unitarity; it recasts locality as a geometric combinatorics problem on polygon triangulations and introduces the 'star test' and 'smoothing' to enforce pole compatibility.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide the first complete all-multiplicity proof that locality (i.e., compatible propagator poles) emerges—not just unitarity—from fundamental on-shell principles, specifically hidden zeros, in contrast to prior work showing unitarity emerging from locality.

Method: Reformulates locality in Tr(φ³) theory as a geometric combinatorics problem: planar propagators correspond to chords of an n-gon; locality requires denominator structures to form triangulations.
