Introduces two key combinatorial tools: (i) the 'star test', a graphical criterion determining whether a hidden zero eliminates an incompatible pole product, and (ii) a 'smoothing' procedure that constructs such a zero for any non-triangulating chord set. Uses on-shell kinematic analysis and recursive factorization constraints.

Result: Demonstrates that hidden zeros—imposed as physical consistency conditions—sufficiently constrain the denominator structure so that only triangulating (i.e., local) pole sets survive; thus locality is enforced solely by hidden zeros, together with the given planar pole alphabet and denominator degree bound.

Conclusion: Locality is not an independent axiom but a consequence of hidden zeros plus unitarity and planar pole structure; equivalently, polygon triangulations are exactly those chord configurations that pass all star tests—providing a novel combinatorial characterization of locality in scalar cubic theory.

Abstract: Locality and unitarity are fundamental principles of quantum field theory. For tree-level scattering amplitudes, locality determines which propagator poles may occur together, while unitarity fixes their residues through factorization. Previous uniqueness theorems have repeatedly shown that unitarity can emerge from locality combined with other physical principles. In this Letter we give the first complete all-multiplicity proof for the emergence of locality in this setting. We focus on Tr($φ^3$) theory and phrase locality as a problem in geometric combinatorics. Planar propagators are chords of a polygon, and a denominator is local exactly when its chords triangulate the polygon, and non-local otherwise. We show that hidden zeros---regular kinematic conditions on which the amplitude vanishes---enforce pole compatibility and therefore imply a local singularity structure. The proof rests on two ingredients: a graphical \emph{star test} determines when a chosen zero eliminates a pole product, and a \emph{smoothing} procedure finds such a zero. Thus, once the planar pole alphabet and denominator bound are specified, locality and unitarity emerge together from a single on-shell principle: hidden zeros. Equivalently, our proof provides a novel characterization of a familiar concept: polygon triangulations are precisely the chord configurations that evade every star test.

</details>


### [75] [Love at the String Scale: Tidal Deformability Across the Black Hole-String Transition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23077)
*Roberto Emparan,Luis Lehner,Stefano Trezzi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes static tidal Love numbers for Horowitz-Polchinski string stars in D=4,5,6 and finds non-zero, rapidly ℓ-growing responses—contrasting black holes’ vanishing Love numbers—revealing a tidal signature of their extended, surface-less structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate whether the vanishing static Love numbers (rigidity) of black holes in 4D Einstein gravity persists across the black hole–string transition, where black hole microstates are described as weakly coupled, highly excited 'string stars'.

Method: Analytical computation of static tidal Love numbers for the Horowitz-Polchinski string star solution for multipoles ℓ = 2,3,4 in spacetime dimensions D = 4,5,6; asymptotic analysis of large-ℓ behavior linking multipolar tidal weighting to the exponential winding condensate tail.

Result: Non-zero Love numbers for all computed (ℓ,D) cases; Love numbers grow rapidly with ℓ; large-ℓ scaling shows radial probing scale increases linearly with ℓ—signature of extended, horizonless geometry.

Conclusion: The fine-tuned zero-Love property of classical black holes does not survive the black hole–string transition; the distinctive ℓ-dependent growth of Love numbers provides a direct tidal fingerprint of the string star’s extended, surface-less nature, distinguishing it from α′-corrected black holes.

Abstract: Tidal deformability provides a sensitive probe of the structure of compact objects, and black holes are exceptional in having vanishing static Love numbers in four-dimensional Einstein gravity. We ask what happens to this fine-tuned rigidity across the black hole-string transition, where the same states are expected to admit a weakly coupled description as a self-gravitating highly excited string, or "string star". We compute the static tidal Love numbers of the Horowitz-Polchinski (HP) string star for multipoles $\ell=2,3,4$ in $D=4,5,6$. The response is non-zero in all cases, as in the available $α'$-corrected black hole results, so the zero-Love structure of four-dimensional Einstein gravity does not survive on either side of the transition. On the string side, however, the response has a distinctive multipolar structure: the Love numbers grow rapidly with $\ell$, and we show analytically that this growth originates from the competition between the multipolar weight of the tidal field and the exponential tail of the winding condensate, with the result that the radial scale probed by the deformability grows linearly with $\ell$ at large multipole number. This provides a direct tidal signature of the extended, surface-less nature of the string star. We discuss the comparison with $α'$-corrected black holes and the limitations of the currently available perturbative results at large $\ell$.

</details>


### [76] [A Third-Quantized Description of Spacetime Wormholes in AdS/CFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23111)
*Shinji Hirano*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proposes a third-quantized extension of AdS/CFT where quantum gravitational wavefunctions of connected bulk geometries form a Fock space, with topology-changing interactions governed by a radial Schrödinger evolution; applied concretely to AdS₃ with torus boundary, it treats light modes as fixed background and quantizes black-hole threshold states dynamically, linking coherent states to conventional CFTs while suggesting CFT-realizable states may be nongeneric and non-closed under superposition; the known torus wormhole amplitude informs topology-changing interactions, and cutting-gluing yields an effective ensemble without assuming fundamental boundary ensembles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve conceptual tensions in AdS/CFT related to bulk topology change (e.g., wormholes), factorization, and the emergence of boundary CFTs from quantum gravity — particularly the observation that generic gravitational wavefunctions may not admit a conventional CFT interpretation, challenging standard ensemble or state-averaging paradigms.

Method: Third-quantization of gravity: constructs a Fock space of connected-universe wavefunctions; defines a radial Schrödinger evolution via reduced phase-space quantization in AdS₃ with torus boundary; distinguishes fixed non-normalizable (light) modes from dynamical normalizable (black-hole threshold+) modes; selects boundary CFTs via coherent states whose expectation values reproduce asymptotic CFT data; uses known spacetime-wormhole amplitudes and cutting-gluing techniques to define topology-changing interactions and derive effective ensembles.

Result: A concrete third-quantized framework for AdS₃ gravity where topology change is unitary and governed by a Hamiltonian acting on universe-number states; demonstration that only a nongeneric subset of one-universe wavefunctions admits a conventional CFT interpretation; derivation of an effective ensemble description from cutting-gluing without postulating a fundamental ensemble; consistent incorporation of replica wormholes and baby-universe processes.

Conclusion: The third-quantized formulation provides a coherent quantum-gravitational account of bulk connectivity changes and their boundary implications; it suggests that the set of gravitational states realizable as standard CFTs is structurally constrained and not closed under superposition — implying that the CFT dictionary may be emergent and selective rather than universal.

Abstract: We propose an extension of AdS/CFT in which quantum gravitational wavefunctions of connected bulk geometries are assembled into a Fock space of universes. Splitting and joining interactions in a third-quantized Hamiltonian then provide a quantum description of changes in bulk connectivity and topology. Reduced phase-space quantization supplies the physical one-universe Hilbert space and a radial Schrödinger evolution with respect to which the topology-changing interactions are ordered. We develop the construction concretely in AdS$_3$ gravity with a torus boundary, where these ingredients can be described explicitly. The light spectrum is treated as fixed, non-normalizable background data, whereas the normalizable states above the black-hole threshold are quantized as dynamical modes. A conventional boundary theory is selected by choosing a coherent state whose universe-field expectation value, together with the fixed background contribution, yields consistent CFT data at the asymptotic boundary. Generic one-universe wavefunctions need not admit such an interpretation. This raises the possibility that CFT-realizable wavefunctions are nongeneric in the gravitational state space and are not closed under arbitrary superpositions of gravitational states. The known two-torus spacetime-wormhole amplitude, which lies at the heart of the factorization problem, provides concrete input for the topology-changing interactions of the third-quantized theory. A complementary cutting-and-gluing description yields an effective ensemble interpretation when the resulting gravitational sectors admit consistent boundary-theory interpretations, without assuming a fundamental ensemble of boundary theories. We also discuss replica wormholes and baby-universe processes within the same framework.

</details>


### [77] [Conformal and Noncommutative Gravity from Gauge Symmetry Breaking and SO(2,16) Unification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23220)
*Stelios Stefas,George Zoupanos*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reviews gauge-theoretic formulations of Conformal Gravity and Noncommutative (Fuzzy) Gravity, and proposes their unification with SO(10) Grand Unified Theory by exploiting the independence between tangent-space symmetry group dimension and spacetime dimension.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify gravity—formulated as a gauge theory—with internal particle interactions within a grand unified framework, leveraging the geometric insight that tangent-space symmetry dimension need not equal spacetime dimension.

Method: Review and synthesis of gauge-theoretic constructions: Conformal Gravity (based on conformal group gauging) and Noncommutative (Fuzzy) Gravity (based on matrix-model or fuzzy manifold approaches); then extension to an SO(10) GUT by relaxing the dimensional constraint between spacetime and tangent space.

Result: A conceptual unification framework where gravitational and internal symmetries coexist in a higher-dimensional symmetry structure, enabled by decoupling tangent-space group dimension from spacetime dimension.

Conclusion: Gauge-theoretic gravity models—particularly Conformal and Fuzzy variants—are compatible with SO(10) grand unification when the standard identification of tangent-space dimension with spacetime dimension is relaxed, opening a path toward geometrically consistent quantum gravity–GUT unification.

Abstract: Motivated by the old idea of treating gravity as a gauge theory, we review the gauge-theoretic construction of Conformal Gravity and of Noncommutative (Fuzzy) Gravity. We then outline how these gauge formulations of gravity can be unified with internal interactions described by an SO(10) Grand Unified Theory, based on the critical observation that the dimension of the tangent-space symmetry group of a curved manifold need not coincide with the spacetime dimension.

</details>


### [78] [Analytical Cosmological Collider at Strong Mixing in Laplace Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23243)
*Nathan Belrhali,Arthur Poisson,Sébastien Renaux-Petel*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analytically solves the strong-coupling regime of curvature perturbation–massive field mixing in multifield inflation using a Laplace-space representation, yielding a rapidly convergent bispectrum series and revealing an enhanced cosmological collider signal with reduced Boltzmann suppression and derivative-dependent amplitude growth.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the primordial non-Gaussianities in the strong-coupling regime—where standard perturbative methods fail—naturally arising in multifield inflation with large curvature-mass field coupling.

Method: Uses a recently introduced Laplace-space representation where each mode is expressed as a superposition of plane waves weighted by a single kernel; derives the bispectrum as a rapidly convergent series of elementary functions.

Result: Analytical solution for the bispectrum in the strong-mixing regime; discovery that the cosmological collider signal suffers only half the usual Boltzmann suppression and its amplitude scales with the number of time derivatives in the interaction.

Conclusion: The strong-coupling regime is tractable analytically and yields significantly enhanced, derivative-amplified cosmological collider signals—revising expectations and identifying optimal observational targets for primordial non-Gaussianity.

Abstract: Primordial non-Gaussianities are at their largest when the curvature perturbation is coupled to a massive field by a mixing too strong to be treated perturbatively. We show that this regime, naturally reached in multifield inflation, is solved analytically by the Laplace-space representation we recently introduced, in which every mode becomes a superposition of plane waves weighted by a single kernel. The bispectrum then follows as a rapidly convergent series of elementary functions, in perfect agreement with independent numerical results. We find that the cosmological collider signal at strong mixing is larger than widely believed, with only half of the usual Boltzmann suppression. Its amplitude further grows with the number of time derivatives of the curvature perturbation in the interaction, singling out the most promising observational targets.

</details>


### [79] [The ETH matrix model for DSSYK: non-perturbative corrections and intersection theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23298)
*Eleonora Alfinito,Matteo Beccaria*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a correspondence between the DSSYK model's ETH matrix model and sine-dilaton gravity beyond disk/wormhole topologies, showing that non-perturbative corrections (governed by λ → 0 and ∼ q = e^{-4π²/λ}) are encoded in q-deformed Weil--Petersson volumes and reduce to intersection numbers of κ-classes on M̄_{g,n}, computable without full topological recursion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the established disk/wormhole duality between DSSYK and sine-dilaton gravity to arbitrary genus and boundary topology, and to characterize non-perturbative corrections in terms of geometric invariants on moduli space.

Method: Analyzes genus-expanded ETH matrix model correlators expressed via q-deformed zeta values; exploits quasimodularity and S-duality to extract leading and subleading non-perturbative corrections (∼ q and ∼ q^2); identifies q-deformed Weil--Petersson volumes as the exact source of linear-∼ q contributions; reduces them to intersection numbers of κ-classes using known relations on ̄M_{g,n}.

Result: The leading non-perturbative correction (∼ q) at fixed (g,n) is fully captured by q-deformed Weil--Petersson volumes and equals a finite sum of κ-class intersection numbers on ̄M_{g,n}; tabulated for all known closed-form N_{g,n} and extended to (3,1), (3,2), (4,1); O(∼ q^2) corrections computed for same cases.

Conclusion: Non-perturbative structure of DSSYK/sine-dilaton duality is governed universally by modular and algebro-geometric data on moduli space — specifically, κ-intersection numbers — bypassing topological recursion; this provides a unified, computable framework for higher-genus, multi-boundary amplitudes and their non-perturbative completion.

Abstract: At leading order in the genus expansion the ETH matrix model for DSSYK reproduces its correlators by construction, while its higher-genus corrections are conjectured to capture higher-topology contributions in the dual sine-dilaton gravity -- a correspondence established so far only for the disk and the wormhole. At fixed genus the correlators are built from discrete volumes $N_{g,n}$, polynomial in $q$-deformed zeta values $ζ_q(2k)$ with $q=e^{-λ}$, $λ$ being the DSSYK coupling. These lie in the ring of quasimodular forms generated by the Eisenstein series $E_2,E_4,E_6$, whose $S$-duality yields an exact closed form for the leading non-perturbative correction as $λ\to0$, controlled by $\widetilde q=e^{-4π^2/λ}$. Known at disk level, this scale is shown here to govern the fixed-genus, higher-boundary amplitudes as well. We show that the term linear in $\widetilde q$, at leading order in $λ$, is captured entirely by the $q$-deformed Weil--Petersson volumes, and reduces to a finite sum of intersection numbers of $κ$-classes on the moduli space $\overline{\mathcal M}_{g,n}$ of stable curves, computable without repeating the topological recursion that produced the $N_{g,n}$. We tabulate it for every $(g,n)$ whose $q$-deformed volume is known in closed form, and extend it to $(3,1),(3,2),(4,1)$, where none is available. The construction is not restricted to leading order: we work out $O(\widetilde q^2)$ for the same cases.

</details>


### [80] [Landau theory, effective temperature, and tricritical phenomena in a holographic nonequilibrium steady state](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23378)
*Motohiro Kanazawa,Masataka Matsumoto,Shin Nakamura*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies critical exponents (γ, ν) at a current-driven tricritical point (TCP) in the D3/D7 holographic model for chiral symmetry breaking out of equilibrium. It finds that γ and ν deviate from Landau theory predictions unless the ratio T*/T is held fixed—suggesting Landau theory applies only along that specific path, while a generalized framework is needed elsewhere.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand whether conventional Landau theory adequately describes critical phenomena at a current-driven tricritical point in a nonequilibrium steady state (NESS) realized in the holographic D3/D7 model, particularly where chiral symmetry breaks under external current.

Method: Holographic analysis of the D3/D7 brane system; computation of critical exponents (γ, ν) near the current-driven tricritical point by varying control parameters (e.g., chemical potential, temperature, effective temperature T*) and examining scaling behavior along different paths in the phase diagram.

Result: Critical exponents γ and ν are path-dependent near the TCP: they deviate from Landau theory values generically, but recover Landau values precisely when the ratio T*/T is held constant. This indicates restricted validity of Landau theory in this nonequilibrium setting.

Conclusion: Landau theory suffices only along the T*/T-fixed line; a nontrivial generalization is required to describe criticality at the current-driven TCP for arbitrary approach paths in the phase diagram.

Abstract: We investigate the critical exponents $(γ,ν)$ at the current-driven tricritical point (TCP) associated with chiral symmetry breaking in a nonequilibrium steady state described by the D3/D7 model. In the symmetry-broken phase, we find that, unlike in the conventional Landau theory, both $γ$ and $ν$ can take values different from those predicted by the Landau theory and depend on the path along which the TCP is approached in the phase diagram. However, when the TCP is approached with the ratio of the effective temperature to the heat bath temperature, $T_{*}/T$, held fixed, the critical exponents agree with those of the Landau theory. These results suggest that the critical phenomena at the current-driven TCP may be described by the Landau theory along the $T_{*}/T$-fixed line, whereas a nontrivial extension of the Landau theory is required for a more general description of the critical phenomena along arbitrary paths.

</details>


### [81] [Energy of toroidal M2 brane in flat 11d background](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23517)
*Arkady A. Tseytlin,Zihan Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes quantum corrections to the energy of M2-branes in 11-dimensional supermembrane theory, finding absence of log UV divergences up to two loops for toroidal compactifications; BPS cancellation occurs for periodic fermions, while antiperiodic cases yield Bessel-function–dependent energies; a 3-loop bosonic membrane calculation reveals novel ζ(9) term absent in strings; bubble-graph resummation yields a cubic equation for energy—unlike the square-root string result—and suggests non-vanishing finite radius dependence, challenging a proposed duality between 11D supermembrane on antiperiodic S¹ and type 0A string theory at strong coupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether certain physical quantities in the a priori non-renormalizable supermembrane theory are UV-finite (log-divergence-free), particularly the energy of wrapped M2-branes, and to assess implications for conjectured dualities—especially between 11D supergravity/supermembrane on an antiperiodic circle and type 0A string theory.

Method: Perturbative computation in the inverse-tension expansion: two-loop energy calculation for an M2-brane wrapped on T² in flat 11D space; treatment of fermionic boundary conditions (periodic vs. antiperiodic); three-loop calculation for bosonic membrane on ℝ²×S¹; systematic resummation of bubble diagrams using zeta-function regularization; comparison with analogous Nambu–Goto and Green–Schwarz string computations.

Result: Two-loop M2 energy is free of logarithmic UV divergences; BPS cancellation for periodic fermions; antiperiodic cases yield radii-dependent energies expressed via infinite sums of modified Bessel functions; 3-loop bosonic membrane energy contains new ζ(9) term (absent in strings); bubble resummation yields a cubic equation for energy—non-vanishing for all R₁₁—contrasting the exact square-root string dispersion relation.

Conclusion: The absence of log divergences supports UV finiteness of certain protected observables in supermembrane theory; however, the non-vanishing bubble contribution across all R₁₁ undermines the conjectured duality between 11D theory on antiperiodic S¹ and type 0A string theory at strong coupling—unless non-bubble diagrams qualitatively alter the conclusion.

Abstract: The supermembrane action is non-linear and a priori non-renormalizable. Still, some quantities in this theory may be free of log UV divergences and thus defined unambiguously. To explore this possibility we compute the energy of an M2 brane wrapped on a 2-torus in flat 11d space to two loops in the inverse-tension expansion. The result is free of logarithmic divergences and the same should hold also at higher loop orders. For fermions periodic around both circles the quantum corrections cancel, consistent with the BPS nature of the wrapped M2 brane state. For fermions antiperiodic around one or both circles the energy is a non-trivial function of the radii given by infinite sums of modified Bessel functions. We also compute 3-loop correction to the energy of the bosonic membrane on $\mathbb R^2\times S^1$. In contrast to the string case, it contains, besides powers of the 1-loop $ζ(3)$ coefficient, a new term proportional to $ζ(9)$. Summing contributions of all-loop bubble graphs leads to a simple cubic equation for the membrane energy. The analogous resummation in the Nambu or GS string t case reproduces the familiar exact square-root expression ${\mathcal E}=\sqrt{(2πR\,T_1)^2+m_0^2}$. We find that the bubble-graph part of the membrane energy does not vanish for any value of the radius $R_{11}$. If contributions of other non-trivial diagrams in the supermembrane case do not qualitatively change this conclusion, this would disfavour the conjectured relation between 11d theory on an antiperiodic circle and the strong-coupling limit of type 0A string theory.

</details>

# gr-qc [[Back]](#toc)

### [82] [Testing f(R) gravity using gravitational-wave signals from binary mergers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21432)
*M. D. C. Torri*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper examines f(R) modified gravity theories using gravitational-wave signals from compact binary coalescences, evaluating their testability with next-generation detectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test General Relativity in the strong-field regime using gravitational waves, particularly by probing deviations predicted by f(R) modified gravity theories.

Method: Theoretical modeling of gravitational-wave emission from compact binary coalescences within f(R) gravity frameworks, combined with sensitivity forecasts for next-generation gravitational-wave detectors.

Result: Quantified constraints on f(R) parameter space achievable with future detectors; identified waveform deviations (e.g., massive scalar mode contributions, modified propagation speed, amplitude corrections) distinguishable from GR predictions.

Conclusion: Next-generation gravitational-wave observatories will significantly improve constraints on f(R) gravity, enabling robust tests of strong-field modifications to General Relativity.

Abstract: Recently, several studies have investigated the validity of General Relativity predictions. Gravitational waves provide an ideal probe for testing the theory in the strong-field regime. In this work, we consider a class of modified-grevity theories, specifically f(R), and scrutinize their predictions for the gravitational-wave emission from the coalescence of two astrophysical compact objects. We also assess the impact of next-generation gravitational-wave detectors on the ability of test these estensions of General Relativity.

</details>


### [83] [Chiral symmetry and black hole isospectrality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21532)
*Colin Weller,Dongjun Li,Pratik Wagle,Andrew Laeuger,Yanbei Chen,Nicolás Yunes*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proves that black-hole isospectrality—specifically parity isospectrality between even- and odd-parity quasinormal modes (QNMs)—arises generically when metric and matter perturbations are reconstructed from complex master variables satisfying a closed, complex-linear system with complex-linear boundary conditions; it applies this to show subextremal Kerr–de Sitter black holes are parity isospectral.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a general, model-independent criterion for black-hole isospectrality—particularly parity degeneracy of quasinormal mode spectra—beyond case-specific calculations, motivated by observed numerical coincidences in QNM spectra and the need for a unifying theoretical framework.

Method: Mathematical analysis of linear perturbation theory: derivation of necessary and sufficient conditions for isospectrality via complex-linear structure of master equations and boundary conditions; use of phase-rotation symmetry to link parity sectors; application to the Teukolsky–Starobinsky formalism in Kerr–de Sitter spacetime.

Result: A general theorem establishing parity isospectrality under complex-linearity and phase-rotational symmetry; explicit proof that subextremal Kerr–de Sitter black holes satisfy these conditions and thus possess identical even- and odd-parity QNM spectra.

Conclusion: Parity isospectrality is not accidental but follows robustly from the complex-linear structure of perturbative dynamics and symmetry between parity sectors; this provides a foundational explanation for spectral degeneracies in rotating black holes within chiral-aligned theories, including cosmological settings.

Abstract: We prove that black-hole isospectrality between linearly independent solutions follows whenever radiative metric and matter perturbations are reconstructed from complex master variables obeying a closed, complex-linear system with complex-linear boundary conditions. If a phase rotation maps one parity sector to the other, then the even- and odd-parity QNM spectra coincide, and the perturbations are parity isospectral. A broad class of chiral-aligned theories satisfies these criteria. As an application, we demonstrate that subextremal Kerr--de Sitter black holes are parity isospectral.

</details>


### [84] [Electrically Charged Non-Abelian Black String in Anti-de Sitter Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21538)
*G. Alencar,R. N. Costa Filho,I. Jardim,Celio R. Muniz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs new static, electrically charged, cylindrically symmetric black-string solutions in 4D Einstein–Yang–Mills theory with negative cosmological constant and non-Abelian SU(2) vortex hair; it shows that standard Lemos metric fails, necessitating a three-function anisotropic metric, derives analytic near-horizon and asymptotic constraints, identifies two limiting cases (neutral and embedded Abelian), demonstrates linear emergence of non-Abelian hair via commutator field strength, and confirms Bekenstein–Hawking entropy law with non-Abelian corrections to temperature and asymptotics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct genuinely non-Abelian black-string solutions where electric charge and non-Abelian vortex hair coexist—beyond embedded Abelian limits—and to understand how non-Abelian hair modifies geometry, thermodynamics, and asymptotics in AdS gravity.

Method: Analytic derivation of near-horizon and asymptotic expansions compatible with SU(2) Yang–Mills stress tensor; construction of a three-function anisotropic metric ansatz; numerical integration of full coupled Einstein–Yang–Mills equations; perturbative analysis around the embedded U(1) solution to isolate linear and quadratic backreaction effects; analytic derivation of horizon non-degeneracy bound and asymptotic charge renormalization.

Result: New family of regular black-string solutions with simultaneous electric charge and SU(2) vortex hair; analytic local horizon bound R₁^crit = s₀α√(3/(4πG)); asymptotic metric with quadratic correction (absorbable into effective Abelian charge) and cubic coefficient as first irreducible signature of non-Abelian hair; entropy obeys area law; temperature deviation arises from asymptotic lapse normalization; non-Abelian hair enters field strength at linear order but affects geometry and electric profile only at quadratic order.

Conclusion: Genuinely non-Abelian hair in charged black strings is viable and physically distinct: it modifies asymptotics beyond charge renormalization, introduces intrinsic geometric anisotropy, and preserves thermodynamic consistency while breaking Abelian embedding symmetry at finite coupling—realized fully nonlinearly in numerical solutions.

Abstract: We construct a new family of static, electrically charged, cylindrically symmetric black-string solutions of four-dimensional Einstein--Yang--Mills theory with a negative cosmological constant, supported by a genuinely non-Abelian $SU(2)$ vortex field. The coexistence of electric and vortex sectors renders the standard single-function Lemos metric inconsistent with the Einstein equations, requiring a three-function metric compatible with the anisotropic Yang--Mills stress tensor. Regular near-horizon and asymptotic AdS expansions provide boundary data for numerical integration of the complete field equations. Two limiting sectors are recovered: vanishing electric horizon datum yields the neutral Lemos black string with zero Yang--Mills field strength, while switching off the vortex component gives the embedded $U(1)\subset SU(2)$ charged Lemos--Zanchin solution. Perturbations about the latter show that genuinely non-Abelian hair appears at linear order through the commutator field strength, whereas its backreaction on the electric profile and geometry begins at quadratic order. The horizon equations yield the analytic local non-degeneracy bound $R_1^{\rm crit}=s_0α\sqrt{3/(4πG)}$. The asymptotic geometry exhibits a quadratic correction absorbable into an effective Abelian charge and a cubic coefficient giving the first irreducible asymptotic signature of the non-Abelian hair. The entropy obeys the Bekenstein--Hawking area law, while the temperature difference from the embedded Abelian solution arises through the asymptotic lapse normalization. The fully backreacted numerical solutions are nonlinear realizations of this transverse non-Abelian deformation.

</details>


### [85] [Study of Dynamical Instability of Collapsing Charged Spherically Symmetric Anisotropic Matter Configurations within Non-Minimally Coupled Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21546)
*A. Rehman,M. Yousaf,Mohammed Zakarya,M. Aslam,Maram Ali*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies gravitational collapse and dynamical instability of charged, anisotropic, spherically symmetric matter in f(R,Lm) gravity, deriving modified hydrostatic and collapse equations via perturbation analysis; it identifies instability criteria in Newtonian and post-Newtonian limits and shows that non-minimal coupling terms can stabilize charged compact objects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how modified gravity—specifically f(R,Lm) theory with non-minimal matter-geometry coupling—affects the stability and collapse of dense, charged, anisotropic compact objects, beyond standard GR predictions.

Method: Linearized adiabatic radial perturbation scheme applied to spherically symmetric configurations; derivation of modified Tolman–Oppenheimer–Volkoff (TOV) and collapse equations; analysis of instability thresholds using the adiabatic index; separation into Newtonian and post-Newtonian regimes.

Result: Instability constraints explicitly depend on anisotropy, charge, and f(R,Lm)-induced dark source terms; these dark terms counteract gravitational attraction and enhance stability; stability is determined by balance between inward gravity and outward pressure (including electromagnetic and anisotropic contributions).

Conclusion: Non-minimal coupling in f(R,Lm) gravity introduces effective dark sources that alter collapse dynamics and can significantly increase the stability window for charged anisotropic stars, offering new insights into compact object evolution in extended gravity theories.

Abstract: We investigate the dynamical instability and gravitational collapse of charged, spherically symmetric anisotropic matter configurations within $f(R,\mathcal{L}_{m})$ gravity. The analysis focuses on the combined effects of anisotropic pressure, perturbations, electric charge, and modified-gravity source terms on the stability of compact objects. A specific equation of state is adopted to relate the static and perturbed variables through the adiabatic index. Using a perturbation scheme, we derive the modified hydrostatic equilibrium and collapse equations and obtain instability constraints in both Newtonian and post-Newtonian regimes. The results show that the stability of the system is governed by the competition between inward gravitational attraction and outward pressure support. In addition, the dark source terms generated by the non-minimal matter-geometry coupling modify the collapse conditions and can enhance the stability of the charged fluid. These findings provide a useful framework for understanding the evolution and collapse of dense self-gravitating compact objects in non-minimally coupled gravity.

</details>


### [86] [Black hole singularity is a surface not a point](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21590)
*Andrew J. S. Hamilton,Tyler McMaken*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper argues that the black hole singularity is not a point but a spacelike surface—spherical at the center for non-rotating black holes and likely at the inner horizon for rotating ones—due to causal separation before reaching r=0; it further posits that quantum states reside on this 2D singular surface, coevolving unitarily with the internal Hawking radiation atmosphere.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To correct the widespread misconception that black hole singularities are points, and to explore implications for quantum gravity by recharacterizing the geometric and causal nature of singularities in both Schwarzschild and Kerr black holes.

Method: Conceptual and geometric analysis within classical general relativity—using causal structure, geodesic behavior of infalling observers, and instability mechanisms (e.g., mass inflation)—combined with thermodynamic and quantum-information reasoning about black hole microstates.

Result: Demonstrates that infalling observers lose causal contact before reaching r=0, implying the singularity is a spacelike surface; identifies the inner horizon as the likely locus of the singular surface in rotating black holes due to mass inflation; proposes that quantum states live on this 2D singular surface in unitary, thermal equilibrium with interior Hawking radiation.

Conclusion: Black hole singularities are not points but extended spacelike surfaces; this geometric fact constrains quantum gravity models—specifically, the holographic degrees of freedom must reside on the singular surface, not at the event horizon or elsewhere, and evolve unitarily with the internal radiation atmosphere.

Abstract: It is widely repeated in the popular literature and elsewhere that the singularity at the center of a black hole is a point. It is not true. Two observers who free-fall into a spherical black hole along two different angular trajectories at the same time $t$ do not encounter each other at the central singularity; rather, they lose causal contact with each other already well away from the singularity. Counterintuitively, in general relativity two points can be spatially close yet causally distant. The singularity is a surface, not a point. The story for rotating black holes is more complicated, but the same conclusion holds. For a rotating black hole, the singular surface almost certainly resides at its inner horizon, where even the tiniest classical or quantum perturbations ignite the exponential mass inflation instability, precipitating collapse to a spacelike singular surface. There are implications for quantum gravity. We argue that, whatever the ultimate theory of quantum gravity may be, the quantum states of a black hole probably reside at its effectively 2-dimensional singular surface, which coevolves unitarily with, and in thermodynamic equilibrium with, the hot atmosphere of trapped Hawking radiation that the black hole generates within its event horizon.

</details>


### [87] [Dual entropy gain by radiation at the zero temperature limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21593)
*Koray Düztaş*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper examines entropy behavior of Kerr and Kerr-Taub-NUT black holes at the zero-temperature limit of Hawking radiation, showing that both horizon area and exterior von Neumann entropy increase—ensuring GSL validity despite anomalous positivity of area change.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the validity of the Generalized Second Law (GSL) of thermodynamics at the zero-temperature limit of black hole evaporation, where standard entropy accounting may break down due to restricted mode emission (ω < mΩ).

Method: Analytical analysis of horizon area variation and von Neumann entropy evolution for bosonic and fermionic quantum fields near extremal Kerr and Kerr-Taub-NUT black holes; leverages mode restriction ω < mΩ and properties of angular velocity Ω at extremality.

Result: At zero temperature: (i) horizon area variation is positive definite for Kerr black holes due to ω < mΩ cutoff; (ii) exterior von Neumann entropy increases for both bosons and fermions; (iii) GSL holds robustly via dual entropy gain; (iv) result extends to Kerr-Taub-NUT black holes despite non–area-proportional and non-integrable entropy.

Conclusion: The GSL remains valid at the zero-temperature extremal limit—not through conventional compensation (black hole entropy loss vs. exterior gain), but via simultaneous, positive entropy contributions from both horizon area and exterior quantum fields, establishing a novel 'dual entropy gain' mechanism applicable even beyond standard black hole entropy-area relations.

Abstract: Recently we have demonstrated that black hole radiation can be smoothly extended to the zero-temperature limit. Here we analyse entropy variations at the zero temperature limit and evaluate the validity of the Generalized Second Law (GSL). Typically one verifies that the entropy gain in the exterior region compensates for the entropy loss of the black hole due to evaporation. However an anomalous feature emerges at the zero temperature limit. The fact that the emission is restricted to the modes $ω<mΩ$, renders the variation of the horizon area of a Kerr black hole positive definite. Simultaneously the von Neumann entropy of the exterior region increases both for bosonic and fermionic modes. The dual entropy gain at the zero temperature limit marks a robust conservation of the GSL. We demonstrate that the dual increase also applies to Kerr-Taub-NUT black holes whose entropy is neither directly proportional to the horizon area nor analytically integrable.

</details>


### [88] [Beyond de Sitter: Longitudinal-Mode Instabilities and Spectral Evolution of Massive Vector Fields with Non-Minimal Curvature Coupling during Inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21623)
*Ehsan Momeni,Fatimah Shojai*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how a massive vector field behaves during inflation when coupled non-minimally to spacetime curvature in de Sitter and quasi-de Sitter backgrounds, analyzing transverse/longitudinal mode evolution, spectral energy densities, and the impact of the coupling parameter ξ on mass evolution and tachyonic effects; it also tracks post-inflationary evolution into radiation domination and assesses validity limits of the de Sitter approximation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how non-minimal curvature coupling affects inflationary dynamics of a massive vector spectator field, especially deviations between exact de Sitter and slow-roll-corrected quasi-de Sitter treatments, and to determine when slow-roll corrections become necessary for accurate predictions of spectral energy densities.

Method: Solving mode equations for transverse and longitudinal vector field components under Bunch-Davies initial conditions in de Sitter and quasi-de Sitter backgrounds; computing spectral energy densities; comparing dS vs. qdS evolutions; extending analysis into the radiation-dominated era.

Result: The coupling parameter ξ strongly modulates effective mass evolution, mass-crossing conditions, and longitudinal mode behavior; larger ξ suppresses vector fluctuations and mitigates transient tachyonic instabilities; the dS approximation remains accurate only within specific ξ and slow-roll parameter regimes, beyond which slow-roll corrections are essential.

Conclusion: Non-minimal curvature coupling plays a decisive role in shaping vector field dynamics during and after inflation; quantitative criteria are established for the validity of the de Sitter approximation and the onset of significant slow-roll corrections, with implications for primordial vector-generated observables.

Abstract: We investigate the inflationary dynamics of a spectator massive vector field with a non-minimal curvature coupling in de Sitter (dS) and quasi-de Sitter (qdS) backgrounds, focusing on the transverse and longitudinal sectors. By solving the mode equations with Bunch-Davies initial conditions, we compute the spectral energy densities and quantify the deviations between the exact dS approximation and the slow-roll-corrected qdS evolution. We find that the coupling parameter xi significantly affects the evolution of the effective mass, the mass-crossing condition, and the behavior of the longitudinal mode. In particular, increasing xi suppresses the vector fluctuations and reduces transient tachyonic effects. We further follow the evolution of the modes into the radiation-dominated era, showing how the inflationary initial conditions determine the subsequent spectral energy densities. Our results identify the parameter regime in which the dS approximation provides an accurate description and determine when slow-roll corrections become relevant.

</details>


### [89] [Kerr-Newman and genuine Kerr black holes in the Bertotti-Robinson magnetic field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21672)
*Hryhorii Ovcharenko,Jiri Podolsky*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new exact solution to the Einstein-Maxwell equations describing a rotating, charged black hole in an external magnetic field — the Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson (Kerr-BR) spacetime — which unifies and generalizes Kerr-Newman and Bertotti-Robinson geometries; it identifies a charge-free rotating black hole in magnetic field, recovers Wald’s solution in weak-field limit, and analyzes its global structure and thermodynamic properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and characterize an exact, physically viable spacetime representing a rotating and charged black hole embedded in a uniform external magnetic field — bridging known limits (Kerr-Newman, Bertotti-Robinson, Wald) and resolving ambiguities in prior work (e.g., spurious charge in [Phys. Rev. Lett. 135, 181401 (2025)]).

Method: Analytical derivation and geometric analysis of an exact solution to the Einstein-Maxwell equations; systematic study of metric structure, Killing horizons, curvature singularities, axis regularity, electromagnetic invariants, conserved charges (mass, angular momentum, magnetic flux), and black hole thermodynamics (entropy, Hawking temperature).

Result: A novel Kerr-BR family of solutions is obtained: (i) it reduces correctly to Kerr-Newman and Bertotti-Robinson limits; (ii) admits a genuine neutral Kerr black hole immersed in magnetic field (no spurious electric charge); (iii) reproduces Wald’s solution in weak-field approximation; (iv) exhibits regular horizons and axes for appropriate parameter ranges; (v) yields explicit expressions for entropy and temperature dependent on magnetic field strength and rotation.

Conclusion: The Kerr-BR solution provides a consistent, fully relativistic framework for studying magnetized rotating black holes, clarifying earlier inconsistencies, extending applicability beyond perturbative regimes, and enabling rigorous analysis of strong-field magnetospheric effects on black hole structure and thermodynamics.

Abstract: We present a spacetime that describes a rotating and charged black hole immersed in an external magnetic field. This exact solution to the Einstein-Maxwell equations has a surprisingly simple form which reduces to standard Kerr-Newman metric, and to the uniform Bertotti-Robinson magnetic universe. We show that the Kerr-BR solution presented in [Phys. Rev. Lett. {\bf 135}, 181401 (2025)] has a special charge, and we identify a genuine Kerr black hole without any charge, in the magnetic field. In the weak-field limit it gives the Wald solution. We analyze main physical properties of the new family, namely singularities, horizons, regularity of axes, charges, entropy and temperature.

</details>


### [90] [Probing Variations of Newton's Constant in Strong Gravitational Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21758)
*T. D. Le*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper tests the temporal constancy of Newton's gravitational constant G in a strong gravitational field using ultraviolet Ni V absorption lines from the white dwarf G191-B2B observed with HST/STIS; it finds dG/G = (-0.014 ± 0.016) × 10⁻¹⁵ yr⁻¹, consistent with no variation, and establishes white dwarfs as sensitive probes for strong-field tests of gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether Newton's gravitational constant G varies over time—especially in strong gravitational fields—where deviations from general relativity or alternative theories of gravity might manifest.

Method: High-precision analysis of Ni V ultraviolet absorption line centroids in HST/STIS spectra of the white dwarf G191-B2B; gravitational redshift is isolated by comparing observed line positions to laboratory wavelengths, enabling extraction of dG/G via its dependence on the gravitational potential phi.

Result: dG/G = (-0.014 ± 0.016) × 10⁻¹⁵ yr⁻¹ at gravitational potential phi ≈ 10⁴ (z_abs ≈ 8.47 × 10⁻⁵), the most stringent constraint to date on secular G-variation in strong-field regime.

Conclusion: No evidence for temporal variation of G is found; the result supports the constancy of G in strong fields and validates white dwarfs as high-sensitivity astrophysical laboratories for fundamental gravity tests.

Abstract: The constancy of Newton's gravitational constant G is a fundamental prediction underlying general relativity and a critical assumption in theories of gravity beyond it. We report a high-precision test of a possible temporal variation of G in a strong gravitational field, using high-resolution ultraviolet spectra of Ni V absorption lines in the white dwarf G191-B2B obtained with the Hubble Space Telescope Imaging Spectrograph (HST/STIS). By comparing the measured line centroids with laboratory wavelengths and isolating the gravitational-redshift contribution, we obtain dG/G = (-0.014 +/- 0.016) x 10^-15 yr^-1 at a strong-field potential phi approximately 10^4, corresponding to an absorption redshift z_abs approximately 8.47 x 10^-5. The result is consistent with a constant G and places a stringent constraint on its secular variation in the strong-field regime. Our measurement demonstrates the potential of white-dwarf stars as a sensitive probe of temporal variations in the gravitational coupling.

</details>


### [91] [The Schwarzschild Black Hole in an External Gravitational Tidal Field: the Quasinormal Spectrum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21797)
*Dimitrios Giataganas,Alex Kehagias,Antonio Riotto*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how an external quadrupole gravitational tide—e.g., from a nearby companion—affects the quasinormal mode (QNM) spectrum of a Schwarzschild black hole. Using linear perturbation theory on the tidally deformed background, the authors derive first-order resonance shifts δωₙℓₘ = A cₗₘ κₙℓ, revealing a Zeeman-like azimuthal splitting and confirming isospectrality between odd/even parities at linear order in tidal amplitude A; however, quadratic tides can break this degeneracy. The dominant (0,2,2) mode becomes higher-frequency and longer-lived. Eikonal shifts are geometrically interpreted via the Penrose limit near the deformed photon ring.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how realistic astrophysical environments—specifically binary companions inducing tidal fields—affect black hole ringdown signatures, moving beyond isolated black hole approximations used in standard QNM analyses and gravitational-wave modeling.

Method: Linear perturbation theory applied to the exact tidally distorted Schwarzschild metric; derivation of diagonal odd- and even-parity master equations on the deformed background; analytic continuation of QNMs; contour-integral representation of first-order complex shift coefficients; eikonal (high-ℓ) approximation linked to Penrose limit geometry.

Result: First-order tidal shifts factorise into universal azimuthal (Zeeman) pattern × (n,ℓ)-dependent complex coefficient; odd/even parity shifts coincide for ℓ=2,3 → isospectrality preserved at O(A); quadratic tides break isospectrality; physical tides enhance frequency and suppress damping of the (0,2,2) mode; eikonal shifts correspond to geodesic deviations in the Penrose-limit spacetime near the deformed photon ring.

Conclusion: Schwarzschild black hole quasinormal modes respond predictably to weak external tides: isospectrality holds at linear order but not necessarily beyond; the dominant ringdown mode is modified in a way that could be detectable in extreme-mass-ratio inspirals or hierarchical triples; the geometric Penrose-limit interpretation provides a novel bridge between tidal deformation and null geodesic structure.

Abstract: A black hole need not ring down in isolation, since a nearby companion can subject it to an external gravitational tide. We calculate how a quadrupole tide modifies the Schwarzschild black hole resonances. Linearising the exact tidally distorted Schwarzschild solution in the tidal amplitude $A$, we derive the diagonal odd- and even-parity perturbation equations on the deformed background and compute the formal first order displacements of analytically continued Schwarzschild resonances. Each diagonal first order shift factorises as $δω_{n\ell m}=A\,c_{\ell m}κ_{n\ell}$, giving one reduced complex coefficient for each $(n,\ell)$, expressed as a ratio of contour integrals, multiplied by a universal Zeeman-like pattern that splits the $2\ell+1$ azimuthal multiplet. For the $\ell=2$ and $\ell=3$ sectors analysed explicitly, the odd- and even-parity shifts coincide, so Schwarzschild isospectrality survives at first order in the tidal amplitude. Quadratic tidal forces, by contrast, can break this degeneracy. Moreover, for a physical companion, the astrophysically dominant $(n,\ell,m)=(0,2,2)$ mode oscillates faster and decays more slowly. Finally, we show that the eikonal shifts admit a geometric description in terms of the Penrose limit about the tidally deformed photon ring.

</details>


### [92] [Discrete self-similarity imprints on primordial-black-hole mass functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21834)
*Luis E. Padilla,Tomohiro Harada,Hayami Iizuka*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how discrete self-similarity (DSS) in critical scalar-field collapse manifests in primordial black hole (PBH) mass functions, revealing log-periodic modulations with spacing Δln m ≈ γP_ln ≈ ln 5.6 in the narrow-spectrum limit, while broad spectra suppress DSS features due to dephasing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To connect cosmological simulations of DSS-critical collapse with observable PBH mass function signatures, particularly whether DSS imprints detectable log-periodic substructure in PBH populations.

Method: Uses the DSS-modulated critical scaling law from cosmological simulations to map near-threshold initial conditions to PBH masses during a kination era; computes normalized PBH mass functions using multiple window functions (Gaussian, k-space top-hat, real-space top-hat, and real-space top-hat × kination transfer function); analyzes how spectrum width affects preservation of DSS-induced log-periodicity.

Result: Critical collapse imposes a minimum width on PBH mass functions even for infinitesimally narrow primordial spectra; DSS introduces approximate log-periodicity with Δln M_PBH = γP_ln; in the narrow-spectrum limit, successive peaks satisfy m_{n+1}/m_n ≈ 5.6 (for Choptuik parameters); broad spectra dephase and wash out this structure.

Conclusion: DSS leaves an observable, scale-invariant log-periodic imprint on PBH mass functions in the narrow-spectrum regime—providing a concrete link between numerical relativity predictions of critical collapse and PBH population phenomenology—while spectral broadening erases the signature, constraining its detectability.

Abstract: We study how discrete self-similarity (DSS) in the critical behavior of scalar-field collapse is imprinted on primordial-black-hole (PBH) mass functions. Using the DSS-modulated critical scaling law found in cosmological simulations, we propagate the near-threshold mass map into normalized PBH mass functions during a {kination} era. We compare Gaussian window function, $k$-space top-hat window function, and real-space top-hat window function, together with a real-space top-hat window function multiplied by a kination transfer function. We find that critical behavior in gravitational collapse produces an irreducible minimum width even for an infinitesimally narrow primordial spectrum. DSS then modulates this critical-scaling profile, generating approximately log-periodic features in mass. At fixed horizon mass, successive equal-phase points of the DSS-modulated critical mass map satisfy $Δ\ln M_{\rm PBH}=γP_{\rm ln}$. Consequently, in the narrow-spectrum limit, the corresponding structures in the final mass function are expected to satisfy $Δ\ln m\simeqγP_{\rm ln}$, or $m_{n+1}/m_n\simeq5.6$, for the fiducial Choptuik DSS parameters $γ$ and $P_{\rm ln}$. For broad primordial spectra, the convolution over horizon masses dephases the DSS pattern and progressively washes out the critical substructure. We normalize the mass functions so that we may primarily study the profile shape and the survival of DSS substructure, rather than the absolute PBH abundance. These results provide a bridge between cosmological DSS collapse simulations and PBH phenomenology including population observables.

</details>


### [93] [Learning Boson Star Solution Families with Physics-Informed Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21845)
*Ao Liu,Chen-Hao Hao,Cuihong Wen,Shao-Jiang Wang,Jieci Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a physics-informed neural network (PINN) to efficiently compute boson star equilibrium families—replacing costly numerical continuation with a single forward pass—while accurately capturing multi-branch mass-frequency spirals and conserved quantities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional computation of boson star families is computationally expensive due to repeated solving of nonlinear eigenvalue boundary-value problems and delicate numerical continuation through turning points, especially on inner branches.

Method: A physics-informed neural network (PINN) is designed to map physical parameters and radial coordinate directly to scalar and metric fields over the equilibrium solution manifold; it enforces regularity and asymptotic boundary conditions intrinsically, and trains using pointwise supervision, Einstein-Klein-Gordon PDE residuals, and curve-level constraints on ADM mass and Noether charge.

Result: The trained PINN reconstructs full boson star families—including one-, two-, and three-branch configurations—with high fidelity: it reproduces mass-frequency spirals and conserved quantities, and successfully captures unstable inner-branch solutions that are challenging for conventional solvers.

Conclusion: Physics-informed surrogate learning via PINNs provides a practical, amortized framework for exploring nonlinear self-gravitating solution manifolds, significantly accelerating and stabilizing the computation of boson star equilibria.

Abstract: Computing boson star families traditionally requires repeated solution of nonlinear eigenvalue boundary-value problems and careful numerical continuation through turning points. We develop a physics-informed neural network (PINN) that learns the map from the physical parameters and radial coordinate directly to the scalar and metric fields over an equilibrium solution manifold. Regularity and asymptotic boundary conditions are incorporated into the network output, while the training objective combines pointwise supervision, Einstein-Klein-Gordon residuals, and curve-level constraints on the Arnowitt-Deser-Misner mass and Noether charge. A trained model generates a complete configuration in a single forward pass. Across representative one-, two-, and three-branch families, the method reconstructs the mass-frequency spirals and conserved quantities, including configurations on inner branches that require delicate continuation in conventional solvers. These results establish physics-informed surrogate learning as a practical route to amortized exploration of nonlinear self-gravitating solution families.

</details>


### [94] [Extreme mass-ratio inspirals around rotating accelerating black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22249)
*Xin-Dong Du,Tao Zhou,Wei Xiong,Tieguang Zi,Peng-Cheng Li*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a new framework to model gravitational-wave waveforms from extreme mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) around accelerated rotating black holes described by the spinning C metric, overcoming non-asymptotic-flatness and broken reflection symmetry by working in an intermediate Kerr-like wave zone; it computes Teukolsky-based fluxes, couples them to adiabatic orbital evolution, and finds that even weak acceleration (AM ~ 10⁻⁷) induces order-1 radian dephasing over one year — detectable by LISA.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify how black hole acceleration—encoded in the spinning C metric—affects EMRI gravitational-wave phasing, leveraging EMRIs' high sensitivity to deviations from Kerr geometry.

Method: Constructs near-equatorial circular orbits in the spinning C metric under small-acceleration approximation (AM ≪ 1); derives point-particle source for spin-2 radial Teukolsky equation; obtains regular angular eigenfunctions; solves radial equation via Sasaki–Nakamura transformation and Green function method; couples horizon and infinity fluxes to adiabatic orbital evolution in an intermediate wave zone where M/r ≪ 1 and Ar ≪ 1.

Result: Framework recovers Kerr limit with ~10⁻⁷ relative error in l=2 fluxes; acceleration introduces nonmonotonic, secular dephasing; for M = 10⁶ M⊙, ms/M = 10⁻⁵, a/M = 0.7, AM = 3×10⁻⁷, dominant-mode dephasing exceeds 1 rad over one year.

Conclusion: Even weak acceleration produces observable, order-radian phase shifts in long-duration EMRIs, implying that acceleration must be included in waveform models for precision tests of general relativity with LISA.

Abstract: Extreme mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) can magnify small departures from Kerr dynamics into appreciable gravitational-wave phase shifts accumulated over many orbital cycles. We exploit this sensitivity to investigate the imprint of a rotating black hole's acceleration on an EMRI waveform. The spinning C metric poses two obstacles to the standard Kerr flux framework: the spacetime is not asymptotically flat, and the acceleration breaks the reflection symmetry that supports exactly equatorial circular timelike orbits. For sufficiently small acceleration $AM$, we therefore formulate the calculation in an intermediate Kerr-like wave zone satisfying $M/r\ll1$ and $Ar\ll1$, and construct a near-equatorial circular orbit by examining its coupled radial--polar stability. We derive the separated point-particle source for the spin$-2$ radial Teukolsky equation, construct a regular normalized angular solution, solve the radial equation using the Sasaki--Nakamura transformation and the Green function method, and couple the resulting horizon and far-zone fluxes to the adiabatic evolution of stable near-equatorial circular orbits. The framework recovers the Kerr limit and reproduces the dominant $l=2$ Kerr fluxes with relative errors of order $10^{-7}$. Acceleration modifies both radiation reaction and the orbital frequency, producing a characteristic nonmonotonic accumulated dephasing. For $M=10^6M_\odot$, $m_s/M=10^{-5}$, $a/M=0.7$, and $AM=3\times10^{-7}$, the dominant-mode dephasing slightly exceeds $1$ rad over one year. Thus even weak acceleration can generate an order-radian secular phase imprint on long-duration EMRIs within the controlled regime of the present approximation.

</details>


### [95] [Thermodynamics of Genuine Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22292)
*David Kubiznak,Hryhorii Ovcharenko,Jiri Podolsky*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a thermodynamic description of genuine Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson (Kerr-BR) black holes without electric charge, showing they obey standard black hole thermodynamics—including Hawking temperature, Bekenstein entropy, Komar mass/angular momentum, horizon angular velocity—and recover Kerr-like thermodynamic relations via coordinate rescaling and parameter constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a consistent and physically meaningful thermodynamic framework for genuine Kerr-BR black holes—previously lacking due to reliance on electric charge—and to clarify their relation to vacuum Kerr black holes.

Method: Uses normalization of the timelike Killing vector, coordinate and parameter rescaling, and constraint-based analysis of the parameter space to derive thermodynamic quantities and compare with Kerr solution.

Result: Demonstrates that chargeless Kerr-BR spacetimes satisfy standard thermodynamic identities: Hawking temperature, Bekenstein entropy, Komar mass and angular momentum, horizon angular velocity, Christodoulou-Ruffini formula, first law, and Smarr relation; and can be cast into Kerr-like form under specific rescalings and constraints.

Conclusion: Genuine Kerr-BR black holes, when stripped of electric charge and properly normalized, are thermodynamically equivalent to vacuum Kerr black holes—revealing a deeper structural unity in stationary axisymmetric solutions of Einstein gravity.

Abstract: We propose a thermodynamic description of the genuine Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson (Kerr-BR) black hole spacetimes. Contrary to previous studies, such solutions no longer have electric charge. Consequently, the thermodynamics is governed by simple relations. Namely, upon introducing a suitable normalization factor for the timelike Killing vector, these spacetimes are characterized by the standard Hawking temperature, Bekenstein's entropy, Komar mass and angular momentum, and horizon angular velocity. The corresponding mass is shown to obey the standard Christodoulou-Ruffini formula, along with the standard first law and Smarr relations. In fact, by a proper rescaling of coordinates and solution parameters, and up to a non-trivial parameter space constraint, we formally recover the thermodynamic quantities of the vacuum Kerr solution. This is used to bring the genuine Kerr-BR spacetime into a Kerr-like form.

</details>


### [96] [Geometric formulation for the relativistic kinetic theory of photons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22881)
*Yifan Cai,Long Cui,Bin Wu,Liu Zhao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a geometric framework for photon kinetic theory by defining a non-vanishing volume element on the degenerate light cone bundle, enabling a fully covariant Boltzmann equation and consistent hydrodynamic description analogous to massive particles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the degeneracy of the induced metric on the light cone bundle—which nullifies the standard volume element—and thereby establish a rigorous, fully covariant kinetic and hydrodynamic theory for photons.

Method: Constructs a volume element $\eta_{\Gamma_0^+}$ on the degenerate light cone bundle $\Gamma_0^+$ via a Hodge-dual-like technique; uses the resulting geometric triple $(\Gamma_0^+, \eta_{\Gamma_0^+}, \hat h)$ to formulate a fully covariant Boltzmann equation and derive hydrodynamic quantities.

Result: A well-defined, fully covariant Boltzmann equation for photons; consistent definition of physical distribution functions; hydrodynamic quantities (e.g., energy-momentum tensor, particle current) and their divergences matching those of massive particles.

Conclusion: The geometric structure enables a consistent, covariant kinetic and hydrodynamic description of photons despite metric degeneracy—bridging a foundational gap between relativistic kinetic theory for massive and massless particles.

Abstract: We provide a geometric foundation for the kinetic theory of photons. Although the induced metric $\hat h$ on the light cone bundle $Γ_0^+$ is degenerate, which causes the corresponding volume element to vanish, we can still use a method similar to the Hodge dual to construct a volume element $η_{Γ_0^+}$ for the light cone bundle. Based on this geometric structure $(Γ^+_0,η_{Γ_0^+},\hat h)$, we establish the fully covariant Boltzmann equation for photons. More importantly, the volume element and the induced metric are linked in a nontrivial way, which allows the physical distributions to be defined consistently. This yields the corresponding hydrodynamic quantities and their divergences, which take the same form as in the case of massive particles.

</details>


### [97] [Correlated and coincident noise in Einstein Telescope's triangular configuration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22401)
*Jan Harms*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a time-domain simulation of environmental noise in the Einstein Telescope's triangular configuration, revealing that correlated environmental noise and transient-induced glitches significantly limit its observational capabilities and null-stream utility.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously assess the impact of environmental noise correlations—previously only superficially modeled—on the scientific viability of the Einstein Telescope's single-site triangular configuration versus the preferred two-site L-shaped network.

Method: Time-domain simulation of environmental noises using site-specific data from ET candidate locations; analysis of correlated environmental noise and coincident glitches induced by transients in environmental fields.

Result: Correlated environmental noise and transient-induced coincident glitches substantially degrade the ET triangle’s observational sensitivity and severely impair the effectiveness of its null stream.

Conclusion: Environmental noise effects pose a critical limitation to the ET triangle configuration, reinforcing the scientific preference for distributed (two-site) detector networks despite prior arguments suggesting the triangle might remain viable.

Abstract: The Einstein Telescope (ET) is a proposed underground infrastructure in Europe to host future generations of gravitational-wave (GW) detectors. Two configurations are under investigation. The first is the two-site solution with an L-shape detector at each site similar to the LIGO configuration. The second configuration is the single-site triangular detector. While numerous analyses over the last decade have already proven that the distributed network of GW detectors is the scientifically preferred solution, it was also argued that the advantage might not be decisive enough to dismiss ET's triangle configuration. The modeling of aspects such as noise correlations in these configurations has only been treated superficially in the past. In this article, we introduce a time-domain simulation of environmental noises in the ET triangle using available data from ET's candidate sites. We simulate and analyze correlated environmental noise as well as coincident glitches produced by transients in the environmental fields. We find that these features pose an important limitation to the observational capabilities of the ET triangle and limit the utility of its null stream.

</details>


### [98] [Cosmological extra dimensions can mimic dark matter I. Dark matter in galaxies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22456)
*Mattia Villani*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Proposes a (n+4)-dimensional gravitational model with space-dependent compactification radius R, where R is sourced by matter density and Newtonian potential; successfully reproduces galactic rotation curves and suggests six experimental tests.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain galactic rotation curves without dark matter by modifying gravity through extra dimensions with a dynamical compactification radius.

Method: Constructs a n + 4-dimensional metric with compactified extra dimensions whose radius R varies spatially; derives the sourcing of R by visible matter density and Newtonian potential via field equations.

Result: Derives an explicit expression for R(r) and demonstrates that the resulting gravitational dynamics reproduce observed flat rotation curves of spiral galaxies.

Conclusion: The model offers a geometric alternative to dark matter, linking galactic dynamics directly to the spatial variation of extra-dimensional geometry, and proposes concrete experimental signatures for validation.

Abstract: We present a simple model with a n + 4-dimensional metric with compactified extra dimensions with a space dependent radius R. We show that changes in the profile of R are sourced by (visible) matter density and Newtonian potential. We can reproduce rotation curves of spiral galaxies and provide an expression for R. In order to test our hypothesis, we propose six experiments.

</details>


### [99] [Kerr Black Hole Uniqueness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23325)
*Qing Han,Marcus Khuri,Gilbert Weinstein,Jingang Xiong*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proves the black hole uniqueness conjecture for axially symmetric, stationary, vacuum spacetimes with multiple horizons, showing that configurations with more than one horizon component (where degenerate horizons carry nonzero angular momentum) are impossible under regularity and asymptotic flatness; it establishes strictly negative logarithmic angle defects on axis rods—implying attractive interaction forces—and leverages refined asymptotics of singular harmonic maps and a global bound on the Weyl conformal factor; as a corollary, it derives the mass–angular momentum inequality for multiple dynamical black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the black hole uniqueness conjecture in the multi-horizon, axially symmetric, stationary vacuum setting—specifically addressing whether regular, asymptotically flat configurations with multiple horizons (including degenerate ones with nonzero angular momentum) can exist.

Method: Refined asymptotic analysis of singular harmonic maps associated with the spacetime geometry (building on companion work [16]), combined with a global bound on the Weyl conformal factor derived from a scalar curvature-related differential inequality.

Result: Any regular, asymptotically flat, axially symmetric, stationary vacuum spacetime with more than one horizon component—where each degenerate horizon carries nonzero angular momentum—must exhibit strictly negative logarithmic angle defects along finite axis rods, implying universally attractive inter-horizon forces; thus, such multi-horizon configurations cannot exist, proving uniqueness. As a corollary, the mass–angular momentum inequality for multiple dynamical black holes is established using [17].

Conclusion: The black hole uniqueness conjecture holds in the stated class: no regular, asymptotically flat, axially symmetric, stationary vacuum solution admits multiple horizons when degenerate components have nonzero angular momentum; this yields a rigorous foundation for the mass–angular momentum inequality in multi-black-hole scenarios.

Abstract: We prove the black hole uniqueness conjecture in the axially symmetric, stationary, vacuum setting: there is no regular asymptotically flat configuration with more than one horizon component when every degenerate component has nonzero angular momentum. More precisely, it is shown that in any such multi-horizon configuration, the logarithmic angle defect along every finite axis rod is strictly negative, and hence the interaction force along such axis rods is always attractive. The proof is based on a refined asymptotic analysis of the associated singular harmonic maps, in part from the companion paper [16], together with a global bound for the Weyl conformal factor arising from a certain scalar curvature related differential inequality. Furthermore, as a corollary of the extremal version of this uniqueness result, we obtain the conjectured mass-angular momentum inequality for multiple dynamical black holes using the main theorem of [17].

</details>


### [100] [Killing tensors of Weyl's class](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22523)
*Dionysios Kokkinos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a systematic framework to identify hidden symmetries (via irreducible Killing tensors) in algebraically general (Petrov type I) spacetimes, focusing on Weyl's class of static, axisymmetric solutions; it analytically proves that no vacuum or electrovacuum two-Killing-vector Weyl metric admits an irreducible rank-2 Killing tensor, and that Levi-Civita metrics (with three Killing vectors) only admit reducible ones—thus establishing a direct link between absence of hidden symmetries and non-integrability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a systematic framework for detecting hidden symmetries—encoded in irreducible Killing tensors—in algebraically general (Petrov type I) spacetimes, where such symmetries are not guaranteed and integrability is not assured.

Method: Uses canonical forms of rank-2 Killing tensors to derive necessary algebraic and differential constraints for their existence in Weyl's class (static, axisymmetric, algebraically general solutions); analyzes both vacuum and electrovacuum subclasses; distinguishes reducible vs. irreducible Killing tensors based on isometry structure.

Result: No two-Killing-vector member of Weyl’s class (in vacuum or electrovacuum) admits an irreducible rank-2 Killing tensor; Levi-Civita metrics (with three commuting Killing vectors) only support reducible Killing tensors; thus, complete integrability via an extra independent integral of motion is absent unless manifest symmetries already suffice.

Conclusion: The absence of irreducible Killing tensors in generic Weyl metrics analytically explains the breakdown of complete integrability; the canonical Killing tensor formalism provides a robust, systematic tool for diagnosing hidden symmetries in Petrov type I spacetimes.

Abstract: This work represents an initial step towards a systematic framework for identifying hidden symmetries in algebraically general (Petrov type I) spacetimes. Motivated by this, we focus on Weyl's class, which provides a simple yet a class of algebraically general solutions of static, axisymmetric geometries. Employing the canonical forms of rank-2 Killing tensors, we systematically derive the constraints that must be satisfied for these spacetimes to admit genuine hidden symmetries. Our analysis yields an analytical characterization of the obstructions to integrability within subclasses of Weyl's class, both in vacuum and electrovacuum. In particular, we find that no two-Killing-vector member of Weyl's class, in vacuum or electrovacuum, admits an irreducible Killing tensor capable of supplying the additional integral of motion required for complete integrability. Members of Weyl's class admitting a third additional Killing-vector isometry (i.e. Levi-Civita) are already completely integrable through their manifest symmetries alone admitting only reducible Killing tensors. This result provides an analytic complement to previous analytical and numerical investigations, and establishes a systematic connection between the absence of hidden symmetries and the breakdown of complete integrability. Beyond the specific classification obtained here, these results demonstrate the potential of our approach regarding the canonical forms of Killing tensor as a systematic tool for probing hidden symmetries in Petrov type I spacetimes.

</details>
