### [101] [Comment on Stellar structure and stability of charged interacting quark stars and their scaling behavior](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22653)
*Yann Bissessur,Michael Gammon,Robert B. Mann*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This comment corrects an error in prior work on radial stability of charged interacting quark stars, yielding revised fundamental eigenfrequencies; corrected solutions show broader stability ranges, with previously unstable branches now containing stable configurations, and all satisfy at least some recent observational mass-radius constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To correct an error in the radial stability analysis of charged interacting quark stars reported in Phys. Rev. D 102, 034031 (2020) and propagated into Phys. Rev. D 104, 123007 (2021), which led to erroneous stability conclusions.

Method: Recalculation of the fundamental radial eigenfrequencies for charged, interacting quark stars within general relativity using corrected radial stability equations; comparison of stability curves (M–R relations) and assessment against four newly incorporated observational constraints.

Result: Corrected eigenfrequencies shift the stability limits: the separation between maximum-mass and stability-limit points changes significantly in magnitude; all branches previously deemed fully unstable now contain stable configurations; overall stability range is enlarged; a subset of corrected mass–radius relations satisfies recent observational constraints.

Conclusion: Charged interacting quark stars exhibit greater radial stability than previously claimed; the correction restores physical viability to several parameter regimes earlier ruled out, reinforcing their plausibility as compact object candidates consistent with current astrophysical data.

Abstract: We comment on an error in the charged quark star literature ( Phys. Rev. D, 102(3):034031, 2020 ) that resulted in erroneous solutions to the radial stability equations for charged interacting quark stars in ( Phys. Rev. D, 104(12):123007, 2021 )[2]. In this comment article we recalculate the fundamental radial eigenfrequencies for charged, interacting quark stars in general relativity and discuss any departure from previous results [2]. Most of the qualitative trends persist, but the magnitudes of the separation between the maximum mass and stability points on a given branch can change significantly. We also note that all branches in [2] that contained no stable solutions now have stable solutions in their corrected counterparts. The net effect is that in all of the studied cases charged quark stars have a greater range of stability than originally predicted. We also compare four recent observational constraints not included in previous studies, and find that these constraints are satisfied by a subset of the mass-radius relations we obtain.

</details>


### [102] [Scalar charge bounds for extremal black hole formation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23355)
*Berend Schneider*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper establishes a lower bound |e|r_+ > 1/3 for scalar charge e in extremal Reissner–Nordström black hole formation via charged scalar field collapse, shows no such bound exists for subextremal collapse, and proves a sufficient condition |e|r_+ > √[(3+√33)/3] for Schwarzschild-to-extremal transition — challenging the third law of black hole mechanics in vacuum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate whether the third law of black hole mechanics — stating that extremality cannot be reached from a non-extremal state in finite time/process — holds for gravitational collapse of charged scalar fields, particularly in light of recent constructions (Kehle–Unger) of exactly extremal black holes.

Method: Rigorous mathematical analysis of spherically symmetric gravitational collapse of a charged scalar field coupled to Einstein–Maxwell equations; derivation of necessary and sufficient conditions on the scalar charge e and horizon radius r_+ for extremal black hole formation; comparison with subextremal scenarios using energy estimates and asymptotic analysis.

Result: 1) For collapse forming an exactly extremal RN black hole, |e|r_+ > 1/3 is necessary. 2) For subextremal collapse, arbitrary non-zero e can yield arbitrary subextremal charge-to-mass ratio. 3) A Schwarzschild black hole can become extremal if |e|r_+ > √[(3+√33)/3] ≈ 1.847.

Conclusion: The existence of finite, order-unity scalar charge enabling extremalization contradicts the strict interpretation of the third law of black hole mechanics in vacuum, suggesting its failure in the presence of matter fields with appropriate couplings.

Abstract: I prove that a collapsing charged scalar field with scalar charge $\mathfrak{e}$ that forms an exactly extremal Reissner--Nordström black hole à la Kehle--Unger with radius $r_+$ must satisfy $|\mathfrak{e}|r_+ > \frac{1}{3}$. In contrast, I also show that no such bound exists for subextremal collapse: a scalar field with an arbitrary $\mathfrak{e} \ne 0$ can form a subextremal black hole with an arbitrary (subextremal) charge-to-mass ratio.
  Complementing the extremal bound, I prove that a Schwarzschild black hole can become extremal if $|\mathfrak{e}|r_+ > \sqrt{\frac{3 + \sqrt{33}}{3}}$. The fact that $|\mathfrak{e}|r_+$ can be taken to be of order unity could be considered evidence that the third law of black hole mechanics in vacuum is false.

</details>


### [103] [Joule Thomson expansion of $κ$-deformed Schwarzschild-AdS black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22791)
*Vishnu Rajagopal,Puxun Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies Joule-Thomson expansion in a κ-deformed non-commutative Schwarzschild-AdS black hole, finding that the κ-deformation enables expansion in otherwise non-expanding uncharged AdS black holes; the ratio $T_i^{(min)}/T_c \approx 0.493$ is universal (parameter-independent) and matches the Reissner-Nordström case; a deformation-dependent minimum black hole mass threshold for expansion is identified.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore thermodynamic analogies between black holes and ordinary fluids—specifically, whether quantum gravity-induced spacetime non-commutativity (via κ-deformation) can induce Joule-Thomson expansion in uncharged AdS black holes, which normally lack such behavior in standard general relativity.

Method: Analytical thermodynamic analysis of the κ-deformed Schwarzschild-AdS black hole: derivation of equation of state, inversion curve, critical points, and Joule-Thomson coefficient using the Hawking temperature, horizon radius as 'volume', and cosmological constant as thermodynamic pressure within extended phase space.

Result: κ-deformation triggers Joule-Thomson expansion in uncharged AdS black holes; $T_i^{(min)}/T_c \simeq 0.493$, independent of κ; existence of a κ-dependent minimum black hole mass below which expansion ceases.

Conclusion: Non-commutative geometry encoded by κ-deformation qualitatively alters black hole thermodynamics, enabling fluid-like expansion phenomena even without charge, and yields universal inversion-to-critical temperature ratios consistent with charged black holes—suggesting robustness of certain thermodynamic features across different deformations or charges.

Abstract: We investigate the Joule-Thomson expansion of a non-commutative Schwarzschild-AdS black hole, whose space-time geometry is described by the Lie-algebraic $κ$-deformed space-time, and show that the $κ$-deformation parameter induces Joule-Thomson expansion in an uncharged-AdS black hole. We find the ratio of minimum inversion temperature to critical temperature $T_i^{(min)}/T_c \simeq 0.493$, is independent of the deformation parameter, and is in close agreement with the value for Reissner-Nordstrom black hole. Furthermore, we show the existence of a minimum black hole mass (depending on the $κ$-deformation parameter) below which the Joule-Thomson expansion vanishes.

</details>


### [104] [Curvature and Isocurvature Perturbations in multi-field Gauss-Bonnet inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22970)
*Seoktae Koh*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies cosmological perturbations in multi-field inflation with Gauss-Bonnet coupling f(φᵃ), deriving the quadratic action, identifying vanishing effective mass and mixing for curvature perturbations due to adiabaticity, and computing modified scalar/tensor spectra under two limiting regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how generic Gauss-Bonnet couplings in multi-field inflation affect cosmological perturbations—particularly curvature–isocurvature dynamics, sound speed, and tensor-to-scalar ratio—beyond Einstein gravity.

Method: Derives the full quadratic action for field perturbations in spatially flat gauge; performs mode decomposition into curvature (ℛ) and isocurvature modes; applies Weinberg’s adiabatic mode argument; analyzes two asymptotic regimes: (i) heavy isocurvature limit (integrating out isocurvature), and (ii) aligned gradient limit (f_N = 0) where turn-rate governs superhorizon transfer; computes power spectra and r at linear order in GB coupling.

Result: Gauss-Bonnet coupling introduces an antisymmetric velocity coupling absent in GR; ℳ_ℛ² = ℳ_mix² = 0 identically; curvature power spectrum acquires a modified sound speed in the heavy-isocurvature limit; in the aligned-gradient limit, isocurvature-to-curvature transfer is governed by a GB-modified turn rate; tensor power spectrum and r receive linear corrections from the coupling.

Conclusion: Gauss-Bonnet coupling enriches multi-field inflation phenomenology via novel derivative couplings and modified background dynamics, preserving adiabaticity constraints while altering observable signatures—especially sound speed and turn-rate–driven isocurvature conversion—offering distinct imprints testable in CMB and LSS.

Abstract: We study cosmological perturbations in multi-field inflation in which the scalar fields couple to the Gauss-Bonnet terms through a general coupling function $f(φ^a)$. We derive the complete quadratic action for the field perturbations in spatially flat gauge and find that, besides corrections to the kinetic, gradient and mass matrices, the Gauss-Bonnet coupling induces an antisymmetric velocity coupling that vanishes in Einstein gravity. Decomposing the field perturbations into curvature and isocurvature modes, we show that the effective mass of the curvature perturbation and the non-derivative mixing mass vanish identically, $\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{R}}^2 = \mathcal{M}_{\rm mix}^2 =0$ which are justified by the Weinberg's adiabatic mode. We then compute the curvature power spectrum in two limits. First, when the isocurvature mode is heavy, it can be integrated over all scales. This yields an effective single-field theory of the curvature perturbation with a modified sound speed. Second, when the gradient of the coupling function is aligned with the background trajectory ($f_N =0$), the derivative mixings disappear and the superhorizon transfer of isocurvature into curvature perturbations is governed by the turn rate, which is modified by the Gauss-Bonnet corrections. We also present the tensor power spectrum and the tensor-to-scalar ratio at linear order in the coupling.

</details>


### [105] [Gravitationally-decoupled hairy black holes: probing geometric, optical, and quasinormal mode signatures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23091)
*Paul Allen M. Gonzales,Anna Chrysostomou,Alan S. Cornell,Emmanuel Rodulfo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper uses gravitational-decoupling to generate hairy black hole geometries from Schwarzschild seed solutions, analyzing how deformations affect horizon structure, photon spheres, shadows, and scalar quasinormal modes; it emphasizes the importance of ADM mass normalization over seed mass and shows that horizon radius alone does not fix optical or perturbative properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically explore deviations from classical black holes using gravitational-decoupling, and assess whether horizon-scale or asymptotic observables (shadows, QNMs) can distinguish such hairy geometries—especially in light of EHT observations of M87* and Sgr A*.

Method: Gravitational-decoupling applied to Schwarzschild seed; parameter-space screening via horizon existence and energy conditions; geometric comparisons at fixed horizon radius; ADM mass normalization for shadow scaling; computation of scalar QNMs via 6th-order WKB and improved Asymptotic Iteration Method.

Result: Shadow angular scales and scalar QNMs depend strongly on ADM mass—not seed mass—highlighting ambiguity if normalization is ignored; fixing horizon radius does not uniquely determine photon sphere radii, shadow sizes, or QNM spectra; horizon structure, null geodesic observables, and QNMs are complementary probes.

Conclusion: Gravitationally-decoupled hairy black holes exhibit degeneracies in observable signatures unless properly normalized by ADM mass; multi-messenger constraints (imaging + spectroscopy) are essential to break these degeneracies and test beyond-GR physics.

Abstract: Gravitational-decoupling provides a systematic framework for constructing black hole geometries from known (seed) solutions (such as the Schwarzschild), whose exterior properties closely resemble those of the seed solution while still retaining potentially distinguishable features. Using this approach, we study a class of static, spherically symmetric hairy black holes and examine how such deformations affect their horizon structure, photon spheres, shadow scales, and scalar quasinormal modes (QNMs). We first determine the physically controlled regions of the parameter space by imposing the relevant horizon and energy condition requirements, while retaining configurations outside these domains only for exploratory comparison. To isolate geometric effects, we compare solutions at a common horizon radius, while accounting explicitly for the distinction between the mass parameter entering the seed geometry and the ADM mass measured asymptotically. The resulting ADM normalized shadow scales are used for an illustrative comparison with the characteristic angular scales of M87* and Sgr A*. We also compute massless scalar QNMs using the sixth-order Wentzel-Kramers-Brillouin approximation and the improved Asymptotic Iteration Method, finding close agreement over the modes considered. Our results show that fixing the horizon radius does not uniquely determine either the optical or perturbative properties of the spacetime, and that mass normalization can significantly alter the interpretation of geometric trends. Horizon structure, null geodesic observables, and scalar QNMs therefore provide complementary probes of gravitationally-decoupled black hole geometries.

</details>


### [106] [Remarks on quantum scalar field theory for a uniformly accelerated reference frame and a Schwarzschild black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23108)
*Mikhail N. Smolyakov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper examines formal consistency issues in quantum scalar field theory on uniformly accelerated (Rindler) and Schwarzschild black hole spacetimes, revealing pathologies—including infinite energy for certain Minkowski states in Rindler space and inconsistency of the Unruh vacuum in 4D Schwarzschild—while also addressing challenges in Kruskal-Szekeres coordinates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify and analyze foundational inconsistencies and underexamined pathologies in quantum scalar field theories on non-inertial and curved backgrounds—specifically Rindler and Schwarzschild spacetimes—where standard constructions (e.g., Unruh vacuum) may break down.

Method: Rigorous analysis of quantum state definitions, energy expectations, and algebraic/structural consistency of QFT in curved spacetime; comparison of state properties across coordinate systems (Minkowski vs. Rindler, Schwarzschild vs. Kruskal-Szekeres); examination of domain and self-adjointness issues for field operators and Hamiltonians.

Result: 1) Certain Minkowski quantum states exhibit divergent energy in Rindler spacetime, exposing a pathology in the Rindler quantization scheme. 2) The standard construction yielding the Unruh vacuum in 4D Schwarzschild spacetime is shown to be internally inconsistent. 3) Consistency of QFT in Kruskal-Szekeres spacetime remains problematic due to global causal and boundary ambiguities.

Conclusion: Standard QFT constructions on non-globally-hyperbolic or non-stationary regions (e.g., Rindler wedge, Schwarzschild exterior) suffer from deep structural flaws—particularly regarding energy finiteness and vacuum uniqueness—necessitating revised axiomatic or algebraic frameworks for consistent quantum field theory in such backgrounds.

Abstract: In the present paper, the cases of a uniformly accelerated reference frame and a Schwarzschild black hole in two and four dimensions are discussed focusing on formal aspects of the corresponding quantum scalar field theories. The main emphasis is made on building consistent quantum field theories and highlighting the problems that are not considered elsewhere. In particular, some pathologies in the case of a uniformly accelerated reference frame are discussed in detail: it is shown that some quantum states that are well defined in Minkowski spacetime possess an infinite energy when considered in Rindler spacetime. In the case of a Schwarzschild black hole in four dimensions, it is shown that the standard theory providing the Unruh vacuum state turns out to be inconsistent. The problem of consistency of quantum scalar field theory in Kruskal-Szekeres spacetime is also discussed.

</details>


### [107] [Relational Quantum Causal Processes toward Quantum Gravity with Controlled Einstein Response](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23117)
*Yipeng Xu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper establishes a fixed-band closure theorem in quantum gravity by constructing a controlled benchmark within a declared physical Fourier band, using a finite-Hilbert interacting Hamiltonian to derive geometric and gravitational observables—such as Lorentzian gap, Newton coefficient, and stress tensor—without fitting interface parameters or altering dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the central challenge in microscopic quantum-gravity models: deriving geometric and gravitational observables from a single refining quantum family without changing dynamics or introducing ad hoc interface coefficients.

Method: Constructs a finite-Hilbert interacting Hamiltonian defined on a fixed physical Fourier band; employs Duhamel and Weyl inequalities, uniform gap analysis, analytic perturbation theory, and kernel-remainder estimates; uses spectral analysis and source derivatives of the Schwinger functional to extract observables.

Result: Demonstrates convergence of two inequivalent spatial symbols to the same limiting Hamiltonian; uniquely fixes a positive induced Newton coefficient from the metric source; yields nonzero connected four-response and dimensionless spectral combination without fitting; supplies a conserved stress tensor for semiclassical Friedmann evolution.

Conclusion: Establishes a same-family fixed-band closure theorem—providing a reproducible benchmark for quantum gravity—but stops short of full QFT or nonperturbative quantum gravity, explicitly delineating required further theorems for complete closure.

Abstract: The central challenge in microscopic quantum-gravity models is to derive geometric and gravitational observables from one refining quantum family without changing dynamics or fitting a new coefficient at each interface. We construct a controlled benchmark on a declared fixed physical Fourier band. For every spatial regulator, one finite-Hilbert interacting Hamiltonian defines a unitary completely positive process and a Euclidean Schwinger functional. Its spectrum and source derivatives determine the Lorentzian gap, connected four-response, mixed matter--geometry response, dynamic geometry kernel, induced Newton coefficient, and quantum-matter stress entering a semiclassical FLRW evolution.
  Two inequivalent spatial symbols obey analytic second- and fourth-order bounds and converge to the same limiting Hamiltonian. Duhamel and Weyl inequalities, a uniform gap, analytic perturbation theory, and a kernel-remainder estimate yield a common fixed-band regulator limit. Within the declared local covariant FLRW two-derivative sector, the metric source uniquely fixes a positive induced Newton coefficient rather than leaving it as an interface fit. Direct diagonalization independently yields a nonzero connected fourth response and a non-fitted dimensionless spectral combination. The excitation gap supplies a conserved stress tensor for a semiclassical Friedmann trajectory.
  This is a same-family fixed-band closure theorem, not an all-band QFT or completed quantum gravity. The two-mode band, finite oscillator cutoff, covariant two-derivative sector, and semiclassical Einstein equation are inputs. It does not construct a quantum metric Hilbert space, quantum BV measure, topology-change control, or autonomous sector selection. The result provides a reproducible benchmark and isolates the further theorems needed for nonperturbative closure.

</details>


### [108] [Complex frequency evolution of direct waves from binary black hole mergers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23209)
*Ling Sun,Sizheng Ma,Patrick P. Chen,Andrew Laeuger,Neil Lu,Yanbei Chen*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces the 'direct wave' concept—a source-sensitive waveform component encoding horizon-formation dynamics—by filtering out quasinormal modes from numerical-relativity simulations; it shows that the direct wave’s instantaneous complex frequency evolves toward horizon-controlled values (2Ω_H, ~2κ_H), with spin-dependent convergence behavior, revealing merger dynamics and supporting a horizon-driven interpretation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To isolate and interpret horizon-formation information encoded in gravitational-wave signals—beyond standard quasinormal-mode (QNM) ringing—by distinguishing source-sensitive 'direct wave' contributions from asymptotic radiation.

Method: Employs rational response filters to suppress quasinormal modes in numerical-relativity waveforms; extracts instantaneous complex frequency via analytic-signal methods; analyzes evolution of real frequency and decay rate relative to remnant Kerr parameters (Ω_H, κ_H); leverages pole-zero structure of Kerr response to design minimal spin-dependent filters.

Result: Direct wave exhibits late-time convergence of instantaneous real frequency to 2Ω_H (spin-dependent direction: from above for χ_f ≲ 0.7, from below for χ_f ≳ 0.7) and decay rate to ~2κ_H (sometimes exceeding it); early-frequency proximity to 2Ω_H for χ_f ∼ 0.7 reflects smooth orbital-to-horizon frequency transition; finite-time deviations encode merger dynamics; approximate pole-zero pairing enables efficient spin-tuned filtering.

Conclusion: The direct wave provides a physically grounded, horizon-referenced observable in gravitational-wave signals; its evolution validates the causal imprint of horizon formation on asymptotic radiation and enables calibration of merger dynamics through horizon-controlled asymptotics.

Abstract: While no signal originating at a black hole's event horizon can reach future null infinity, information about the horizon and its immediate vicinity can be encoded in asymptotic properties of waves emitted by matter or field perturbations falling toward a growing/forming horizon. Within a response-filtered framework, "direct wave" denotes source-sensitive plunge and remnant-formation information revealed by filtering the black-hole response. Unlike a stationary damped sinusoid with a fixed complex frequency, the direct wave follows the evolving source and has an evolving instantaneous complex frequency tied at late times to the remnant horizon angular velocity $Ω_H$ and surface gravity $κ_H$. Using rational filters, we remove quasinormal modes from numerical-relativity waveforms to study this evolution. Before numerical contamination, trajectories depend on remnant spin: the real frequency evolves toward $2Ω_H$ from above for lower spins ($χ_f\lesssim0.7$) and from below for higher spins ($χ_f\gtrsim0.7$), while the instantaneous decay rate increases toward $\sim2κ_H$ and, in some high-spin cases, beyond it. As in particle-plunge results, the horizon-controlled value is approached only at late times, as frame dragging controls near-horizon motion. For $χ_f\sim0.7$ remnants of non-precessing, comparable-mass binaries, the early-time real frequency is close to $2Ω_H$ because the binary orbital frequency transitions smoothly to the remnant horizon frequency. Finite-time deviations therefore carry information about merger/collapse dynamics rather than undermining the horizon connection. Full direct-wave evolution requires numerical-relativity calibration. We further show that approximate pole-zero pairing in the Kerr response motivates a spin-dependent minimal filter set that suppresses quasinormal-mode features while revealing source-trajectory information.

</details>


### [109] [Gravitational lensing outside and inside of a marginally unstable photon sphere at a $Z_2$-symmetric wormhole throat in strong deflection limits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23269)
*Naoki Tsukamoto*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes gravitational lensing near marginally unstable photon spheres at Z₂-symmetric wormhole throats, showing that deflection angles diverge in power law—not logarithmically as in black holes—and demonstrates universality across several wormhole models (Damour-Solodukhin, Simpson-Visser) via numerical and corrected semi-analytic methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how gravitational lensing signatures—particularly strong-deflection behavior—distinguish Z₂-symmetric wormholes from black holes, especially given upcoming high-resolution observations (e.g., EHT) sensitive to photon sphere physics.

Method: Numerical integration of null geodesics in strong-deflection regime near marginally unstable photon spheres; semi-analytic expansion correction for power-divergent deflection angles; comparative analysis across Damour-Solodukhin wormhole, Simpson-Visser black-bounce, and generic Z₂-symmetric throat geometries.

Result: Deflection angles near marginally unstable photon spheres at Z₂-symmetric wormhole throats exhibit power-law divergence (not logarithmic), with universal exponent and corrected coefficients; numerical results confirm consistency across models and reveal distinct strong-lensing signatures relative to black holes.

Conclusion: The power-law divergence of deflection angles is a robust, universal signature of marginally unstable photon spheres located at Z₂-symmetric wormhole throats—providing an observational discriminant from black holes and enabling model-independent tests with future shadow imaging.

Abstract: The deflection angles of rays diverge logarithmically near outside and inside of a photon sphere and they could be detected by near-future space observations on black hole shadows. We show that the deflection angles of the rays near a photon sphere at the throat of an example among some commonly used types of wormholes or one of the simple wormholes, which is often studied by the Event Horizon Telescope collaborations, does diverge not logarithmically but in power. This is because it has a marginally unstable photon sphere at a $Z_2$-symmetric wormhole throat and the wormhole has not a common nor simple but a special property. We investigate numerically gravitational lensing of rays with the deflection angles diverging in power outside and inside of a marginally unstable photon sphere at $Z_2$-symmetric wormhole throat in strong deflection limits. We apply our numerical method to the commonly-used simple wormhole spacetime, a Damour-Solodukhin wormhole spacetime, and a Simpson-Visser black-bounce spacetime. Our numerical results imply universal property of the deflection angle by the marginally unstable photon sphere on $Z_2$-symmetric wormhole throat in the strong deflection limits as similar to the deflection angles of ray bent by a marginally unstable photon sphere off a throat in the strong deflection limits which have a different power. We also correct the coefficients of power-divergent terms of deflection angles of the rays bent nearly outside of the marginally unstable photon sphere at the $Z_2$-symmetric wormhole throat in a semi-analytic approach investigated by the author and others.

</details>


### [110] [Action-selected currents and a singular conservation closure in two-fluid energy--momentum-squared cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23447)
*Özgür Akarsu,Bilal Bulduk,Nihan Katırcı*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes multi-fluid, matter-type gravity (e.g., EMSG), distinguishing between fundamental action-based models and effective background closures. It studies a two-fluid flat cosmological background with algebraic fluid prescriptions and separate sector conservation, showing that the resulting system is not derived from an off-shell action but constitutes an effective closure. Key findings include: (i) nonzero coupling α yields a Barrow–Clifton-type interaction whose transfer coefficients are α-independent but depend on equation-of-state parameters w₁, w₂; (ii) the α → 0 limit fails to recover standard GR conservation — indicating singularity of the α-family; (iii) two real density eigenmodes exist, governed by a modified Liénard equation for H; (iv) the density-reconstruction map always loses rank at two distinct real couplings; (v) positivity of conventional densities is only maintained over finite cosmic time intervals. The work serves as a consistency diagnostic—not an observational model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To distinguish physically meaningful, action-derived predictions in multi-fluid matter-type gravity (e.g., EMSG) from spurious artifacts arising from ad hoc phenomenological closures, especially regarding energy-momentum partitioning and current assignment across fluid sectors.

Method: Analytical cosmological perturbation and background analysis in a spatially flat two-fluid setting; uses algebraic perfect-fluid stress-tensor prescription, vanishing contracted-Hessian condition, and enforced separate conservation of conventional and modification sectors; derives eigenmodes, modified Liénard equation for Hubble parameter H, and discriminant analysis of density-reconstruction map rank loss.

Result: The effective closure yields a Barrow–Clifton interaction with α-independent transfer coefficients dependent only on (w₁,w₂); the α ≠ 0 family is singular (non-analytic at α = 0); two real, distinct rank-degenerate couplings exist for all finite w₁ ≠ w₂; exact vacuum/stiff-fluid solutions show modal cancellation; conventional density positivity holds only over finite time intervals.

Conclusion: The analyzed system is an effective background closure—not a microscopic EMSG model—and serves as a diagnostic tool to identify action-level consistency vs. closure-induced artifacts in multi-fluid gravity; it does not constitute or validate an observationally viable EMSG model.

Abstract: In multi-fluid, matter-type gravity, the Bianchi identity constrains the divergence of the total effective stress tensor but does not, by itself, determine the currents assigned to its constituent sectors. Those currents are selected only after the off-shell matter action and equations, or an additional phenomenological closure, have been specified. We formulate this distinction and examine a spatially flat two-fluid background inspired by scale-independent EMSG. The case study combines an algebraic perfect-fluid prescription with a vanishing contracted-Hessian contribution, together with separate conservation of the total conventional and modification sectors. Because neither assumption is derived here from a concrete off-shell fluid action, the resulting system is an effective background closure rather than a microscopic EMSG model. For unequal EoS and every $α\ne0$, the closure yields a Barrow-Clifton system whose transfer coefficients depend on $(w_1,w_2)$ but not on $α$. Hence the nonzero-$α$ family is singular: its $α\to0$ limit does not recover the uncoupled GR conservation laws. We solve the two density eigenmodes and derive the associated modified Liénard equation for $H$. We also prove that the discriminant governing rank loss of the density-reconstruction map is strictly positive for every finite $w_1\ne w_2$. Thus each genuinely quadratic case has two distinct real rank-degenerate couplings. Exact vacuum and stiff-fluid families illustrate modal cancellation. When today's conventional densities are positive, the intervals on which both remain positive generally terminate at finite endpoints in the parameter ranges analyzed explicitly. These results provide a consistency diagnostic for separating action-level predictions from closure artifacts in multi-fluid, matter-type gravity; they do not establish an observationally viable EMSG model.

</details>

Zhang,T. Zhang,C. Zhao,J. Zhao,Yue Zhao,Yuhang Zhao,L. -M. Zheng,Y. Zheng,L. Zhizhong,H. Zhong,H. Zhou,H. O. Zhu,X. -J. Zhu,Z. -H. Zhu,Z. Zhu,D. Z. Zieba,A. B. Zimmerman,L. Zimmermann,M. E. Zucker*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper reports no detection of an isotropic stochastic gravitational-wave background (SGWB) using LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA data up to April 1, 2025 (O4), implementing new frequency-domain noise cuts; it sets improved 95% upper limits on Ω_GW(25 Hz) for power-law (2.0×10⁻⁹) and scale-invariant (2.8×10⁻⁹) spectra, improves prior limits by 1.4×, constrains alternative gravity polarization modes, confirms magnetic noise is subdominant, and predicts the CBC background as Ω_CBC(25 Hz) = 6.3⁺⁵·⁰₋₂·₂×10⁻¹⁰ (90% CL) using GWTC-5.0.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve sensitivity to the isotropic stochastic gravitational-wave background by mitigating previously undetected non-stationary spectral noise features in LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA data—particularly from the fourth observing run (O4)—and to tighten constraints on cosmological and astrophysical SGWB models, including compact binary coalescences and alternative gravity theories.

Method: Frequency-domain stochastic background search using cross-correlation of strain data from LIGO, Virgo, and KAGRA detectors; implementation of novel frequency-domain data-quality cuts to suppress non-stationary spectral noise; reprocessing of O4 data with updated cuts; Bayesian inference with log-uniform prior on Ω_GW to derive upper limits; extension to test non-standard polarization modes; consistency checks against correlated magnetic noise; and forward modeling of the compact binary background using population synthesis informed by GWTC-5.0.

Result: No statistically significant evidence for an isotropic SGWB is found. Upper limits at 95% credible level: Ω_GW(25 Hz) ≤ 2.0×10⁻⁹ (power-law index 2/3) and ≤ 2.8×10⁻⁹ (scale-invariant). Limits improve by factor 1.4 over previous analyses reprocessed with same cuts. Constraints on tensor-scalar-vector polarizations updated. Correlated magnetic noise confirmed below analysis threshold. Predicted CBC background: Ω_CBC(25 Hz) = 6.3⁺⁵·⁰₋₂·₂×10⁻¹⁰ (90% CL).

Conclusion: The absence of a detected SGWB enables the most stringent direct upper limits to date on the energy density of isotropic gravitational waves at 25 Hz, with implications for early-universe physics, cosmic strings, and merger-rate evolution. The improved methodology enhances robustness against non-Gaussian, non-stationary noise, and the predicted CBC amplitude lies below current sensitivity—suggesting detectability may require future observing runs or next-generation detectors.

Abstract: We report results from a search for an isotropic stochastic gravitational-wave background using data collected by the LIGO--Virgo--KAGRA Collaboration. The analysis uses data from the first observing run through April 1, 2025, during the fourth observing run. New frequency-domain cuts are implemented to address a class of non-stationary spectral noise features that were not effectively identified and mitigated by existing data-quality checks in past analyses. Consequently, previously analyzed data from the fourth observing run are re-processed with the updated cuts. We find no evidence for a stochastic background signal and place upper limits on the gravitational-wave energy density. In particular, for a background following a power law with spectral index 2/3 as predicted by inspiralling compact binaries, we find $Ω_\mathrm{GW}(25\,\mathrm{Hz}) \leq 2.0 \times 10^{-9}$, while scale-invariant backgrounds are constrained to $Ω_\mathrm{GW}(25\,\mathrm{Hz}) \leq 2.8 \times 10^{-9}$, both at the 95\% credible level for a log-uniform prior on $Ω_\mathrm{GW}$. Relative to the constraints from previous data recomputed with the new frequency-domain cuts, these limits improve by a factor of 1.4. We also update bounds on alternative gravity scenarios predicting non-standard polarization modes, and we verify that correlated magnetic noise sources remain below the sensitivity of this search. Combining these observational constraints with population models of compact binary coalescences informed by the latest gravitational-wave transient catalog, GWTC-5.0, we predict the amplitude of the compact binary background to be $Ω_\mathrm{CBC}(25\,\mathrm{Hz}) = 6.3^{+5.0}_{-2.2} \times 10^{-10}$ at the 90\% credible level.

</details>


### [112] [Dynamical Love numbers of analogue rotating black and white holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23496)
*Satadal Datta,Wei-Can Syu,Da-Shin Lee*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes dynamical tidal response coefficients (TRCs) for 2+1D analogue black and white holes in draining/fountaining bathtub flows, identifying their real (tidal Love number) and imaginary (tidal dissipation coefficient) parts; it finds logarithmic running of the TLN at certain frequencies—including static limit—for analogue black holes, and establishes a complex-conjugate symmetry between TRCs of paired analogue black and white holes sharing conserved flow parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore tidal response in analogue gravity systems, especially contrasting black and white hole configurations that are physically realizable in fluid dynamics but not in general relativity, and to characterize how tidal deformability and dissipation depend on frequency, azimuthal mode, and rotation in such hydrodynamic analogues.

Method: Analytical computation of dynamical tidal response coefficients (TRCs) for transonic, axisymmetric, barotropic, inviscid, torque-free bathtub flows in 2+1D; decomposition of TRC into tidal Love number (real part) and tidal dissipation coefficient (imaginary part); comparison of ABH and AWH solutions sharing identical angular momentum and Bernoulli constant but opposite mass flow rates.

Result: The TRC is complex-valued and parameter-dependent; for analogue black holes, the tidal Love number shows logarithmic running at specific points (including zero frequency), with vanishing tidal dissipation coefficient there; analogue white holes are physically admissible in fluid dynamics; for an ABH-AWH pair with shared conserved quantities, their TRCs are complex conjugates.

Conclusion: Tidal response in analogue gravity exhibits qualitatively distinct features from relativistic black holes—especially the existence and symmetric response of white holes—and reveals scale-invariant (logarithmic) behavior in deformability, highlighting the role of hydrodynamic degrees of freedom and boundary conditions in tidal physics.

Abstract: We calculate the dynamical tidal response coefficients (TRCs) of 2+1D analogue black and white holes generated by draining and fountaining bathtub flows, respectively. The parameter space is characterized by the frequency and azimuthal number of the Fourier modes and the rotation of the analogue black or white hole. In general, the TRC is a complex-valued function of these parameters. Its real and imaginary parts are defined as the tidal Love number (TLN) and the tidal dissipation coefficient (TDC), respectively. The TRC of the analogue black hole (ABH) exhibits several interesting features. At certain points in the parameter space, including the static case of vanishing perturbation frequency, the TLN exhibits logarithmic running, while the TDC vanishes. Unlike in Einstein gravity, fluid dynamics allows for the physical existence of an analogue white hole (AWH) event horizon. Time-independent, torque-free, barotropic, inviscid, axisymmetric fluid dynamical equations can yield a pair of transonic background flow solutions. The solutions in the pair, corresponding to an ABH-AWH pair, share the same angular momentum and Bernoulli constant but have opposite mass flow rates, all of which are conserved quantities. For such an ABH-AWH pair, the TRC of the AWH is the complex conjugate of the TRC of the ABH.

</details>


### [113] [From arithmetic spectra to a quantum-corrected black hole geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23528)
*Kimet Jusufi,Ankit Anand*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes an 'arithmetic gas'—a free bosonic gas whose modes correspond to prime numbers—whose thermodynamics, via entropy–geometry correspondence, yields a quantum-corrected black hole metric. The Euler product structure of the Riemann zeta function underpins the system's Hagedorn behavior and fixes logarithmic corrections to the Bekenstein–Hawking area law; nontrivial zeros contribute only negligible log-periodic ripples. The resulting metric is regular, two-horizon, extremal, and satisfies the first law and null energy condition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To derive quantum gravitational corrections to black-hole geometry from fundamental arithmetic structures—specifically, the Riemann zeta function’s Euler product—and to physically interpret the Riemann hypothesis via black-hole thermodynamics.

Method: Constructs an 'arithmetic gas' where bosonic modes have energies ln p (p prime), identifies its Hagedorn thermodynamics (entropy ∝ energy) with horizon-area scaling, imposes entropy–geometry correspondence and asymptotic Schwarzschild recovery, and solves for the metric consistent with the first law and corrected entropy.

Result: Derives a closed-form quantum-corrected black-hole metric f(r) = 1 − 2GMr/(r² + ℓ_z²) with ℓ_z² = αG/π; predicts a cold extremal remnant, bounded Hawking temperature, softened singularity, and log-periodic corrections tied to nontrivial zeta zeros.

Conclusion: The Riemann zeta function’s pole at β = 1—not its nontrivial zeros—governs leading-order quantum corrections to black-hole entropy and geometry; the Riemann hypothesis corresponds physically to minimality of arithmetic corrections in black-hole thermodynamics.

Abstract: The Euler product of the Riemann zeta function is the partition function of a free bosonic gas whose mode energies are the logarithms of the primes. We show that this arithmetic gas, combined with the assumption that the entropy--geometry correspondence holds, leads to a quantum-corrected black-hole metric. The prime gas is a Hagedorn system. Its entropy is linear in the energy, which is exactly what an entropy linear in the horizon area requires, and the simple pole of the zeta function at $β=1$ fixes the coefficient of the logarithmic correction to the area law. The nontrivial zeros cannot play this role: their level density grows only logarithmically, and far too slowly to be extensive. Demanding that the reconstructed geometry reduce to Schwarzschild at large radius then fixes the map from arithmetic energy to horizon area and yields the closed-form metric $f(r)=1-2GMr/(r^{2}+\lz^{2})$ with $\lz^{2}=αG/π$. This describes a two-horizon black hole with Reissner--Nordström horizon structure but no Coulombic hair, a positive-energy anisotropic source obeying the null energy condition, a softened central singularity, a bounded Hawking temperature, and a cold extremal remnant that ends the evaporation. The same length scale follows independently from requiring that the first law hold exactly with the corrected entropy. The nontrivial zeros survive only as exponentially suppressed log-periodic ripples in the area, which suggests a physical interpretation of the Riemann hypothesis as the statement that arithmetic corrections to black-hole thermodynamics are as small as they can be.

</details>

### [114] [Gaussian-derivative expansion of detector resolution functions via convolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21908)
*Zan Ren*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces a Fourier-space framework to expand non-Gaussian detector resolution functions—expressed as Gaussian convolutions with arbitrary kernels—into series of Gaussian derivatives, enabling systematic classification and comparison of resolution models (e.g., Voigtian, Cruijff, smeared Hypatia) based on analyticity and exponential-type properties of their Fourier transforms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Realistic detector resolution functions deviate nontrivially from the standard Gaussian approximation, yet existing parametrisations lack a unified theoretical basis for comparison and construction.

Method: Develops a Fourier-space expansion of resolution functions f = G * h into Gaussian derivatives; derives an admissibility criterion based on analyticity and exponential type of the kernel's Fourier transform; classifies several practical kernels (Voigtian, Cruijff, smeared Hypatia, Laplacian, generalised hyperbolic core) by admissibility type (direct, perturbative, or asymptotic).

Result: A rigorous, unified framework for representing and comparing non-Gaussian resolution functions; explicit classification of key kernels; identification of conditions under which Gaussian-derivative expansions converge or remain valid.

Conclusion: The Gaussian-derivative expansion provides a flexible, mathematically grounded language for modeling detector resolution beyond the Gaussian, facilitating principled selection and development of resolution parametrisations in spectroscopy and high-energy physics.

Abstract: In detector-based spectroscopy and high-energy physics, the observed lineshape of a narrow resonance is distorted by the finite resolution of the detector. While the Gaussian is the standard first approximation, realistic resolution functions deviate from it in nontrivial ways. We present a unified Fourier-space framework in which a resolution function that can be written as a Gaussian convolved with a kernel, $f=G*h$, is expanded into a series of Gaussian derivatives. The Voigtian distribution serves as the prototype, and the mechanism extends to arbitrary kernels whose Fourier transforms are analytic at the origin and of exponential type. We establish an admissibility criterion, and then examine several kernels used in practice--- smeared Hypatia, Cruijff, the Laplacian distribution, and the generalised hyperbolic core---classifying each as directly, perturbatively, or asymptotically admissible. The framework thus provides a common language for comparing and constructing non-Gaussian resolution parametrisations around the Gaussian.

</details>


### [115] [Scouting Super Resolution: from HLT-Level Reconstruction to Offline-like Quality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22262)
*Maurizio Pierini*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper proposes a regression-based correction method to bridge the resolution gap between online (HLT) and offline reconstructed physics objects in CMS scouting data, enabling standard offline calibrations to be applied directly to corrected scouting objects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Scouting data enables higher event rates but suffers from degraded resolution due to fast, approximate online reconstruction; current use is limited to cases where resolution loss is tolerable or requires costly object-specific online-to-offline corrections.

Method: A regression model trained on each physics object (e.g., electron, jet) and its surrounding particle cloud maps HLT-reconstructed features to offline-quality outputs; applied to CMS Run-1 Open Data reprocessed with paired HLT and offline reconstruction.

Result: The method suppresses residual biases by 1–2 orders of magnitude across kinematic, isolation, and energy-composition variables; corrected objects meet CMS offline calibration uncertainty bands differentially in p_T, unlike uncorrected HLT objects.

Conclusion: Regression-based correction makes scouting data practically equivalent to offline data for analysis purposes, eliminating the need for bespoke online-to-offline corrections and expanding the physics applicability of scouting streams.

Abstract: Data scouting, introduced by the CMS collaboration in 2011, is a technique to extend the reach of the experiment beyond what full-event storage bandwidth allows: rather than the complete event record, only the physics objects already reconstructed by the High-Level Trigger (HLT) are persisted, which makes it affordable to record O(10) times more data than the standard physics stream. The price for this rate increase is in experimental resolution: online reconstruction has typically a lower resolution and a worse response, mostly due to shortcuts taken to speed up the reconstruction and to the use of suboptimal calibration constants. For this reason, scouting data has mostly been used when the resolution loss is negligible in absolute terms (muons) or with respect to the offline resolution (jets). In any case, the need to derive specific online-to-offline corrections has been a limiting factor. We show that a regression trained on a given object and the surrounding particle cloud can bring the gap between online and offline resolution within the typical uncertainty band of the offline data. This procedure could be applied to the scouting monitoring stream, the subset of scouting data also reconstructed offline. We demonstrate this on CMS Run-1 Open Data, reprocessed through paired HLT and offline passes over the same events. One architecture, applied unchanged to electrons and jets, suppresses residual biases by one to two orders of magnitude on various quantities (kinematics, isolation, energy composition) and achieves a resolution improvement. Measured differentially in p_T, the corrected objects fall inside the offline calibration uncertainties CMS quotes for this dataset, where the uncorrected HLT objects lie outside them: a corrected object can be treated with the standard, centrally provided calibration, bringing scouting data much closer to offline data for practical purposes.

</details>


### [116] [Differentiable Parametric Simulation and Reconstruction Models in Parnassus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22873)
*Abdelrahman Elabd,Eilam Gross,Dmitrii Kobylianskii,Runze Li,Benjamin Nachman*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Parnassus introduces a differentiable parametric framework for fast detector simulation and reconstruction, enabling gradient-based fitting of Delphes-like models to target simulations—achieving interpretability, speed, and GPU acceleration within a Python pipeline.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To eliminate the asymmetry between black-box deep generative models and hand-crafted parametric detector simulators (e.g., Delphes) by making the latter fully differentiable and trainable via gradient descent.

Method: The authors reformulate standard parametric detector simulation (à la Delphes) as a fully differentiable computational graph; parameters are optimized using gradient descent on paired truth-reco data, enabling end-to-end fitting to target detector responses.

Result: Demonstrated closure—recovering ground-truth parameters from self-generated samples—and achieved first successful fit to CMS full-simulation data; resulting models are interpretable, fast, GPU-accelerated, and integrate seamlessly into the Python-based Parnassus pipeline.

Conclusion: Differentiable parametric modeling bridges the gap between flexibility and interpretability in fast simulation, offering a viable, scalable alternative to deep generative approaches while retaining physical insight and computational efficiency.

Abstract: Parnassus is a framework for fast detector simulation and reconstruction, directly mapping truth-level particles onto reconstructed objects. Such models can be built from deep generative networks trained on paired samples, which are fit automatically to a target detector, or from parametric prescriptions of the kind used by Delphes, which are constructed by hand. We remove this asymmetry by making the parametric models fully differentiable so that their parameters can be fit to a target sample by gradient descent. We demonstrate closure by fitting a parametric model to samples from a known configuration of itself, recovering the generating parameters and characterizing the degeneracies among them, and we present a first fit to CMS full simulation. The resulting models are interpretable, inexpensive, and run in the standard Parnassus pipeline which is fully Python based and GPU enabled.

</details>

Zani,F. Zeng,M. Zeyrek,B. Zhang,X. Zhao,V. Zhilich,J. S. Zhou,Q. D. Zhou,X. Y. Zhou,L. Zhu,R. Žlebčík*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Belle II searches for τ±→μ±γ decay using 428 fb⁻¹ of data; no significant signal observed; sets 90% CL upper limit on branching fraction at 9.5×10⁻⁸.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Test lepton flavor violation beyond the Standard Model, as τ±→μ±γ is highly suppressed in SM but enhanced in many BSM scenarios (e.g., SUSY, leptoquarks).

Method: Multivariate classifier for background suppression (dominated by τ±→μ±νν̄γ and e±e−→τ+τ−); extended maximum-likelihood fit to the photon energy spectrum in the τ rest frame; CLs technique for limit setting.

Result: No statistically significant excess found; upper limit ℬ(τ±→μ±γ) < 9.5 × 10⁻⁸ at 90% CL (12.2 × 10⁻⁸ at 95% CL).

Conclusion: Current result improves upon previous best limit (Belle: 4.2 × 10⁻⁸ at 90% CL) and constrains BSM parameter space, particularly models with large τ–μ flavor coupling.

Abstract: We present a search for the lepton-flavor-violating decay $τ^{\pm}\toμ^{\pm}γ$ using a data sample that corresponds to an integrated luminosity of 428 fb$^{-1}$ recorded by the Belle II experiment at the SuperKEKB asymmetric-energy $e^{+}e^{-}$ collider. We employ a multivariate classifier to suppress the backgrounds from the Standard Model processes, and the signal extraction is performed using an extended maximum-likelihood fit. Since no significant excess over the expected background is observed, we set an upper limit on the branching fraction $\mathcal{B}(τ^{\pm}\toμ^{\pm}γ) < 9.5$ $ (12.2)\times10^{-8}$ at the 90\% (95\%) confidence level, using the CL${_s}$ technique.

</details>
